<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 103G. WATERS OF THE STATE</h2>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL PROVISIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.001">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.001 CITATION; WATER LAW.</h1>
        <p>Chapters 103A, 103B, 103C, 103D, 103E, 103F, and 103G constitute the water law of this state and may be cited as the "Water Law."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.001.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.1.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.005">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.005 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Abandon.</span></h2>
          <p>"Abandon" means to give up the use and maintenance of structures or improvements to realty and to surrender them to deterioration. Abandon does not refer to intent to surrender or relinquish title to or a possessory interest in the real property where the structures or improvements are located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Agricultural land.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural land" means: land used for horticultural, row, close grown, pasture, and hayland crops; growing nursery stocks; animal feedlots; farm yards; associated building sites; and public and private drainage systems and field roads located on any of the foregoing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Altered natural watercourse.</span></h2>
          <p>"Altered natural watercourse" means a former natural watercourse that has been affected by artificial changes to straighten, deepen, narrow, or widen the original channel.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Appropriating.</span></h2>
          <p>"Appropriating" means withdrawal, removal, or transfer of water from its source regardless of how the water is used.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Artificial watercourse.</span></h2>
          <p>"Artificial watercourse" means a watercourse artificially constructed by human beings where a natural watercourse was not previously located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Basin of origin.</span></h2>
          <p>"Basin of origin" means the drainage basin of the Great Lakes, the Red River of the North, the Mississippi River, or the Missouri River.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Board.</span></h2>
          <p>"Board" means the Board of Water and Soil Resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 8b]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Constructed management facilities for stormwater.</span></h2>
          <p>"Constructed management facilities for stormwater" means ponds, basins, holding tanks, cisterns, infiltration trenches and swales, or other best management practices that have been designed, constructed, and operated to store or treat stormwater in accordance with local, state, or federal requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.8b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8b.<span class="headnote">Consumptive use.</span></h2>
          <p>"Consumptive use" means water that is withdrawn from its source for immediate further use in the area of the source and is not directly returned to the source.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Director.</span></h2>
          <p>"Director" means the director of the Division of Ecological and Water Resources of the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Division.</span></h2>
          <p>"Division" means the Division of Ecological and Water Resources of the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.9b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9b.</h2>
          <p class="in">[Renumbered subd 9d]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.9c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9c.<span class="headnote">Ecosystem harm.</span></h2>
          <p>"Ecosystem harm" means to change the biological community and ecology in a manner that results in loss of ecological structure or function.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.9d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9d.<span class="headnote">Electronic transmission.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Electronic transmission" means the transfer of data or information through an electronic data interchange system consisting of, but not limited to, computer modems and computer networks. Electronic transmission specifically means electronic mail, unless other means of electronic transmission are mutually agreed to by the sender and recipient.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>MS 2010 [Renumbered subd 9a]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.</h2>
          <p>MS 1994 [Renumbered subd 10e]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.<span class="headnote">50 to 80 percent area.</span></h2>
          <p>"50 to 80 percent area" means a county or watershed with at least 50 but less than 80 percent of the presettlement wetland acreage intact.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10b.<span class="headnote">Greater than 80 percent area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Greater than 80 percent area" means a county, watershed, or, for purposes of wetland replacement, bank service area where 80 percent or more of the presettlement wetland acreage is intact and:</p>
          <p>(1) ten percent or more of the current total land area is wetland; or</p>
          <p>(2) 50 percent or more of the current total land area is state or federal land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10c.<span class="headnote">Hayland.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hayland" means an area that was mechanically harvested or that was planted with annually seeded crops in a crop rotation seeding of grasses or legumes in six of the last ten years prior to January 1, 1991.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10d.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 10h]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd 10e.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 10i]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10f.</h2>
          <p>MS 2011 Supp [Renumbered subd 9b]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10g">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10g.<span class="headnote">In-lieu fee program.</span></h2>
          <p>"In-lieu fee program" means a program in which wetland replacement requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222">103G.222</a> are satisfied through payment of money to the board or a board-approved sponsor to develop replacement credits according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.12">103G.2242, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10h">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10h.<span class="headnote">Less than 50 percent area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Less than 50 percent area" means a county, watershed, or, for purposes of wetland replacement, bank service area with less than 50 percent of the presettlement wetland acreage intact or any county, watershed, or bank service area not defined as a "greater than 80 percent area" or "50 to 80 percent area."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.10i">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10i.<span class="headnote">Local government unit.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Local government unit" means:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) outside of the seven-county metropolitan area, a city council, county board of commissioners, or a soil and water conservation district or their delegate;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) in the seven-county metropolitan area, a city council, a town board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/368.01">368.01</a>, a watershed management organization under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.211">103B.211</a>, or a soil and water conservation district or their delegate;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) on state land, the agency with administrative responsibility for the land; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) for wetland banking projects established solely for replacing wetland impacts under a permit to mine under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.481">93.481</a>, the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Meandered lake.</span></h2>
          <p>"Meandered lake" means a body of water except streams located within the meander lines shown on plats made by the United States General Land Office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.11a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 15e]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Municipality.</span></h2>
          <p>"Municipality" means a home rule charter or statutory city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Natural watercourse.</span></h2>
          <p>"Natural watercourse" means a natural channel that has definable beds and banks capable of conducting confined runoff from adjacent land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.13a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 13c]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.13b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13b.<span class="headnote">Negative impact to surface waters.</span></h2>
          <p>"Negative impact to surface waters" means a change in hydrology sufficient to cause aquatic ecosystem harm or alter riparian uses long term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.13c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13c.<span class="headnote">Once-through system.</span></h2>
          <p>"Once-through system" means a space heating, ventilating, air conditioning (HVAC), or refrigeration system used for any type of temperature or humidity control application, utilizing groundwater, that circulates through the system and is then discharged without reusing it for a higher priority purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Ordinary high-water level.</span></h2>
          <p>"Ordinary high-water level" means the boundary of water basins, watercourses, public waters, and public waters wetlands, and:</p>
          <p>(1) the ordinary high-water level is an elevation delineating the highest water level that has been maintained for a sufficient period of time to leave evidence upon the landscape, commonly the point where the natural vegetation changes from predominantly aquatic to predominantly terrestrial;</p>
          <p>(2) for watercourses, the ordinary high-water level is the elevation of the top of the bank of the channel; and</p>
          <p>(3) for reservoirs and flowages, the ordinary high-water level is the operating elevation of the normal summer pool.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.14a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14a.</h2>
          <p>MS 1994 [Renumbered subd 14b]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.14a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14a.<span class="headnote">Pasture.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pasture" means an area that was grazed by domesticated livestock or that was planted with annually seeded crops in a crop rotation seeding of grasses or legumes in six of the last ten years prior to January 1, 1991.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.14b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14b.<span class="headnote">Political subdivision.</span></h2>
          <p>"Political subdivision" means a county, city, town, school district, or other local government jurisdiction to which the state provides state aids or on which the state imposes state mandates.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.14c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14c.<span class="headnote">Presettlement wetland.</span></h2>
          <p>"Presettlement wetland" means a wetland or public waters wetland that existed in this state at the time of statehood in 1858.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.14d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14d.<span class="headnote">Project.</span></h2>
          <p>"Project" means a specific plan, contiguous activity, proposal, or design necessary to accomplish a goal as defined by the local government unit. As used in this chapter, a project may not be split into components or phases for the purpose of gaining additional exemptions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Public waters.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Public waters" means:</p>
          <p>(1) water basins assigned a shoreland management classification by the commissioner under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.201">103F.201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.221">103F.221</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) waters of the state that have been finally determined to be public waters or navigable waters by a court of competent jurisdiction;</p>
          <p>(3) meandered lakes, excluding lakes that have been legally drained;</p>
          <p>(4) water basins previously designated by the commissioner for management for a specific purpose such as trout lakes and game lakes pursuant to applicable laws;</p>
          <p>(5) water basins designated as scientific and natural areas under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.033">84.033</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) water basins located within and totally surrounded by publicly owned lands;</p>
          <p>(7) water basins where the state of Minnesota or the federal government holds title to any of the beds or shores, unless the owner declares that the water is not necessary for the purposes of the public ownership;</p>
          <p>(8) water basins where there is a publicly owned and controlled access that is intended to provide for public access to the water basin;</p>
          <p>(9) natural and altered watercourses with a total drainage area greater than two square miles;</p>
          <p>(10) natural and altered watercourses designated by the commissioner as trout streams; and</p>
          <p>(11) public waters wetlands, unless the statute expressly states otherwise.</p>
          <p>(b) Public waters are not determined exclusively by:</p>
          <p>(1) the proprietorship of the underlying, overlying, or surrounding land;</p>
          <p>(2) whether it is a body or stream of water that was navigable in fact or susceptible of being used as a highway for commerce at the time this state was admitted to the union; or</p>
          <p>(3) their inclusion in or exclusion from the public waters inventory required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.201">103G.201</a>. This clause is effective July 1, 2027.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15a.<span class="headnote">Public waters wetlands.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public waters wetlands" means all types 3, 4, and 5 wetlands, as defined in United States Fish and Wildlife Service Circular No. 39 (1971 edition), not included within the definition of public waters, that are ten or more acres in size in unincorporated areas or 2-1/2 or more acres in incorporated areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15b.</h2>
          <p>MS 2010 [Renumbered subd 15f]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15c.</h2>
          <p>MS 2010 [Renumbered subd 15g]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15d.</h2>
          <p>MS 2010 [Renumbered subd 15h]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15e.<span class="headnote">Shallow lake.</span></h2>
          <p>"Shallow lake" means a body of water, excluding a stream, that is greater than or equal to 50 acres in size and less than or equal to 15 feet in maximum depth.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15f.<span class="headnote">Shoreland wetland protection zone.</span></h2>
          <p>"Shoreland wetland protection zone" means:</p>
          <p>(1) for local government units that have a shoreland management ordinance approved under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.201">103F.201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.221">103F.221</a>, the shoreland wetland protection zone is:</p>
          <p>(i) 1,000 feet from the ordinary high-water level of a water basin that is a public water identified in the shoreland management ordinance or the shoreland area approved by the commissioner as provided in the shoreland management rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.211">103F.211</a>, whichever is less; or</p>
          <p>(ii) 300 feet from the ordinary high-water level of a watercourse identified in the shoreland management ordinance or the shoreland area approved by the commissioner as provided in the shoreland management rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.211">103F.211</a>, whichever is less; and</p>
          <p>(2) for local government units that do not have a shoreland management ordinance approved under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.201">103F.201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.221">103F.221</a>, the shoreland wetland protection zone is:</p>
          <p>(i) 1,000 feet from the ordinary high-water level of a water basin that is a public water that is at least ten acres in size within municipalities and at least 25 acres in size in unincorporated areas; or</p>
          <p>(ii) 300 feet from the ordinary high-water level of a watercourse identified by the public waters inventory under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.201">103G.201</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15g">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15g.<span class="headnote">Silviculture.</span></h2>
          <p>"Silviculture" means the management of forest trees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15h">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15h.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 15j]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15i">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15i.<span class="headnote">Sustainable diversion limit.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sustainable diversion limit" means a maximum amount of water that can be removed directly or indirectly from a surface water body in a defined geographic area on a monthly or annual basis without causing a negative impact to the surface water body.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.15j">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15j.<span class="headnote">Utility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Utility" means a sanitary sewer, storm sewer, potable water distribution, and transmission, distribution, or furnishing, at wholesale or retail, of natural or manufactured gas, electricity, telephone, or radio service or communications.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Water basin.</span></h2>
          <p>"Water basin" means an enclosed natural depression with definable banks, capable of containing water, that may be partly filled with waters of the state and is discernible on aerial photographs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Waters of the state.</span></h2>
          <p>"Waters of the state" means surface or underground waters, except surface waters that are not confined but are spread and diffused over the land. Waters of the state includes boundary and inland waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.17a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17a.<span class="headnote">Watershed.</span></h2>
          <p>"Watershed" means the 81 major watershed units delineated by the map, "State of Minnesota Watershed Boundaries - 1979."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.17b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17b.<span class="headnote">Wetland type.</span></h2>
          <p>"Wetland type" means a wetland type classified according to <i>Wetlands of the United States</i>, United States Fish and Wildlife Service Circular 39 (1971 edition) or <i>A Hydrogeomorphic Classification for Wetlands</i>, United States Army Corps of Engineers (August 1993), including updates, supplementary guidance, and replacements, if any, as determined by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.</h2>
          <p>MS 1994 [Renumbered subd 15a]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.005.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Wetlands.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Wetlands" means lands transitional between terrestrial and aquatic systems where the water table is usually at or near the surface or the land is covered by shallow water. For purposes of this definition, wetlands must have the following three attributes:</p>
          <p>(1) have a predominance of hydric soils;</p>
          <p>(2) are inundated or saturated by surface water or groundwater at a frequency and duration sufficient to support a prevalence of hydrophytic vegetation typically adapted for life in saturated soil conditions; and</p>
          <p>(3) under normal circumstances support a prevalence of such vegetation.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of regulation under this chapter, the term wetlands does not include public waters wetlands as defined in subdivision 15a.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), wetlands includes deepwater aquatic habitats that are not public waters or public waters wetlands. For purposes of this paragraph, "deepwater aquatic habitats" has the meaning given in <i>Corps of Engineers Wetlands Delineation Manual</i>, United States Army Corps of Engineers (January 1987).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.005.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.2.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/597/#laws.0.62.0">1990 c 597 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.1.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 1</a>-6; art 10 s 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.49.0">1994 c 643 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.3.0">1995 c 218 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.10.0">1996 c 462 s 10</a>-22,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/2/#laws.0.8.0">1997 c 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 382 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.111.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 111</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.63.0">2011 c 107 s 63</a>,64; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.79.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 79</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.11.0">2014 c 248 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.81.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 81</a>,148; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.107.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 107</a>-109; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.28.0">2023 c 25 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.77.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 77</a>-79,111; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.3.75.0">2024 c 90 art 3 s 75</a>-77; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.47.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMMISSIONER'S AUTHORITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.101">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.101 WATER CONSERVATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.101.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Development.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop a water resources conservation program for the state. The program must include conservation, allocation, and development of waters of the state for the best interests of the people.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.101.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program to guide permit issuance and dams.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must be guided by the program in issuing permits for the use and appropriation of the waters of the state and the construction, reconstruction, repair, removal, or abandonment of dams, reservoirs, and other control structures.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.3.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.105">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.105 COOPERATION WITH OTHER AGENCIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.105.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Other states and federal government.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may cooperate and enter into agreements with the United States government, a state department, or a state or country adjacent to this state to implement this chapter and chapter 103F. The commissioner may cooperate with departments of the government of the United States in the execution of surveys within the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.105.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State and local officials; enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Personnel of the Pollution Control Agency, the Health Department, and county and municipal governments must cooperate with the commissioner in monitoring and enforcing water permits. County attorneys, sheriffs, and other peace officers and other officers having enforcement authority must take all action to the extent of their authority, respectively, that may be necessary or proper for the enforcement of the provisions, rules, standards, orders, or permits specified in this chapter and chapter 103F.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.4.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.4.0">1995 c 218 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.111">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.111 REPRESENTING STATE IN WATER ISSUES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.111.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner; federal water issues.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may appear, represent, and act for the state in any matter relating to an application to be made to the federal government relating to waters of the state or their use and may act in a manner to protect the interests of the people of the state consistent with this chapter and chapter 103F.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.111.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Director; water proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>The director may appear for the state in any matter or proceeding affecting waters of the state to give hydrologic and hydraulic engineering advice and information in connection with the proceeding.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.111.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.5.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.5.0">1995 c 218 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.115">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.115 ENJOINING WATER-FLOW INTERFERENCE OUTSIDE STATE.</h1>
        <p>If a person, firm, association, corporation, or a state or political subdivision, agency, or commission of a state disturbs, obstructs, or interferes with the natural flow or condition of public waters beyond the boundaries of this state in a manner that seriously affects the public welfare and interests of this state, the commissioner may institute proceedings in behalf of this state in a court having jurisdiction to abate or enjoin the continuance of the disturbance, obstruction, or interference.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.115.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.6.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.121">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.121 COMMISSIONER'S AUTHORITY TO INVESTIGATE AND CONSTRUCT PROJECTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.121.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Surveys and investigations.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may conduct surveys, investigations, and studies, and prepare maps of the waters of the state and topography of the state to implement this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.121.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acquiring property.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may acquire title to private property for an authorized purpose by purchase or by eminent domain. The use of property for projects to implement this chapter is a public purpose. On request by the commissioner, the attorney general shall acquire title to private property for projects under this chapter as provided in chapter 117.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.121.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may approve contracts for projects under this chapter and change the plans of the projects when necessary, and supervise, control, and accept the projects when complete. The commissioner may pay for projects and expenses incurred in connection with the projects from funds available to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.7.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.6.0">1995 c 218 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.125">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.125 DIRECTOR'S AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.125.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Cooperation with government agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>The director shall cooperate with agencies and departments of the state and federal government relating to projects affecting waters of the state and shall make recommendations to the agencies involved and to the governor about the desirability, feasibility, and practicability of the proposed projects.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.125.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cooperative agreements.</span></h2>
          <p>The director, with approval of the commissioner, may make cooperative agreements with and cooperate with any person, corporation, or government authority to implement this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.125.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Standards for forms and maps.</span></h2>
          <p>The director may adopt rules to standardize forms and maps, sizes of maps, plats, drawings, and specifications in proceedings related to waters of the state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.125.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.8.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 186 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.127">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.127 PERMIT PROGRAM UNDER SECTION 404 OF FEDERAL CLEAN WATER ACT.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, the commissioner, with the concurrence of the Board of Water and Soil Resources and the commissioner of agriculture, may adopt rules establishing a permit program for regulating the discharge of dredged and fill material into the waters of the state as necessary to obtain approval from the United States Environmental Protection Agency to administer the permit program under section 404 of the federal Clean Water Act, United States Code, title 33, section 1344. The rules may not be more restrictive than the program under section 404, or state law, if it is more restrictive than the federal program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.127.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.9.1.0">1991 c 354 art 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.23.0">1996 c 462 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.131">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.131 VENUE OF CERTAIN ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.131.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Water located in one county.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, an action for declaratory judgment that is brought under chapter 555 by or against the commissioner must be venued in the county where the water, watercourse, or water basin is located, if the water, watercourse, or water basin is located in one county. This section applies to actions to determine the validity of the commissioner's final decision regarding:</p>
          <p>(1) the classification of waters of the state as public waters; or</p>
          <p>(2) the drainage of water basins or watercourses as provided in chapter 103E.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.131.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Water located in more than one county.</span></h2>
          <p>If the water, watercourse, or water basin is located in more than one county, then the venue is the judicial district where the majority of the water, watercourse, or water basin is located.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.131.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.9.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.134">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.134 ORDERS AND INVESTIGATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner has the following powers and duties when acting pursuant to the enforcement provisions of this chapter:</p>
        <p>(1) to adopt, issue, reissue, modify, deny, revoke, enter into, or enforce reasonable orders, schedules of compliance, and stipulation agreements;</p>
        <p>(2) to issue notices of violation;</p>
        <p>(3) to require a person holding a permit issued under this chapter or otherwise impacting the public waters of the state without a permit issued under this chapter to:</p>
        <p>(i) make reports;</p>
        <p>(ii) install, use, and maintain monitoring equipment or methods;</p>
        <p>(iii) perform tests according to methods, at locations, at intervals, and in a manner as the commissioner prescribes; and</p>
        <p>(iv) provide other information as the commissioner may reasonably require; and</p>
        <p>(4) to conduct investigations; issue notices, public and otherwise; and order hearings as the commissioner deems necessary or advisable to discharge duties under this chapter, including but not limited to issuing permits and authorizing an employee or agent appointed by the commissioner to conduct the investigations and other authorities cited in this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.134.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.80.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 80</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.135">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.135 ENFORCING COMMISSIONER'S ORDERS.</h1>
        <p>Upon application of the commissioner, the district court of a county where a project is entirely or partially located may by injunction enforce compliance with, or restrain the violation of, an order of the commissioner made under this chapter or chapter 103F, or restrain the violation of this chapter or chapter 103F.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.135.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.10.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.7.0">1995 c 218 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.141">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.141 PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.141.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Misdemeanors.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 2, a person is guilty of a misdemeanor who:</p>
          <p>(1) undertakes or procures another to undertake an alteration in the course, current, or cross section of public waters or appropriates waters of the state without previously obtaining a permit from the commissioner, regardless of whether the commissioner would have granted a permit had an application been filed;</p>
          <p>(2) undertakes or procures another to undertake an alteration in the course, current, or cross section of public waters or appropriates waters of the state in violation of or in excess of authority granted under a permit issued by the commissioner, regardless of whether an application had been filed for permission to perform the act involved or whether the act involved would have been permitted had a proper application been filed;</p>
          <p>(3) undertakes or procures another to undertake an alteration in the course, current, or cross section of public waters or appropriates waters of the state after a permit to undertake the project has been denied by the commissioner; or</p>
          <p>(4) violates a provision of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.141.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Violating section 404 permits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Whenever the commissioner finds that a person is in violation of a condition or limitation set forth in a permit issued under the rules adopted by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.127">103G.127</a>, the commissioner shall issue an order requiring the person to comply with the condition or limitation, or the commissioner shall bring a civil action in accordance with paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may commence a civil action for appropriate relief in district court, including a permanent or temporary injunction, for a violation for which the commissioner is authorized to issue a compliance order under paragraph (a). The court may restrain the violation and require compliance.</p>
          <p>(c) A person who violates a condition or limitation in a permit issued by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.127">103G.127</a>, and a person who violates an order issued by the commissioner under paragraph (a), is subject to a civil penalty not to exceed $25,000 per day for each violation. In determining the amount of a civil penalty the court shall consider the seriousness of the violation or violations, the economic benefit, if any, resulting from the violation, any history of violations, any good faith efforts to comply with the applicable requirements, the economic impact of the penalty on the violator, and other matters justice may require.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.141.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.11.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.9.2.0">1991 c 354 art 9 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.145">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.145 APPLICATION.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in this chapter supersedes or amends section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/92.45">92.45</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.145.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.12.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.146">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.146 DUTY OF CANDOR.</h1>
        <p>(a) A person must not knowingly:</p>
        <p>(1) make a false statement of fact or fail to correct a false statement of material fact regarding any matter pertaining to this chapter;</p>
        <p>(2) fail to disclose information that the person knows is necessary for the commissioner to make an informed decision under this chapter; or</p>
        <p>(3) offer information that the person knows to be false.</p>
        <p>(b) If a person has offered material information to the commissioner and the person comes to know the information is false, the person must take reasonable remedial measures to provide the accurate information.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.146.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.81.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 81</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PUBLIC WATERS DESIGNATION AND USE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.201">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.201 PUBLIC WATERS INVENTORY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall maintain a public waters inventory map of each county that shows the waters of this state that are designated as public waters under the public waters inventory and classification procedures prescribed under Laws 1979, chapter 199, and shall provide access to a copy of the maps. As county public waters inventory maps are revised according to this section, the commissioner shall send a notification or a copy of the maps to the auditor of each affected county.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner must revise the map of public waters established under Laws 1979, chapter 199, to reclassify those types 3, 4, and 5 wetlands previously identified as public waters wetlands under Laws 1979, chapter 199, as public waters or as wetlands under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005">103G.005</a>, subdivision 19. The commissioner may only reclassify public waters wetlands as public waters if:</p>
        <p>(1) they are assigned a shoreland management classification by the commissioner under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.201">103F.201</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.221">103F.221</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) they are classified as lacustrine wetlands or deepwater habitats according to Classification of Wetlands and Deepwater Habitats of the United States (Cowardin, et al., 1979 edition); or</p>
        <p>(3) the state or federal government has become titleholder to any of the beds or shores of the public waters wetlands, subsequent to the preparation of the public waters inventory map filed with the auditor of the county, pursuant to paragraph (a), and the responsible state or federal agency declares that the water is necessary for the purposes of the public ownership.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner must provide notice of the reclassification to the local government unit, the county board, the watershed district, if one exists for the area, and the soil and water conservation district. Within 60 days of receiving notice from the commissioner, a party required to receive the notice may provide a resolution stating objections to the reclassification. If the commissioner receives an objection from a party required to receive the notice, the reclassification is not effective. If the commissioner does not receive an objection from a party required to receive the notice, the reclassification of a wetland under paragraph (b) is effective 60 days after the notice is received by all of the parties.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner shall give priority to the reclassification of public waters wetlands that are or have the potential to be affected by public works projects.</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner may revise the public waters inventory map of each county:</p>
        <p>(1) to reflect the changes authorized in paragraph (b); and</p>
        <p>(2) as needed, to:</p>
        <p>(i) correct errors in the original inventory;</p>
        <p>(ii) add or subtract trout stream tributaries within sections that contain a designated trout stream following written notice to the landowner;</p>
        <p>(iii) add depleted quarries, and sand and gravel pits, when the body of water exceeds 50 acres and the shoreland has been zoned for residential development; and</p>
        <p>(iv) add or subtract public waters that have been created or eliminated as a requirement of a permit authorized by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.245">103G.245</a>.</p>
        <p>(f) $1,000,000 is appropriated from the general fund each year in fiscal years 2025 through 2032 to the commissioner to update the public water inventory as required in this section. The commissioner must develop and implement a process to update the public water inventory. This paragraph expires June 30, 2032.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.201.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.13.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.3.0">2000 c 382 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.24.0">2001 c 7 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/146/#laws.0.5.0">2001 c 146 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/138/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 138 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.45.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.51.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.48.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 48</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.205">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.205 EFFECT OF PUBLIC WATERS DESIGNATION.</h1>
        <p>The designation of waters of this state as public waters does not:</p>
        <p>(1) grant the public additional or greater right of access to the waters;</p>
        <p>(2) diminish the right of ownership or usage of the beds underlying the designated public waters;</p>
        <p>(3) affect state law forbidding trespass on private lands; and</p>
        <p>(4) require the commissioner to acquire access to the designated public waters under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.141">97A.141</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.205.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.14.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.211">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.211 DRAINING PUBLIC WATERS PROHIBITED WITHOUT REPLACEMENT.</h1>
        <p>Except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.221">103G.221</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.235">103G.235</a>, public waters may not be drained, and a permit authorizing drainage of public waters may not be issued, unless the public waters to be drained are replaced by public waters that will have equal or greater public value.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.211.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.15.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.215">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.215 AGRICULTURAL USE OF PUBLIC WATERS DURING DROUGHT.</h1>
        <p>A property owner may use the bed of public waters for pasture or cropland during periods of drought if:</p>
        <p>(1) dikes, ditches, tile lines, or buildings are not constructed; and</p>
        <p>(2) the agricultural use does not result in the drainage of the public waters.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.215.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.16.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.216">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.216 REPORTING FISH KILLS IN PUBLIC WATERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.216.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2165">103G.2165</a>, "fish kill" means an incident resulting in the death of 25 or more fish within one linear mile of a flowing water or 25 or more fish within a square mile of a nonflowing water, excluding fish lawfully taken under the game and fish laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.216.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reporting requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>A state or county staff person or official who learns of a fish kill in public waters must report the location of the fish kill to the Minnesota state duty officer within one hour of being notified of a fish kill or within four hours of first observing the fish kill. The Minnesota state duty officer must alert the Departments of Agriculture, Health, and Natural Resources and the Pollution Control Agency of the location of the fish kill within one hour of being notified of the fish kill. When a fish kill is reported, it must be posted to the <i>EQB Monitor</i> in the next scheduled posting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.216.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.82.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 82</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2165">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2165 DEVELOPMENT OF FISH KILL RESPONSE PROTOCOL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2165.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Development of protocol.</span></h2>
          <p>By June 30, 2024, the commissioners of agriculture, health, and natural resources and the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency must update the fish kill response guidance by developing a protocol. The protocol must consist of steps that state agencies responding to a report of a fish kill under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.216">103G.216</a> must take to ascertain cause of or contributing factors to the fish kill based on scientific data and information gathered through investigation, as well as a communication plan to inform the public of potential hazards. The protocol must address:</p>
          <p>(1) how to approach sampling for aquatic life in most fish kill situations;</p>
          <p>(2) the types of locations from which samples described in clause (1) should be taken;</p>
          <p>(3) the types of locations where water samples should be taken from the body of water in which the fish kill occurred, as well as tributary streams and private wells with landowner consent that should also be sampled;</p>
          <p>(4) the types of locations from which soil and groundwater samples should be taken to ascertain whether contaminants traveled overland or underground to reach the body of water in which the fish kill occurred;</p>
          <p>(5) where other sampling should occur to determine the presence of contaminants that may have contributed to the fish kill;</p>
          <p>(6) developing a comprehensive list of contaminants, including degradation products, for which the materials sampled in clauses (3) to (5) should be tested;</p>
          <p>(7) the appropriate concentration limits to be used in testing samples for the presence of contaminants, allowing for the possibility that the fish kill may have resulted from the interaction of two or more contaminants present at concentrations below the level associated with toxic effects resulting from exposure to each individual chemical;</p>
          <p>(8) proper handling, storage, and treatment necessary to preserve the integrity of the samples described in this subdivision to maximize the information the samples can yield regarding the cause of the fish kill;</p>
          <p>(9) the organs and other parts of the fish and other aquatic creatures that should be analyzed to maximize the information the samples can yield regarding the cause of the fish kill;</p>
          <p>(10) identifying a rapid response team of interagency staff or an independent contractor with the necessary data collection equipment that can travel to the site of the fish kill to collect samples within 24 to 48 hours of the incident;</p>
          <p>(11) a communications plan with a health-risk assessment to notify potentially impacted downstream users of the surface water of the potential hazards and those in the vicinity whose public or private water supply, including surface water or groundwater, may be impacted; and</p>
          <p>(12) the proposed content and timing for investigation reports filed following fish kills. Investigation reports should identify the probable causes and include recommendations to prevent similar incidents in the future.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2165.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Review of protocol.</span></h2>
          <p>The Departments of Agriculture, Health, and Natural Resources and the Pollution Control Agency must post the draft protocol to their websites for a 60-day period for public review and comment. The Departments of Agriculture, Health, and Natural Resources and the Pollution Control Agency must hold one or more public informational meetings on the draft protocol. The Departments of Agriculture, Health, and Natural Resources and the Pollution Control Agency must consider comments submitted during the public comment period before posting the final protocol to their websites.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2165.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>Once the protocol has been published, the relevant state agencies must follow the protocol and must maintain data related to each fish kill response documenting the extent to which the protocol was followed and any reasons why it was not. Once the protocol is in effect, investigation reports for fish kills must be posted to the <i>EQB Monitor</i>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2165.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Updating protocol.</span></h2>
          <p>The updated protocol must be reviewed by the commissioners of agriculture, health, and natural resources and the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency at least every five years according to the procedures in this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2165.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.83.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 83</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.217">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.217 DRIFTLESS AREA; WATER RESOURCES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Groundwater discharge from natural springs and seepage areas in the driftless area of Minnesota, corresponding to the area of the state contained within the boundaries of the Department of Natural Resources Paleozoic plateau ecological section, is vital to sustaining the coldwater aquatic ecosystems in the region, as well as the recreational, commercial, agricultural, environmental, aesthetic, and economic well-being of the region.</p>
        <p>(b) Within the boundaries of the Department of Natural Resources Paleozoic plateau ecological section, no excavation or mining of silica sand, including, but not limited to, digging, excavating, mining, drilling, blasting, tunneling, dredging, stripping, or shafting, may occur within one mile of a designated trout stream as listed in Minnesota Rules unless a silica sand mining trout stream setback permit has been issued by the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(c) Before issuing a permit under this section, the commissioner shall:</p>
        <p>(1) require a project proposer to do a hydrogeological evaluation and collect any other information necessary to assess potential impacts to hydrogeological features, including private and public drinking water supply wells; and</p>
        <p>(2) identify appropriate setbacks from designated trout streams, springs, and other hydrogeologic features and any other restrictions necessary to protect trout stream water quantity, quality, and habitat.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner may assess the project proposer fees to cover the reasonable costs of duties performed under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.217.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.66.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 66</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WETLANDS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.221">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.221 DRAINING PUBLIC WATERS WETLANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.221.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Draining public waters wetlands prohibited without replacement.</span></h2>
          <p>Public waters wetlands may not be drained, and a permit authorizing drainage of public waters wetlands may not be issued, unless the public waters wetlands to be drained are replaced by wetlands that will have equal or greater public value.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.221.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.10.12.0">1991 c 354 art 10 s 12</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.221.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.10.12.0">1991 c 354 art 10 s 12</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.221.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.17.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.1.19.0">1999 c 86 art 1 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2212">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2212 CONTRACTOR'S RESPONSIBILITY WHEN WORK DRAINS OR FILLS WETLANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2212.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conditions to drain or fill wetlands.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An agent or employee of another may not drain or fill a wetland, wholly or partially, unless the agent or employee has:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) obtained a signed statement from the property owner stating that the wetland replacement plan required for the work has been obtained or that a replacement plan is not required; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) mailed or sent by electronic transmission a copy of the statement to the local government unit with jurisdiction over the wetland.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2212.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Violation; separate offense.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Violation of this section is a separate and independent offense from other violations of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2212">103G.2212</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.237">103G.237</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2212.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Form for compliance.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The board shall develop a form to be distributed to contractors' associations, local government units, and soil and water conservation districts to comply with this section. The form must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a listing of the activities for which a replacement plan is required;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a description of the penalties for violating sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2212">103G.2212</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.237">103G.237</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the telephone number to call for information on the responsible local government unit;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a statement that national wetland inventory maps are on file with the soil and water conservation district office; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) spaces for a description of the work and the names, mailing addresses or other contact information, and telephone numbers of the person authorizing the work and the agent or employee proposing to undertake it.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2212.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 382 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.65.0">2011 c 107 s 65</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.222">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.222 REPLACEMENT OF WETLANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.222.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Wetlands must not be drained or filled, wholly or partially, unless replaced by actions that provide at least equal public value under a replacement plan approved as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>, a replacement plan under a local governmental unit's comprehensive wetland protection and management plan approved by the board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2243">103G.2243</a>, or, if a permit to mine is required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.481">93.481</a>, under a mining reclamation plan approved by the commissioner under the permit to mine. Project-specific wetland-replacement plans submitted as part of a project for which a permit to mine is required and approved by the commissioner on or after July 1, 1991, may include surplus wetland credits to be allocated by the commissioner to offset future mining-related wetland impacts under any permits to mine held by the permittee, the operator, the permittee's or operator's parent, an affiliated subsidiary, or an assignee pursuant to an assignment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.481#stat.93.481.5">93.481, subdivision 5</a>. For project-specific wetland replacement completed prior to wetland impacts authorized or conducted under a permit to mine within the Great Lakes and Rainy River watershed basins, those basins are considered a single watershed for purposes of determining wetland-replacement ratios. Mining reclamation plans must apply the same principles and standards for replacing wetlands that are applicable to mitigation plans approved as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>. The commissioner must provide notice of an application for wetland replacement under a permit to mine to the county in which the impact is proposed and the county in which a mitigation site is proposed. Public value must be determined in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.3355">103B.3355</a> or a comprehensive wetland protection and management plan established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2243">103G.2243</a>. Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.221">103G.221</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2372">103G.2372</a> also apply to excavation in permanently and semipermanently flooded areas of wetlands.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Replacement must be guided by the following principles in descending order of priority:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) avoiding the direct or indirect impact of the activity that may destroy or diminish the wetland;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) minimizing the impact by limiting the degree or magnitude of the wetland activity and its implementation;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) rectifying the impact by repairing, rehabilitating, or restoring the affected wetland environment;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) reducing or eliminating the impact over time by preservation and maintenance operations during the life of the activity;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) compensating for the impact by restoring a wetland; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) compensating for the impact by replacing or providing substitute wetland resources or environments.</p>
          <p class="bl">For a project involving the draining or filling of wetlands in an amount not exceeding 10,000 square feet more than the applicable amount in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2241#stat.103G.2241.9">103G.2241, subdivision 9</a>, paragraph (a), the local government unit may make an on-site sequencing determination without a written alternatives analysis from the applicant.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If a wetland is located in a cultivated field, then replacement must be accomplished through restoration only without regard to the priority order in paragraph (b), provided that the altered wetland is not converted to a nonagricultural use for at least ten years.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If a wetland is replaced under paragraph (c), or drained under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2241">103G.2241</a>, subdivision 1, clause (1), the local government unit may require a deed restriction that prohibits nonagricultural use for at least ten years. The local government unit may require the deed restriction if it determines the wetland area drained is at risk of conversion to a nonagricultural use within ten years based on the zoning classification, proximity to a municipality or full service road, or other criteria as determined by the local government unit.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Restoration and replacement of wetlands must be accomplished in accordance with the ecology of the landscape area affected and ponds that are created primarily to fulfill stormwater management, and water quality treatment requirements may not be used to satisfy replacement requirements under this chapter unless the design includes pretreatment of runoff and the pond is functioning as a wetland.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Except as provided in paragraph (g), for a wetland or public waters wetland located on nonagricultural land, replacement must be in the ratio of two acres of replaced wetland for each acre of drained or filled wetland.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) For a wetland or public waters wetland located on agricultural land or in a greater than 80 percent area, replacement must be in the ratio of one acre of replaced wetland for each acre of drained or filled wetland.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Wetlands that are restored or created as a result of an approved replacement plan are subject to the provisions of this section for any subsequent drainage or filling.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) Except in a greater than 80 percent area, only wetlands that have been restored from previously drained or filled wetlands, wetlands created by excavation in nonwetlands, wetlands created by dikes or dams along public or private drainage ditches, or wetlands created by dikes or dams associated with the restoration of previously drained or filled wetlands may be used for wetland replacement according to rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.1">103G.2242, subdivision 1</a>. Modification or conversion of nondegraded naturally occurring wetlands from one type to another are not eligible for wetland replacement.</p>
          <p class="in">(j) The Technical Evaluation Panel established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.2">103G.2242, subdivision 2</a>, shall ensure that sufficient time has occurred for the wetland to develop wetland characteristics of soils, vegetation, and hydrology before recommending that the wetland be deposited in the statewide wetland bank. If the Technical Evaluation Panel has reason to believe that the wetland characteristics may change substantially, the panel shall postpone its recommendation until the wetland has stabilized.</p>
          <p class="in">(k) This section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.223">103G.223</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2364">103G.2364</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2365">103G.2365 </a>apply to the state and its departments and agencies.</p>
          <p class="in">(l) For projects involving draining or filling of wetlands associated with a new public transportation project, and for projects expanded solely for additional traffic capacity, public transportation authorities may purchase credits from the board at the cost to the board to establish credits. Proceeds from the sale of credits provided under this paragraph are appropriated to the board for the purposes of this paragraph. For the purposes of this paragraph, "transportation project" does not include an airport project.</p>
          <p class="in">(m) A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for individual projects that result in the filling or draining of wetlands for the repair, rehabilitation, reconstruction, or replacement of a currently serviceable existing state, city, county, or town public road necessary, as determined by the public transportation authority, to meet state or federal design or safety standards or requirements, excluding new roads or roads expanded solely for additional traffic capacity lanes. This paragraph only applies to authorities for public transportation projects that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) minimize the amount of wetland filling or draining associated with the project and consider mitigating important site-specific wetland functions on site;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) except as provided in clause (3), submit project-specific reports to the board, the Technical Evaluation Panel, the commissioner of natural resources, and members of the public requesting a copy at least 30 days prior to construction that indicate the location, amount, and type of wetlands to be filled or drained by the project or, alternatively, convene an annual meeting of the parties required to receive notice to review projects to be commenced during the upcoming year; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) for minor and emergency maintenance work impacting less than 10,000 square feet, submit project-specific reports, within 30 days of commencing the activity, to the board that indicate the location, amount, and type of wetlands that have been filled or drained.</p>
          <p class="in">Those required to receive notice of public transportation projects may appeal minimization, delineation, and on-site mitigation decisions made by the public transportation authority to the board according to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.9">103G.2242, subdivision 9</a>. The Technical Evaluation Panel must review minimization and delineation decisions made by the public transportation authority and provide recommendations regarding on-site mitigation if requested to do so by the local government unit, a contiguous landowner, or a member of the Technical Evaluation Panel.</p>
          <p class="in">Except for public transportation projects that occur on state roads, for which the state Department of Transportation is responsible for the wetland replacement, the board must replace the wetlands, and wetland areas of public waters if authorized by the commissioner or a delegated authority, drained or filled by public transportation projects on existing roads.</p>
          <p class="in">Public transportation authorities at their discretion may deviate from federal and state design standards on existing road projects when practical and reasonable to avoid wetland filling or draining, provided that public safety is not unreasonably compromised. The local road authority and its officers and employees are exempt from liability for any tort claim for injury to persons or property arising from travel on the highway and related to the deviation from the design standards for construction or reconstruction under this paragraph. This paragraph does not preclude an action for damages arising from negligence in construction or maintenance on a highway.</p>
          <p class="in">(n) If a landowner seeks approval of a replacement plan after the proposed project has already affected the wetland, the local government unit may require the landowner to replace the affected wetland at a ratio not to exceed twice the replacement ratio otherwise required.</p>
          <p class="in">(o) A local government unit may request the board to reclassify a county or watershed on the basis of its percentage of presettlement wetlands remaining. After receipt of satisfactory documentation from the local government, the board shall change the classification of a county or watershed. If requested by the local government unit, the board must assist in developing the documentation. Within 30 days of its action to approve a change of wetland classifications, the board shall publish a notice of the change in the Environmental Quality Board Monitor.</p>
          <p class="in">(p) One hundred citizens who reside within the jurisdiction of the local government unit may request the local government unit to reclassify a county or watershed on the basis of its percentage of presettlement wetlands remaining. In support of their petition, the citizens shall provide satisfactory documentation to the local government unit.
