The local government unit shall consider the petition and forward the request to the board under paragraph (o) or provide a reason why the petition is denied.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.222.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Restoration project; priorities.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall give priority to restoration projects that:</p>
          <p>(1) will encourage land use that leads to more compact development or redevelopment;</p>
          <p>(2) will encourage public or private infrastructure investments which connect ecosystems and communities, attract private sector investment in commercial or residential properties adjacent to the public improvement; or</p>
          <p>(3) are located in critical rural and urban watersheds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.222.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Wetland replacement siting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Impacted wetlands outside of a greater than 80 percent area must not be replaced in a greater than 80 percent area. All wetland replacement must follow this priority order:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) in the same minor watershed as the impacted wetland;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) in the same watershed as the impacted wetland;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) in the same wetland bank service area as the impacted wetland; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) in another wetland bank service area.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), wetland banking credits approved according to a complete wetland banking application submitted to a local government unit by April 1, 1996, may be used to replace wetland impacts resulting from public transportation projects statewide.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), clauses (1) and (2), the priority order for replacement by wetland banking begins at paragraph (a), clause (3), according to rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.1">103G.2242, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) When reasonable, practicable, and environmentally beneficial replacement opportunities are not available in siting priorities listed in paragraph (a), the applicant may seek opportunities at the next level.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) For the purposes of this section, "reasonable, practicable, and environmentally beneficial replacement opportunities" are defined as opportunities that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) take advantage of naturally occurring hydrogeomorphological conditions and require minimal landscape alteration;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) have a high likelihood of becoming a functional wetland that will continue in perpetuity;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) do not adversely affect other habitat types or ecological communities that are important in maintaining the overall biological diversity of the area; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) are available and capable of being done after taking into consideration cost, existing technology, and logistics consistent with overall project purposes.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Regulatory agencies, local government units, and other entities involved in wetland restoration shall collaborate to identify potential replacement opportunities within their jurisdictional areas.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The board must establish wetland-replacement ratios and wetland bank service area priorities to implement the siting and targeting of wetland replacement and encourage the use of high priority areas for wetland replacement.</p>
          <p>(h) Wetland replacement sites identified in accordance with the priority order for replacement siting in paragraph (a) as part of the completion of an adequate environmental impact statement may be approved for a replacement plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.481">93.481</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2243">103G.2243</a> without further modification related to the priority order, notwithstanding availability of new mitigation sites or availability of credits after completion of an adequate environmental impact statement. Wetland-replacement-plan applications must be submitted within one year of the adequacy determination of the environmental impact statement to be eligible for approval under this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.222.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.8.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/175/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 175 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/627/#laws.0.3.0">1994 c 627 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.24.0">1996 c 462 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 382 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.112.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 112</a>,113; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.44.0">2004 c 255 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.120.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 120</a>,121; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/179/#laws.0.33.0">2008 c 179 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.66.0">2011 c 107 s 66</a>,67; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.41.0">2012 c 272 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.82.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 82</a>,83; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.110.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 110</a>,111; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.3.78.0">2024 c 90 art 3 s 78</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.223">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.223 CALCAREOUS FENS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Calcareous fens, as identified by the commissioner by written order published in the State Register, may not be filled, drained, or otherwise degraded, wholly or partially, by any activity, unless the commissioner, under an approved management plan, decides some alteration is necessary or as provided in paragraph (b). Identifications made by the commissioner are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386 </a>does not apply.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner may allow water appropriations that result in temporary reductions in groundwater resources on a seasonal basis under an approved calcareous fen management plan.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.223.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.9.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.43.0">2004 c 221 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.112.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 112</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2241">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2241 EXEMPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agricultural activities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for:</p>
          <p>(1) impacts to wetlands on agricultural land labeled prior-converted cropland and impacts to wetlands resulting from drainage maintenance activities authorized by the United States Department of Agriculture, Natural Resources Conservation Service, on areas labeled farmed wetland, farmed-wetland pasture, and wetland. The prior-converted cropland, farmed wetland, farmed-wetland pasture, or wetland must be labeled on a valid final certified wetland determination issued by the Natural Resources Conservation Service in accordance with Code of Federal Regulations, title 7, part 12, as amended. It is the responsibility of the owner or operator of the land to provide a copy of the final certified wetland determination to, and allow the Natural Resources Conservation Service to share related information with, the local government unit and the board for purposes of verification;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) activities in a wetland conducted as part of normal farming practices. For purposes of this clause, "normal farming practices" means farming, silvicultural, grazing, and ranching activities such as plowing, seeding, cultivating, and harvesting for the production of feed, food, and fiber products, but does not include activities that result in the draining of wetlands;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) soil and water conservation practices approved by the soil and water conservation district, after review by the Technical Evaluation Panel;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) wetland impacts resulting from aquaculture activities, including pond excavation and construction and maintenance of associated access roads and dikes, authorized under and conducted in accordance with a permit issued by the United States Army Corps of Engineers under section 404 of the federal Clean Water Act, United States Code, title 33, section 1344, but not including construction or expansion of buildings;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) wetland impacts resulting from wild rice production activities, including necessary diking and other activities, authorized under and conducted in accordance with a permit issued by the United States Army Corps of Engineers under section 404 of the federal Clean Water Act, United States Code, title 33, section 1344; or</p>
          <p class="in">(6) agricultural activities on agricultural land that is subject to the swampbuster provisions of the federal farm program restrictions consistent with a memorandum of understanding and related agreements between the board and the United States Department of Agriculture, Natural Resources Conservation Service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Drainage.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A replacement plan is not required for draining or filling of wetlands, except for draining wetlands that have been in existence for more than 25 years, resulting from maintenance and repair of existing drainage systems, including public drainage systems.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A public drainage authority may, as part of the repair of a public drainage system, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103E.005#stat.103E.005.12">103E.005, subdivision 12</a>, install control structures, realign the ditch, construct dikes along the ditch, or make other modifications as necessary to prevent the drainage of wetlands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Federal approvals.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for activities authorized under the federal Clean Water Act, section 404, or the Rivers and Harbors Act, section 10, regulations that meet minimum state standards under this chapter and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103A.202">103A.202</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.3355">103B.3355</a> and that have been approved by the Board of Water and Soil Resources, the commissioners of natural resources and agriculture, and the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Wetland restoration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) activities in a wetland restored or created for conservation purposes under a contract or easement providing the landowner with the right to drain the restored or created wetland; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) activities in a wetland restored or created by a landowner without any assistance or financing from public agencies or private entities other than the landowner and the wetland has not been used for wetland replacement or deposited in the state wetland bank.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Incidental wetlands.</span></h2>
          <p>A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for activities in a wetland created solely as a result of:</p>
          <p>(1) beaver dam construction;</p>
          <p>(2) blockage of culverts through roadways maintained by a public or private entity;</p>
          <p>(3) actions by public or private entities that were taken for a purpose other than creating the wetland; or</p>
          <p>(4) any combination of clauses (1) to (3).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Utilities; public works.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for wetland impacts resulting from:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) new placement or maintenance, repair, enhancement, realignment, or replacement of existing utility or utility-type service, including pipelines, when wetland impacts are authorized under and conducted in accordance with a permit issued by the United States Army Corps of Engineers under section 404 of the federal Clean Water Act, United States Code, title 33, section 1344, and the direct and indirect impacts of the proposed project have been avoided and minimized to the extent possible;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) activities associated with operation, routine maintenance, or emergency repair of existing utilities and public work structures, including pipelines, provided the activities do not result in additional wetland intrusion or additional draining or filling of a wetland either wholly or partially; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) repair and updating of existing subsurface sewage treatment systems necessary to comply with local, state, and federal regulations.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Work of an emergency nature may proceed as necessary, and any drain or fill activities must be addressed with the local government unit after the emergency work has been completed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Forestry.</span></h2>
          <p>A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for:</p>
          <p>(1) temporarily crossing or entering a wetland to perform silvicultural activities, including timber harvest as part of a forest management activity, so long as the activity limits the impact on the hydrologic and biologic characteristics of the wetland; the activities do not result in the construction of dikes, drainage ditches, tile lines, or buildings; and the timber harvesting and other silvicultural practices do not result in the drainage of the wetland or public waters; or</p>
          <p>(2) permanent access for forest roads across wetlands so long as the activity limits the impact on the hydrologic and biologic characteristics of the wetland; the construction activities do not result in the access becoming a dike, drainage ditch, or tile line; filling is avoided wherever possible; and there is no drainage of the wetland or public waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.170.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 170</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">De minimis.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in paragraphs (d), (e), (f), (g), (h), and (i), a replacement plan for wetlands is not required for impacts to the following amounts of wetlands, excluding the permanently and semipermanently flooded areas of wetlands, as part of a project outside of the shoreland wetland protection zone:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) one-quarter acre of wetland in a greater than 80 percent area;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) one-tenth acre of wetland in a 50 to 80 percent area; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) one-twentieth acre of wetland in a less than 50 percent area.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided in paragraphs (e), (f), (g), (h), and (i), a replacement plan for wetlands is not required for up to 100 square feet of impacts to wetlands as part of a project within the shoreland wetland protection zone beyond the shoreland building setback zone.</p>
          <p>(c) Except as provided in paragraphs (e), (f), (g), (h), and (i), a replacement plan for wetlands is not required for up to 20 square feet of impacts to wetlands as part of a project within the shoreland building setback zone, as defined in the local shoreland management ordinance. The amount in this paragraph may be increased to 100 square feet if permanent water runoff retention or infiltration measures are established in proximity as approved by the shoreland management authority.</p>
          <p>(d) Except as provided in paragraphs (c), (e), (f), (g), (h), and (i), a replacement plan is not required for up to 400 square feet of impacts to the permanently and semipermanently flooded areas of wetlands as part of a project.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The amounts listed in paragraphs (a), (b), (c), and (d) may not be combined on a project.</p>
          <p>(f) When the total area of impacts to wetlands as part of a project exceeds the applicable amount in this subdivision, a replacement plan is required for the entire amount.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) This exemption may not be combined with another exemption in this section on a project.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Property may not be divided to increase the amounts listed in paragraph (a), (b), (c), or (d).</p>
          <p>(i) If a local ordinance or similar local control is more restrictive than this subdivision, the local standard applies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Wildlife habitat.</span></h2>
          <p>A replacement plan for wetlands is not required for:</p>
          <p>(1) deposition of spoil resulting from excavation within a wetland for a wildlife habitat improvement project, if:</p>
          <p>(i) the area of deposition does not exceed five percent of the wetland area or one-half acre, whichever is less, and the spoil is stabilized and permanently seeded to prevent erosion;</p>
          <p>(ii) the project does not have an adverse impact on any species designated as endangered or threatened under state or federal law; and</p>
          <p>(iii) the project will provide wildlife habitat improvement as certified by the soil and water conservation district; or</p>
          <p>(2) duck blinds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2241.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Exemption conditions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A person conducting an activity in a wetland under an exemption in subdivisions 1 to 10 shall ensure that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) appropriate erosion control measures are taken to prevent sedimentation of the water;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the activity does not block fish passage in a watercourse; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the activity is conducted in compliance with all other applicable federal, state, and local requirements, including best management practices and water resource protection requirements established under chapter 103H.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) An activity is exempt if it qualifies for any one of the exemptions, even though it may be indicated as not exempt under another exemption.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Persons proposing to conduct an exempt activity are encouraged to contact the local government unit or the local government unit's designee for advice on minimizing wetland impacts.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The board shall develop rules that address the application and implementation of exemptions and that provide for estimates and reporting of exempt wetland impacts, including those in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2241">103G.2241, subdivisions 2</a>, 6, and 9.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2241.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.10.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/175/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 175 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/226/#laws.0.20.0">1993 c 226 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/627/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 627 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.25.0">1996 c 462 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.6.0">2000 c 382 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.122.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 122</a>-127; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.55.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/109/#laws.0.14.0">2009 c 109 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.42.0">2012 c 272 s 42</a>,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.3.79.0">2024 c 90 art 3 s 79</a>-82</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2242">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2242 WETLAND VALUE REPLACEMENT PLANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board, in consultation with the commissioner, shall adopt rules governing the approval of wetland value replacement plans under this section and public-waters-work permits affecting public waters wetlands under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.245">103G.245</a>. These rules must address the criteria, procedure, timing, and location of acceptable replacement of wetland values and may address the state establishment and administration of a wetland banking program for public and private projects, including provisions for an in-lieu fee program; mitigating and banking other water and water-related resources; the administrative, monitoring, and enforcement procedures to be used; provisions that protect, or mitigate impacts to, intermittent and perennial watercourses upstream of public waters identified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.15">103G.005, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (9) or (10); and a procedure for the review and appeal of decisions under this section. In the case of peatlands, the replacement plan rules must consider the impact on carbon. Any in-lieu fee program established by the board must conform with Code of Federal Regulations, title 33, section 332.8, as amended.</p>
          <p>(b) After the adoption of the rules, a replacement plan must be approved by a resolution of the governing body of the local government unit, consistent with the provisions of the rules or a comprehensive wetland protection and management plan approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2243">103G.2243</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) If the local government unit fails to apply the rules or fails to implement a local comprehensive wetland protection and management plan established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2243">103G.2243</a>, the government unit is subject to penalty as determined by the board.</p>
          <p>(d) When making a determination under rules adopted pursuant to this subdivision on whether a rare natural community will be permanently adversely affected, consideration of measures to mitigate any adverse effect on the community must be considered.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Questions concerning the public value, location, size, or type of a wetland must be submitted to and determined by a Technical Evaluation Panel after an on-site inspection. The Technical Evaluation Panel must be composed of a technical professional employee of the board, a technical professional employee of the local soil and water conservation district or districts, a technical professional with expertise in water resources management appointed by the local government unit, and a technical professional employee of the Department of Natural Resources for projects affecting public waters or wetlands adjacent to public waters.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For wetland boundary determinations, the panel must use <i>Corps of Engineers Wetland Delineation Manual</i>, United States Army Corps of Engineers (January 1987), including updates, supplementary guidance, and replacements, if any. For wetland type determinations, the panel must also use <i>Wetlands of the United States</i>, United States Fish and Wildlife Service Circular 39 (1971 edition); <i>Classification of Wetlands and Deepwater Habitats of the United States</i>, United States Fish and Wildlife Service (August 2013 edition); or <i>A Hydrogeomorphic Classification for Wetlands</i>, United States Army Corps of Engineers (August 1993), according to rules authorized under this part and including updates, supplementary guidance, and replacements, if any, for any of these publications.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The panel must provide the wetland determination and recommendations on other technical matters to the local government unit that must approve a replacement plan, sequencing, exemption determination, no-loss determination, or wetland boundary or type determination and may recommend approval or denial of the plan. The authority must consider and include the decision of the Technical Evaluation Panel in their approval or denial of a plan or determination.</p>
          <p>(d) A member of the Technical Evaluation Panel that has a financial interest in a wetland bank or management responsibility to sell or make recommendations in their official capacity to sell credits from a publicly owned wetland bank must disclose that interest, in writing, to the Technical Evaluation Panel and the local government unit.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Persons conducting wetland or public waters boundary delineations or type determinations are exempt from the requirements of chapter 326. The board may develop a professional wetland delineator certification program.</p>
          <p>(f) The board must establish an interagency team to assist in identifying and evaluating potential wetland replacement sites. The team must consist of members of the Technical Evaluation Panel and representatives from the Department of Natural Resources; the Pollution Control Agency; the United States Army Corps of Engineers, St. Paul district; and other organizations as determined by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Wetland boundary or type determination.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A landowner may apply for a wetland boundary or type determination from the local government unit. The landowner applying for the determination is responsible for submitting proof necessary to make the determination, including, but not limited to, wetland delineation field data, observation well data, topographic mapping, survey mapping, and information regarding soils, vegetation, hydrology, and groundwater both within and outside of the proposed wetland boundary.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A local government unit that receives an application under paragraph (a) may seek the advice of the Technical Evaluation Panel as described in subdivision 2 and, if necessary, expand the Technical Evaluation Panel. The local government unit may delegate the decision authority for wetland boundary or type determinations to designated staff or establish other procedures it considers appropriate.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The local government unit decision must be made in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.99">15.99</a>. Within ten calendar days of the decision, the local government unit decision must be mailed or sent by electronic transmission to the landowner, members of the Technical Evaluation Panel, the watershed district or watershed management organization, if one exists, and individual members of the public who request a copy. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.99">15.99</a>, subdivision 2, the board must establish by rule timelines for project review and comment for wetland banking projects.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The local government unit decision is valid for five years unless the Technical Evaluation Panel determines that natural or artificial changes to the hydrology, vegetation, or soils of the area have been sufficient to alter the wetland boundary or type.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Replacement completion.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Replacement of wetland values must be completed prior to or concurrent with the actual draining or filling of a wetland, unless:</p>
          <p>(1) an irrevocable bank letter of credit or other financial assurance acceptable to the local government unit or the board is given to the local government unit or the board to guarantee the successful completion of the replacement; or</p>
          <p>(2) the replacement is approved under an in-lieu fee program according to rules adopted under subdivision 1. In the case of an in-lieu fee program established by a board-approved sponsor, the board may require that a financial assurance in an amount and method acceptable to the board be given to the board to ensure the approved sponsor fulfills the sponsor's obligation to complete the required wetland replacement.</p>
          <p>(b) The board may establish, sponsor, or administer a wetland banking program, which may include provisions allowing monetary payment to the wetland banking program for impacts to wetlands. The board may acquire land in fee title, purchase or accept easements, enter into agreements, and purchase existing wetland replacement credits to facilitate the wetland banking program. The board may establish wetland credit and in-lieu fee payment amounts and hold money in an account in the special revenue fund, which is appropriated to the board to be used solely for establishing replacement wetlands and administering the wetland banking program.</p>
          <p>(c) The board shall coordinate the establishment and operation of a wetland bank with the United States Army Corps of Engineers, the Natural Resources Conservation Service of the United States Department of Agriculture, and the commissioners of natural resources, agriculture, and the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Decision.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receiving and considering all required data, the local government unit reviewing replacement plan applications, sequencing applications, and exemption or no-loss determination requests must act on all replacement plan applications, sequencing applications, and exemption or no-loss determination requests in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.99">15.99</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Processing fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The local government unit and soil and water conservation district may charge processing fees in amounts not greater than are necessary to cover the reasonable costs of implementing the rules adopted under subdivision 1 and for technical and administrative assistance to landowners in processing other applications for projects affecting wetlands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Notice of application.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Application for approval of a replacement plan under this section must be reviewed by the local government according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.99#stat.15.99.3">15.99, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a). Copies of the complete application must be mailed or sent by electronic transmission to the members of the Technical Evaluation Panel, the managers of the watershed district if one exists, and the commissioner of natural resources. Individual members of the public who request a copy shall be provided information to identify the applicant and the location and scope of the project.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For the purpose of this subdivision, "application" includes a revised application for replacement plan approval and an application for a revision to an approved replacement plan if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the wetland area to be drained or filled under the revised replacement plan is at least ten percent larger than the area to be drained or filled under the original replacement plan; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the wetland area to be drained or filled under the revised replacement is located more than 500 feet from the area to be drained or filled under the original replacement plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Notice of decision.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Within ten days of the approval or denial of a replacement plan under this section, notice of the decision must be mailed or sent by electronic transmission to members of the Technical Evaluation Panel, the applicant, individual members of the public who request a copy, the managers of the watershed district, if one exists, and the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Public comment period.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for activities impacting less than 10,000 square feet of wetland, before approval or denial of a replacement plan under this section, comments may be made by the public to the local government unit for a period of 15 days or more, as determined by the local government unit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Appeals to board.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Appeal of a replacement plan, sequencing, exemption, wetland banking, wetland boundary or type determination, or no-loss decision may be obtained by mailing a petition and payment of a filing fee, which shall be retained by the board to defray administrative costs, to the board within 30 days after the postmarked date of the mailing or date of sending by electronic transmission specified in subdivision 7. If appeal is not sought within 30 days, the decision becomes final. If the petition for hearing is accepted, the amount posted must be returned to the petitioner. Appeal may be made by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the wetland owner;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) any of those to whom notice is required to be mailed or sent by electronic transmission under subdivision 7; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) 100 residents of the county in which a majority of the wetland is located.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Within 30 days after receiving a petition, the board shall decide whether to grant the petition and hear the appeal. The board shall grant the petition unless the board finds that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the appeal is without significant merit, trivial, or brought solely for the purposes of delay;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the petitioner has not exhausted all local administrative remedies;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) expanded technical review is needed;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the local government unit's record is not adequate; or</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the petitioner has not posted a letter of credit, cashier's check, or cash if required by the local government unit.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) In determining whether to grant the appeal, the board, executive director, or dispute resolution committee shall also consider the size of the wetland, other factors in controversy, any patterns of similar acts by the local government unit or petitioner, and the consequences of the delay resulting from the appeal.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If an appeal is granted, the appeal must be heard by the committee for dispute resolution of the board, and a decision must be made by the board within 60 days of filing the local government unit's record and the written briefs submitted for the appeal and the hearing. The decision must be served by mail or by electronic transmission to the parties to the appeal and is not subject to the provisions of chapter 14. A decision whether to grant a petition for appeal and a decision on the merits of an appeal must be considered the decision of an agency in a contested case for purposes of judicial review under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the board shall establish a fee schedule to defray the administrative costs of appeals made to the board under this subdivision. Fees established under this authority shall not exceed $1,000. Establishment of the fee is not subject to the rulemaking process of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Appeals of restoration or replacement orders.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A landowner or other responsible party may appeal the terms and conditions of a restoration or replacement order within 30 days of receipt of written notice of the order. The time frame for the appeal may be extended beyond 30 days by mutual agreement, in writing, between the landowner or responsible party, the local government unit, and the enforcement authority. If the written request is not submitted within 30 days, the order is final. The board's executive director must review the request and supporting evidence and render a decision within 60 days of receipt of a petition. A decision on an appeal must be considered the decision of an agency in a contested case for purposes of judicial review under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Local requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The rules adopted under subdivision 1 shall allow for local government units to use their own notice and public comment procedures so long as the requirements of this section are satisfied.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Expired.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Replacement credits.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) No public or private wetland restoration, enhancement, or construction may be allowed for replacement unless specifically designated for replacement and paid for by the individual or organization performing the wetland restoration, enhancement, or construction.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to a wetland whose owner has paid back with interest the individual or organization restoring, enhancing, or constructing the wetland.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222#stat.103G.222.1">103G.222, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (i), the following actions, and others established in rule, that are consistent with criteria in rules adopted by the board in conjunction with the commissioners of natural resources and agriculture, are eligible for replacement credit as determined by the local government unit or the board, including enrollment in a statewide wetlands bank:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) reestablishment of permanent native, noninvasive vegetative cover on a wetland on agricultural land that was planted with annually seeded crops, was in a crop rotation seeding of pasture grasses or legumes, or was in a land retirement program during the past ten years;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) buffer areas of permanent native, noninvasive vegetative cover established or preserved on upland adjacent to replacement wetlands;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) wetlands restored for conservation purposes under terminated easements or contracts;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) water quality treatment ponds constructed to pretreat stormwater runoff prior to discharge to wetlands, public waters, or other water bodies, provided that the water quality treatment ponds must be associated with an ongoing or proposed project that will impact a wetland and replacement credit for the treatment ponds is based on the replacement of wetland functions and on an approved stormwater management plan for the local government; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) in a greater than 80 percent area, restoration and protection of streams and riparian buffers that are important to the functions and sustainability of aquatic resources.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222#stat.103G.222.1">103G.222, subdivision 1</a>, paragraphs (f) and (g), the board may establish by rule different replacement ratios for restoration projects with exceptional natural resource value.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.44.0">1996 c 462 s 44</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Fees established.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Fees must be assessed for managing wetland bank accounts and transactions as follows:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) account maintenance annual fee: one percent of the value of credits not to exceed $500;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) account establishment, deposit, or transfer: 6.5 percent of the value of credits not to exceed $1,000 per establishment, deposit, or transfer; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) withdrawal fee: 6.5 percent of the value of credits withdrawn.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The board must establish fees based on costs to the agency below the amounts in paragraph (a) for single-user or other dedicated wetland banking accounts.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Fees for single-user or other dedicated wetland banking accounts established pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.10i">103G.005, subdivision 10i</a>, clause (4), are limited to establishment of a wetland banking account and are assessed at the rate of 6.5 percent of the value of the credits not to exceed $1,000.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The board may assess a fee to pay the costs associated with establishing conservation easements, or other long-term protection mechanisms prescribed in the rules adopted under subdivision 1, on property used for wetland replacement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2242.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Fees paid to board.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All fees established in subdivisions 9 and 14 must be paid to the Board of Water and Soil Resources and are annually appropriated to the board for the purpose of administration of the wetland bank and to process appeals under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.9">103G.2242, subdivision 9</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2242.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.11.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/175/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 175 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/627/#laws.0.5.0">1994 c 627 s 5</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.26.0">1996 c 462 s 26</a>-32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/312/#laws.0.4.0">1998 c 312 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.9.0">2000 c 382 s 9</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.25.0">2001 c 7 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/146/#laws.0.6.0">2001 c 146 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.114.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 114</a>,115; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.128.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 128</a>-132; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.56.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.68.0">2011 c 107 s 68</a>-73; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.44.0">2012 c 272 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.84.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 84</a>-89,148; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.113.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 113</a>,114; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.85.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.5.20.0">2023 c 60 art 5 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.3.83.0">2024 c 90 art 3 s 83</a>-86</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2243">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2243 LOCAL COMPREHENSIVE WETLAND PROTECTION AND MANAGEMENT PLANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2243.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General requirements; notice and participation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As an alternative to the rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.1">103G.2242, subdivision 1</a>, and the public value criteria established or approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.3355">103B.3355</a>, a comprehensive wetland protection and management plan may be developed by a local government unit, or one or more local government units operating under a joint powers agreement, provided that:</p>
          <p>(1) a notice is made at the beginning of the planning process to the board, the commissioner of natural resources, the Pollution Control Agency, the commissioner of agriculture, local government units, and local citizens to actively participate in the development of the plan; and</p>
          <p>(2) the plan is implemented by ordinance as part of the local government's official controls under chapter 394, for a county; chapter 462, for a city; chapter 366, for a town; and by rules adopted under chapter 103D, for a watershed district; and chapter 103B, for a watershed management organization.</p>
          <p>(b) An organization that is invited to participate in the development of the local plan, but declines to do so and fails to participate or to provide written comments during the local review process, waives the right during board review to submit comments, except comments concerning consistency of the plan with laws and rules administered by that agency. In determining the merit of an agency comment, the board shall consider the involvement of the agency in the development of the local plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2243.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Plan contents.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A comprehensive wetland protection and management plan may:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) provide for classification of wetlands in the plan area based on:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) an inventory of wetlands in the plan area;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) an assessment of the wetland functions listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.3355">103B.3355</a>, using a methodology chosen by the Technical Evaluation Panel from one of the methodologies established or approved by the board under that section; and</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) the resulting public values;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) vary application of the sequencing standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222#stat.103G.222.1">103G.222, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), for projects based on the classification and criteria set forth in the plan;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) vary the replacement standards of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222#stat.103G.222.1">103G.222, subdivision 1</a>, paragraphs (f) and (g), based on the classification and criteria set forth in the plan, for specific wetland impacts provided there is no net loss of public values within the area subject to the plan, and so long as:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) in a 50 to 80 percent area, a minimum acreage requirement of one acre of replaced wetland for each acre of drained or filled wetland requiring replacement is met within the area subject to the plan; and</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) in a less than 50 percent area, a minimum acreage requirement of two acres of replaced wetland for each acre of drained or filled wetland requiring replacement is met within the area subject to the plan, except that replacement for the amount above a 1:1 ratio can be accomplished as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242#stat.103G.2242.12">103G.2242, subdivision 12</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) in a greater than 80 percent area, allow replacement credit, based on the classification and criteria set forth in the plan, for any project that increases the public value of wetlands, including activities on adjacent upland acres.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2243.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Board review and approval; mediation; judicial review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The plan is deemed approved 60 days after the local government submits the final plan to the board, unless the board disagrees with the plan as provided in paragraph (d).</p>
          <p>(b) The board may not disapprove a plan if the board determines the plan meets the requirements of this section.</p>
          <p>(c) In its review of a plan, the board shall advise the local government unit of those elements of the plan that are more restrictive than state law and rules for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.237#stat.103G.237.5">103G.237, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) If the board disagrees with the plan or any elements of the plan, the board shall, in writing, notify the local government of the plan deficiencies and suggested changes. The board shall include in the response to the local government the scientific justification, if applicable, for the board's concerns with the plan. Upon receipt of the board's concerns with the plan, the local government has 60 days to revise the plan and resubmit the plan to the board for reconsideration, or the local government may request a hearing before the board. The board shall hold a hearing within the boundaries of the jurisdiction of the local government within 60 days of the request for hearing. After the hearing, the board shall, within 60 days, prepare a report of its decision and inform the local government.</p>
          <p>(e) If, after the hearing, the board and local government disagree on the plan, the board shall, within 60 days, initiate mediation through a neutral party. If the board and local government unit agree in writing not to use mediation or the mediation does not result in a resolution of the differences between the parties, then the board may commence a declaratory judgment action in the district court of the county where the local government unit is located. If the board does not commence a declaratory judgment action within the applicable 60-day period, the plan is deemed approved.</p>
          <p>(f) The declaratory judgment action must be commenced within 60 days after the date of the written agreement not to use mediation or 60 days after conclusion of the mediation. If the board commences a declaratory judgment action, the district court shall review the board's record of decision and the record of decision of the local government unit. The district court shall affirm the plan if it meets the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2243.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Effective date; replacement decisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The plan becomes effective as provided in subdivision 3, paragraphs (d) to (f), and after adoption of the plan into the official controls of the local government.</p>
          <p>(b) After the effective date of a plan, a local government unit shall make replacement decisions consistent with the plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2243.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Plan amendments.</span></h2>
          <p>Amendments to the plan become effective upon completion of the same process required for the original plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2243.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Water planning processes apply.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided for in this section, all other requirements relating to development of the plan must be consistent with the water plan processes under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.231">103B.231</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.311">103B.311</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2243.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.33.0">1996 c 462 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/2/#laws.0.9.0">1997 c 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/312/#laws.0.5.0">1998 c 312 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.26.0">2001 c 7 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.133.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 133</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2244">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2244 WETLAND CREATION OR RESTORATION WITHIN PIPELINE EASEMENT.</h1>
        <p>A person proposing to create or restore a wetland within the easement of a pipeline as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299J.02#stat.299J.02.11">299J.02, subdivision 11</a>, shall first notify the easement holder and the director of the Office of Pipeline Safety in writing. The person may not create or restore the wetland if, within 90 days after receiving the required notice, the easement holder or the director of the Office of Pipeline Safety provides to the person a written notice of objection that includes the reasons for the objection.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2244.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.34.0">1996 c 462 s 34</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.225">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.225 STATE WETLANDS AND PUBLIC DRAINAGE SYSTEMS.</h1>
        <p>If the state owns public waters wetlands on or adjacent to existing public drainage systems, the state shall consider the use of the public waters wetlands as part of the drainage system. If the public waters wetlands interfere with or prevent the authorized functioning of the public drainage system, the state shall provide for necessary work to allow proper use and maintenance of the drainage system while still preserving the public waters wetlands.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.225.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.18.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.12.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2251">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2251 STATE CONSERVATION EASEMENTS; WETLAND BANK CREDIT.</h1>
        <p class="in">In greater than 80 percent areas, preservation of wetlands, riparian buffers, and watershed areas essential to maintaining important functions and sustainability of aquatic resources in the watershed that are protected by a permanent conservation easement as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84C.01">84C.01</a> and held by the board may be eligible for wetland replacement or mitigation credits, according to rules adopted by the board. To be eligible for credit under this section, a conservation easement must be established after May 24, 2008, and approved by the board. Wetland areas on private lands preserved under this section are not eligible for replacement or mitigation credit if the area has been protected using public conservation funds.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2251.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.26.0">2008 c 357 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.1.14.0">2008 c 368 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.74.0">2011 c 107 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.90.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 90</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.231">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.231 PROPERTY OWNER'S USE OF PUBLIC WATERS WETLANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.231.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agricultural use during drought.</span></h2>
          <p>A property owner may use the bed of public waters wetlands for pasture or cropland during periods of drought if:</p>
          <p>(1) dikes, ditches, tile lines, or buildings are not constructed; and</p>
          <p>(2) the agricultural use does not result in the drainage of the public waters wetlands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.231.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Filling for irrigation booms.</span></h2>
          <p>A landowner may fill a public waters wetland to accommodate wheeled booms on irrigation devices if the fill does not impede normal drainage.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.231.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Peat mining.</span></h2>
          <p>Peat mining, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.461">93.461</a>, is permitted subject to the mine permit and reclamation requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.44">93.44</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.51">93.51</a>, and the rules adopted under those restrictions, except as provided for in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.035">84.035</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.036">84.036</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.231.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.19.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.13.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 13</a>; art 8 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.235">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.235 RESTRICTIONS ON ACCESS TO WETLANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.235.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Wetlands adjacent to roads.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">To protect the public health or safety, local units of government may by ordinance restrict public access to public waters wetlands from municipality, county, or township roads that abut public waters wetlands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.235.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Privately restored or created wetlands.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When a landowner creates a new wetland or restores a formerly existing wetland on private land that is adjacent to public land or a public road right-of-way, there is no public access to the created or restored wetland if posted by the landowner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.235.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.20.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.14.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.134.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 134</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2364">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2364 PROPERTY OWNER'S USE OF WETLANDS.</h1>
        <p>(a) A property owner may use the bed of wetlands for pasture or cropland during periods of drought if:</p>
        <p>(1) dikes, ditches, tile lines, or buildings are not constructed; and</p>
        <p>(2) the agricultural use does not result in the drainage of the wetlands.</p>
        <p>(b) A landowner may fill a wetland to accommodate wheeled booms on irrigation devices if the fill does not impede normal drainage.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2364.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.15.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2365">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2365 CONTROLLING NOXIOUS WEEDS.</h1>
        <p>Noxious weeds, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18.77#stat.18.77.8">18.77, subdivision 8</a>, must be controlled on wetlands as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18.78">18.78</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2365.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.16.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.30.0">1995 c 186 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.103G.2369"><b>103G.2369</b> [Repealed, 1991 354 art 7 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/175/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 175 s 7</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.237">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.237 COMPENSATION FOR LOSS OF PRIVATE USE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.237.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>A person whose replacement plan is not approved must be compensated as provided in this section. The person may drain or fill the wetland without an approved replacement plan if the person:</p>
          <p>(1) is eligible for compensation under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(2) applies for compensation in accordance with subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(3) does not receive the compensation required in subdivision 4 within 90 days after the application for compensation is received by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.237.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A person is eligible for compensation if:</p>
          <p>(1) the person applies for replacement plan approval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) the replacement plan is not approved or the plan conditions make the proposed use unworkable or not feasible;</p>
          <p>(3) the person appeals the disapproval of the plan;</p>
          <p>(4) the proposed use would otherwise be allowed under federal, state, and local laws, rules, ordinances, and other legal requirements;</p>
          <p>(5) the person has suffered or will suffer damages;</p>
          <p>(6) disallowing the proposed use will enhance the public value of the wetland; and</p>
          <p>(7) the person applies to the board for compensation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.237.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for compensation must be made on forms prescribed by the board and include:</p>
          <p>(1) the location and public value of the wetland where the use was proposed;</p>
          <p>(2) a description and reason for the proposed wetland use; and</p>
          <p>(3) the objection to the replacement plan, if any.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.237.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board shall award compensation in an amount equal to the greater of:</p>
          <p>(1) 50 percent of the value of the wetland, calculated by multiplying the acreage of the wetland by the greater of:</p>
          <p>(i) the average equalized estimated market value of agricultural property in the township as established by the commissioner of revenue at the time application for compensation is made; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the assessed value per acre of the parcel containing the wetland, based on the assessed value of the parcel as stated on the most recent tax statement; or</p>
          <p>(2) $200 per acre of wetland subject to the replacement plan, increased or decreased by the percentage change of the assessed valuation of land in the township where the wetland is located from the 1995 valuation.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who receives compensation under paragraph (a) shall convey to the board a permanent conservation easement as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.515#stat.103F.515.4">103F.515, subdivision 4</a>. An easement conveyed under this paragraph is subject to correction and enforcement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.515">103F.515</a>, subdivisions 8 and 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.237.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Compensation claims against local government units.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) At the request of a local government unit against which a compensation action is brought based at least in part on the local government unit's application of this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222">103G.222</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2241">103G.2241</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2243">103G.2243</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2372">103G.2372</a>, or rules adopted by the board to implement these sections, the state, through the attorney general, shall intervene in the action on behalf of the local government unit and shall thereafter be considered a defendant in the action. A local government unit making a request under this paragraph shall provide the attorney general with a copy of the complaint as soon as possible after being served. If requested by the attorney general, the court shall grant additional time to file an answer equal to the time between service of the complaint on the local government unit and receipt of the complaint by the attorney general.</p>
          <p>(b) The state is liable for costs, damages, fees, and compensation awarded in the action based on the local government unit's adoption or implementation of standards that are required by state law, as determined by the court. The local government unit is liable for costs, damages, fees, and compensation awarded in the action based on local standards that are more restrictive than state law and rules.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purposes of this subdivision, "compensation action" means an action in which the plaintiff seeks compensation for a taking of private property under the state or federal constitution.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.237.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.17.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/627/#laws.0.10.0">1994 c 627 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.35.0">1996 c 462 s 35</a>,36</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2372">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2372 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2372.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority; orders.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources, conservation officers, and peace officers shall enforce laws preserving and protecting groundwater quantity, wetlands, and public waters. The commissioner of natural resources, a conservation officer, or a peace officer may issue a cease and desist order to stop any illegal activity adversely affecting groundwater quantity, a wetland, or public waters.</p>
          <p>(b) In the order, or by separate order, the commissioner, conservation officer, or peace officer may require restoration or replacement of the wetland or public waters, as determined by the local soil and water conservation district for wetlands and the commissioner of natural resources for public waters. Restoration or replacement orders may be recorded or filed in the office of the county recorder or registrar of titles, as appropriate, in the county where the real property is located by the commissioner of natural resources, conservation officers, or peace officers as a deed restriction on the property that runs with the land and is binding on the owners, successors, and assigns until the conditions of the order are met or the order is rescinded. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/386.77">386.77</a>, the agency shall pay the applicable filing fee for any document filed under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) If a court has ruled that there has not been a violation of the restoration or replacement order, an order may not be recorded or filed under this section.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner must remove a deed restriction filed or recorded under this section on homesteaded property if the owner requests that it be removed and a court has found that the owner of the property is not guilty or that there has not been a violation of the restoration or replacement order. Within 30 days of receiving the request for removal from the owner, the commissioner must contact, in writing, the office of the county recorder or registrar of titles where the order is recorded or filed, along with all applicable fees, and have the order removed. Within 30 days of receiving notification from the office of the county recorder or registrar of titles that the order has been removed, the commissioner must inform the owner that the order has been removed and provide the owner with a copy of any documentation provided by the office of the county recorder or registrar of titles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2372.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Misdemeanor.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of an order issued under subdivision 1 is a misdemeanor and must be prosecuted by the county attorney where the wetland or public waters are located or the illegal activity occurred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2372.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Restitution.</span></h2>
          <p>The court may, as part of sentencing, require a person convicted under subdivision 2 to restore or replace the wetland or public waters, as determined by the local soil and water conservation district for wetlands and the commissioner of natural resources for public waters.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2372.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.6.18.0">1991 c 354 art 6 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.15.0">2000 c 382 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/146/#laws.0.8.0">2001 c 146 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/138/#laws.0.2.0">2005 c 138 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.115.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 115</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.103G.2373"><b>103G.2373</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/220/#laws.8.16.0">2002 c 220 art 8 s 16</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2374">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2374 ELECTRONIC TRANSMISSION.</h1>
        <p class="in">For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2212">103G.2212</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2372">103G.2372</a>, notices and other documents may be sent by electronic transmission unless the recipient has provided a mailing address and specified that mailing is preferred.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2374.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.75.0">2011 c 107 s 75</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2375">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2375 ASSUMPTION OF SECTION 404 OF FEDERAL CLEAN WATER ACT.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, the Board of Water and Soil Resources, in consultation with the commissioners of natural resources, agriculture, and the Pollution Control Agency, may adopt or amend rules establishing a program for regulating the discharge of dredged and fill material into the waters of the state as necessary to obtain approval from the United States Environmental Protection Agency to administer, in whole or part, the permitting and wetland banking programs under section 404 of the federal Clean Water Act, United States Code, title 33, section 1344. The rules may not be more restrictive than the program under section 404 or state law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2375.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.45.0">2012 c 272 s 45</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WORK AFFECTING PUBLIC WATERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.241">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.241 CONTRACTOR'S RESPONSIBILITY WHEN WORK AFFECTS PUBLIC WATERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.241.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conditions to affect public waters.</span></h2>
          <p>An agent or employee of another may not construct, reconstruct, remove, or make a change in a reservoir, dam, or waterway obstruction on a public water or in any manner change or diminish the course, current, or cross section of public waters unless the agent or employee has:</p>
          <p>(1) obtained a signed statement from the property owner stating that the permits required for the work have been obtained or a permit is not required; and</p>
          <p>(2) mailed or electronically transmitted a copy of the statement to the regional office of the Department of Natural Resources where the proposed work is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.241.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Violation; separate offense.</span></h2>
          <p>Violation of this section is a separate and independent offense from other violations of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.241.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Form for compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop a form to be distributed to contractors' associations and county auditors to comply with this section. The form must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a listing of the activities for which a permit is required;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the penalties for violating this chapter;</p>
          <p>(3) the mailing addresses, electronic mail addresses, and telephone numbers of the regional offices of the Department of Natural Resources;</p>
          <p>(4) a statement that water inventory maps completed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.201">103G.201</a> are on file with the auditors of the counties; and</p>
          <p>(5) spaces for a description of the work and the names, mailing addresses, electronic mail addresses, and telephone numbers of the person authorizing the work and the agent or employee proposing to undertake it.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.241.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
