        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.21.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.86.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 86</a>,87</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.245">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.245 WORK IN PUBLIC WATERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permit requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivisions 2, 11, and 12, the state, a political subdivision of the state, a public or private corporation, or a person must have a public-waters-work permit to:</p>
          <p>(1) construct, reconstruct, remove, abandon, transfer ownership of, or make any change in a reservoir, dam, or waterway obstruction on public waters; or</p>
          <p>(2) change or diminish the course, current, or cross section of public waters, entirely or partially within the state, by any means, including filling, excavating, or placing of materials in or on the beds of public waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>A public-waters-work permit is not required for:</p>
          <p>(1) work in altered natural watercourses that are part of drainage systems established under chapter 103D or 103E if the work in the waters is undertaken according to chapter 103D or 103E;</p>
          <p>(2) a drainage project for a drainage system established under chapter 103E that does not substantially affect public waters; or</p>
          <p>(3) culvert restoration or replacement of the same size and elevation, if the restoration or replacement does not impact a designated trout stream.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permit application.</span></h2>
          <p>Application for a public-waters-work permit must be in writing to the commissioner on forms prescribed by the commissioner. The commissioner may issue a state general permit to a governmental subdivision or to the general public under which more than one project may be conducted under a single permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Boathouses and boat storage structures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following definitions apply to this subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) "boathouse" means a structure or watercraft that is moored by spuds, cables, ropes, anchors, or chains that may be intended for habitation and has walls, a roof, and either an open well for boats or a floor from wall to wall and does not include watercraft that are designed and operated as motorboats;</p>
          <p>(2) "motorboat" means a watercraft that is designed for and is capable of navigation on the water and that has an adequately sized external or internal mechanical propulsion system for the type of watercraft; and</p>
          <p>(3) "boat storage structure" means a structure that is used for storing boats or float planes.</p>
          <p>(b) Boathouses and boat storage structures are prohibited on public waters of Minnesota, except as allowed by paragraphs (c) to (f).</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may issue a public-waters-work permit for boathouses, when approved by the local governmental unit and:</p>
          <p>(1) only in areas of historic use for the structures, as determined by the commissioner, and where the boathouse was in existence on public waters prior to January 1, 1997; or</p>
          <p>(2) where the boathouse serves as a public service structure within a permitted commercial marina.</p>
          <p>(d) A boathouse in existence on public waters prior to January 1, 1997, may be repaired or replaced, provided that the repairs or replacement are consistent with the permit issued by the commissioner under paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner may issue a public-waters-work permit for the repair or replacement of boat storage structures when:</p>
          <p>(1) approved by the local governmental unit;</p>
          <p>(2) the boat storage structure was in existence prior to 1979 and is currently used for boat storage;</p>
          <p>(3) the boat storage structure is not habitable and is not connected to a sewage system;</p>
          <p>(4) the local government unit has had the opportunity to review the boat storage structure application and has not provided written comments opposing the application;</p>
          <p>(5) the total area of boat storage structures on the applicant's property and on public waters adjacent to the applicant's property is not increased;</p>
          <p>(6) the height of boat storage structures is not increased more than one foot, unless boat storage structures are consolidated and the same pitch roof results in an increased height; and</p>
          <p>(7) the public-waters-work permit with the specific dimensions and location of the boat storage structure is recorded in the real estate records of the office of the county recorder or registrar of titles in the county in which the applicant's property is located.</p>
          <p>(f) A boat storage structure may be repaired, replaced, or consolidated, provided that the repairs, replacement, or consolidation are consistent with the permit issued by the commissioner under paragraph (e). The repair or replacement of a boat storage structure may include:</p>
          <p>(1) the replacement of the foundation of the boat storage structure, provided that the material below the ordinary high-water mark is not toxic to aquatic life; and</p>
          <p>(2) the consolidation of multiple boat storage structures.</p>
          <p>(g) Notwithstanding sections 103F.201 to 103F.221, and rules adopted under those sections, the local zoning authority may approve a boat storage structure that is at or above the ordinary high-water level to replace a boat storage structure that is at or below the ordinary high-water level of a public water if the boat storage structure was in existence prior to 1979. The replacement boat storage structure may not exceed the total area of the boat storage structure being replaced. A boat storage structure that is replaced under this paragraph must be removed prior to building the replacement structure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Delegating permit authority to local units of government.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may delegate public-waters-work permit authority to the appropriate county or municipality or to watershed districts or watershed management organizations that have elected to assert local authority over protected waters. The public-waters-work permit authority must be delegated under guidelines of the commissioner and the delegation must be done by agreement with the involved county, municipality, watershed district, or water management organization and in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.315">103G.315</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For projects affecting public waters wetlands and for wetland areas of public waters affected by a public transportation project as determined by the commissioner, the commissioner may waive the requirement for a public-waters-work permit if the local government unit makes a replacement, no-loss, or exemption determination in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103A.201">103A.201</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.3355">103B.3355</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222">103G.222</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2372">103G.2372</a>, and rules adopted pursuant to these same sections.</p>
          <p>(c) For projects affecting both public waters and wetlands, the local government unit may, by written agreement with the commissioner, waive the requirement for a replacement plan, no-loss, or exemption determination if a public-waters-work permit is required and the commissioner includes the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103A.201">103A.201</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.3355">103B.3355</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222">103G.222</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2372">103G.2372</a>, and rules adopted pursuant to these same sections in the public-waters-work permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Conforming with water and related land resource management plans.</span></h2>
          <p>A public-waters-work permit may not be issued under this section if the project does not conform to state, regional, and local water and related land resources management plans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Effect on environment and mitigation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A public-waters-work permit may be issued only if the project will involve a minimum encroachment, change, or damage to the environment, particularly the ecology of the waterway.</p>
          <p>(b) If a major change in the resource is justified, public-waters-work permits must include provisions to compensate for the detrimental aspects of the change.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Excavation in public waters.</span></h2>
          <p>Public-waters-work permits for projects that involve excavation in the beds of public waters may be granted only if:</p>
          <p>(1) the area where the excavation will take place is covered by a shoreland zoning ordinance approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) the work under the permit is consistent with the shoreland zoning ordinance; and</p>
          <p>(3) the permit includes provisions for the deposition of excavated materials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Project affecting floodwaters.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A public-waters-work permit for a project affecting floodwaters may be granted only if:</p>
          <p>(1) the area covered by the public-waters-work permit is governed by a floodplain management ordinance approved by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) the conduct authorized by the public-waters-work permit is consistent with the floodplain management ordinance, if the commissioner has determined that enough information is available for the adoption of a floodplain ordinance.</p>
          <p>(b) A public-waters-work permit involving the control of floodwaters by structural means, such as dams, dikes, levees, and channel improvements, may be granted only after the commissioner has considered all other flood damage reduction alternatives. In developing a policy on placing emergency levees along the banks of public waters under emergency flood conditions, the commissioner shall consult and cooperate with the office of emergency services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Changing level of public waters.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A public-waters-work permit that will change the level of public waters may not be issued unless:</p>
          <p>(1) the shoreland adjacent to the waters to be changed is governed by a shoreland zoning ordinance approved by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) the change in water level is consistent with the shoreland zoning ordinance.</p>
          <p>(b) Standards and procedures for use in deciding the level of public waters must ensure that the rights of all persons are protected when public water levels are changed and must provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) technical advice to persons involved;</p>
          <p>(2) establishing alternatives to help local agencies resolve water level conflicts; and</p>
          <p>(3) mechanics necessary for local resolution of water problems within the state guidelines.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Emergency repairs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The owner of a dam, reservoir, control structure, or waterway obstruction may make repairs that are immediately necessary in case of emergency without a public-waters-work permit under subdivision 1. The owner must immediately notify the commissioner of the emergency and of the emergency repairs being made. The owner must apply for a public-waters-work permit for the emergency repairs and necessary permanent repairs as soon as practicable.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision does not apply to routine maintenance not affecting the safety of the structures.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner declares there is an emergency and repairs or remedial action are immediately necessary to safeguard life and property, the repairs, remedial action, or both, must be started immediately by the owner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.245.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Operating structure predating permit requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>The owner of a dam, reservoir, control structure, or waterway obstruction constructed before a public-waters-work permit was required by law must maintain and operate the dam, reservoir, control structure, or waterway obstruction in a manner approved and prescribed by rule by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.245.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.22.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.8.0">1995 c 218 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/443/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 443 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/247/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 247 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/382/#laws.0.17.0">2000 c 382 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/146/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 146 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/2/#laws.1.14.0">2003 c 2 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/138/#laws.0.3.0">2005 c 138 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/180/#laws.0.1.0">2006 c 180 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.46.0">2012 c 272 s 46</a>,47; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.54.0">2014 c 289 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.91.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 91</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.251">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.251 INVESTIGATING ACTIVITIES AFFECTING WATERS OF THE STATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.251.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Investigations.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that an investigation is in the public interest, the commissioner may investigate and monitor activities being conducted with or without a permit that may affect waters of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.251.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Findings and order.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) With or without a public hearing, the commissioner may make findings and issue orders related to activities being conducted without a permit that affect waters of the state as otherwise authorized under this chapter.</p>
          <p>(b) A copy of the findings and order must be served on the person to whom the order is issued.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner issues the findings and order without a hearing, the person to whom the order is issued may file a demand for a hearing with the commissioner. The demand for a hearing must be accompanied by the bond as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.311#stat.103G.311.6">103G.311, subdivision 6</a>, and the hearing must be held in the same manner and with the same requirements as a hearing held under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.311#stat.103G.311.5">103G.311, subdivision 5</a>. The demand for a hearing and bond must be filed by 30 days after the person is served with a copy of the commissioner's order.</p>
          <p>(d) The hearing must be conducted as a contested case hearing under chapter 14.</p>
          <p>(e) If the person to whom the order is addressed does not demand a hearing or demands a hearing but fails to file the required bond:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner's order becomes final at the end of 30 days after the person is served with the order; and</p>
          <p>(2) the person may not appeal the order.</p>
          <p>(f) An order of the commissioner may be recorded or filed by the commissioner in the office of the county recorder or registrar of titles, as appropriate, in the county where the real property is located as a deed restriction on the property that runs with the land and is binding on the owners, successors, and assigns until the conditions of the order are met or the order is rescinded.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.251.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.23.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 186 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/138/#laws.0.4.0">2005 c 138 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.19.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WATER DIVERSION AND APPROPRIATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.255">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.255 ALLOCATING AND CONTROLLING WATERS OF THE STATE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall administer:</p>
        <p>(1) the use, allocation, and control of waters of the state;</p>
        <p>(2) the establishment, maintenance, and control of lake levels and water storage reservoirs; and</p>
        <p>(3) the determination of the ordinary high-water level of waters of the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.255.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.24.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 186 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.261">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.261 WATER ALLOCATION PRIORITIES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall adopt rules for allocation of waters based on the following priorities for the consumptive appropriation and use of water:</p>
        <p>(1) first priority, domestic water supply, excluding industrial and commercial uses of municipal water supply, and use for power production that meets the contingency planning provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.285#stat.103G.285.6">103G.285, subdivision 6</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) second priority, a use of water that involves consumption of less than 10,000 gallons of water per day;</p>
        <p>(3) third priority, agricultural irrigation, and processing of agricultural products involving consumption in excess of 10,000 gallons per day;</p>
        <p>(4) fourth priority, power production in excess of the use provided for in the contingency plan developed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.285#stat.103G.285.6">103G.285, subdivision 6</a>;</p>
        <p>(5) fifth priority, uses, other than agricultural irrigation, processing of agricultural products, and power production, involving consumption in excess of 10,000 gallons per day; and</p>
        <p>(6) sixth priority, nonessential uses.</p>
        <p>(b) For the purposes of this section, "consumption" means water withdrawn from a supply that is lost for immediate further use in the area.</p>
        <p>(c) Appropriation and use of surface water from streams during periods of flood flows and high water levels must be encouraged subject to consideration of the purposes for use, quantities to be used, and the number of persons appropriating water.</p>
        <p>(d) Appropriation and use of surface water from lakes of less than 500 acres in surface area must be discouraged.</p>
        <p>(e) The treatment and reuse of water for nonconsumptive uses shall be encouraged.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.261.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.4.1.0">1989 c 326 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.25.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/426/#laws.1.13.0">1990 c 426 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 186 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.48.0">2012 c 272 s 48</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.265">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.265 WATER SUPPLY; MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.265.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Assurance of supply.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop and manage water resources to assure an adequate supply to meet long-range seasonal requirements for domestic, municipal, industrial, agricultural, fish and wildlife, recreational, power, navigation, and quality control purposes from waters of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.265.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Diversion greater than 2,000,000 gallons per day.</span></h2>
          <p>A water-use permit or a plan that requires a permit or the commissioner's approval, involving a diversion of waters of the state of more than 2,000,000 gallons per day average in a 30-day period, to a place outside of this state or from the basin of origin within this state may not be granted or approved until a determination is made by the commissioner that the water remaining in the basin of origin will be adequate to meet the basin's water resources needs during the specified life of the diversion project and, for groundwater, the diversion meets the applicable standards under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.287#stat.103G.287.5">103G.287, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.265.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.108.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 108</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.265.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Consumptive use of more than 2,000,000 gallons per day.</span></h2>
          <p> A water-use permit or a plan that requires a permit or the commissioner's approval, involving a consumptive use of more than 2,000,000 gallons per day average in a 30-day period, may not be granted or approved until a determination is made by the commissioner that the water remaining in the basin of origin will be adequate to meet the basin's water resources needs during the specified life of the consumptive use and, for groundwater, the consumptive use meets the applicable standards under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.287#stat.103G.287.5">103G.287, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.265.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Diversion or consumptive use from Great Lakes greater than 5,000,000 gallons per day.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A water-use permit or a plan that requires a permit or the commissioner's approval, involving a diversion or consumptive use of waters of the state from the Great Lakes water basin within this state where the diversion or consumptive use of waters would be more than 5,000,000 gallons per day average in a 30-day period, may not be granted or approved until:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner has notified and solicited comments on the proposed diversion or consumptive use from the offices of the governors of the Great Lakes states and premiers of the Great Lakes provinces, the appropriate water management agencies of the Great Lakes states and provinces, and the international joint commission;</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner has considered the comments and concerns of the offices, agencies, and commission to which notice was given under clause (1); and</p>
          <p>(3) the diversion or consumptive use has been approved by the legislature.</p>
          <p>(b) If an objection is made to the proposed diversion or consumptive use by an office, agency, or commission to which notice was given under paragraph (a), clause (1), the commissioner must convene a meeting with the affected office, agency, or commission to investigate and consider the issues involved, and to seek a mutually agreeable solution to be recommended to the commissioner. In making a final decision on the approval of a permit or plan subject to review under this subdivision, the commissioner shall consider the record of the meeting and the recommendation. The commissioner must send notification of the final decision to each office, agency, or commission to which notice was given under paragraph (a), clause (1).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.265.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.26.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/406/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 406 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 186 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.49.0">2012 c 272 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.67.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 67</a>,68</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.27">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.27 WATER MANAGEMENT ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.27.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established; sources.</span></h2>
          <p>The water management account is created in the natural resources fund in the state treasury. Revenues collected from permit application fees, water use fees, field inspection fees, penalties, and other receipts according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271">103G.271</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301">103G.301</a> shall be deposited in the account. Interest earned on money in the account accrues to the account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.27.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purposes of account.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the water management account may be spent only for the costs associated with administering this chapter.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.13.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.271">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.271 APPROPRIATION AND USE OF WATERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permit required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), the state, a person, partnership, or association, private or public corporation, county, municipality, or other political subdivision of the state may not appropriate or use waters of the state without a water-use permit from the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) This section does not apply to the following water uses:</p>
          <p>(1) use for a water supply by less than 25 persons for domestic purposes, except as required by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.287">103G.287</a>, subdivision 4, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(2) nonconsumptive diversion of a surface water of the state from its natural channel for the production of hydroelectric or hydromechanical power at structures that were in existence on and before July 1, 1937, including repowering, upgrades, or additions to those facilities; or</p>
          <p>(3) appropriation or use of stormwater collected and used to reduce stormwater runoff volume, treat stormwater, or sustain groundwater supplies when water is extracted from constructed management facilities for stormwater.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may issue a state general permit for appropriation of water to a governmental subdivision or to the general public. The general permit may authorize more than one project and the appropriation or use of more than one source of water. Water-use permit processing fees and reports required under subdivision 6 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.281#stat.103G.281.3">103G.281, subdivision 3</a>, are required for each project or water source that is included under a general permit, except that no fee is required for uses totaling less than 15,000,000 gallons annually.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consistent with state and local plans.</span></h2>
          <p>A water-use permit may not be issued under this section unless it is consistent with state, regional, and local water and related land resources management plans if the regional and local plans are consistent with statewide plans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permit restriction during summer months.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must not modify or restrict the amount of appropriation from a groundwater source authorized in a water-use permit issued to irrigate agricultural land between April 1 and October 1, or, for agricultural land with a crop, until November 15, unless the commissioner determines the authorized amount of appropriation endangers a domestic water supply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Minimum-use exemption and local approval of low-use permits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except for local permits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.211#stat.103B.211.4">103B.211, subdivision 4</a>, a water-use permit is not required for the appropriation and use of less than 10,000 gallons per day and totaling no more than 1,000,000 gallons per year, except as required by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.287#stat.103G.287.4">103G.287, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) Water-use permits for more than the minimum amount but less than an intermediate amount prescribed by rule must be processed and approved at the municipal, county, or regional level based on rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The rules must include provisions for reporting to the commissioner the amounts of water appropriated under local permits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Mt. Simon-Hinckley aquifer.</span></h2>
          <p> The commissioner may not issue new water-use permits that will appropriate water from the Mt. Simon-Hinckley aquifer unless the appropriation is for potable water use, there are no feasible or practical alternatives to this source, and a water conservation plan is incorporated with the permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.4b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Bulk transport or sale.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To maintain the supply of drinking water for future generations and except as provided under paragraph (b), the commissioner may not issue a new water-use permit to appropriate water in excess of one million gallons per year for bulk transport or sale of water for consumptive use to a location more than 50 miles from the point of the proposed appropriation.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to a water-use permit for a public water supply, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.382#stat.144.382.4">144.382, subdivision 4</a>, issued to a local unit of government, rural water district established under chapter 116A, or Tribal unit of government if:</p>
          <p>(1) the use is solely for the public water supply;</p>
          <p>(2) the local unit of government, rural water district established under chapter 116A, or Tribal unit of government has a property interest at the point of the appropriation;</p>
          <p>(3) the communities that will use the water are located within 100 miles of the point of appropriation; and</p>
          <p>(4) the requirements in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.265">103G.265</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.285">103G.285</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.287">103G.287</a> are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Once-through water-use permits prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (c), the commissioner may not issue a water-use permit to increase the volume of appropriation from a groundwater source for a once-through cooling system.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided in paragraph (c), once-through system water-use permits using in excess of 5,000,000 gallons annually must be terminated by the commissioner, unless the discharge is into a public water basin within a nature preserve approved by the commissioner and established prior to January 1, 2001. The commissioner may issue a permit for a system in existence prior to January 1, 2015, for up to 5,000,000 gallons annually. Existing once-through systems must not be expanded and are required to convert to water efficient alternatives within the design life of existing equipment.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) and (b), the commissioner, with the approval of the commissioners of health and the Pollution Control Agency, may issue once-through system water-use permits on an annual basis for groundwater thermal exchange devices or aquifer storage and recovery systems that return all once-through system water to the source aquifer. Water-use permit processing fees in subdivision 6, paragraph (a), apply to all water withdrawals under this paragraph, including any reuse of water returned to the source aquifer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Maintaining surface water levels.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 5, paragraph (b), the commissioner shall, by January 31, 1994, revoke all existing permits, and may not issue new permits, for the appropriation or use of groundwater in excess of 10,000,000 gallons per year for the primary purpose of maintaining or increasing surface water levels in the seven-county metropolitan area and in other areas of concern as determined by the commissioner. This subdivision does not apply until January 1, 1998, to a municipality that, by January 1, 1994, submits a plan acceptable to the commissioner for maintaining or increasing surface water levels using sources other than groundwater.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Water-use permit; processing fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as described in paragraphs (b) to (g), a water-use permit processing fee must be prescribed by the commissioner in accordance with the schedule of fees in this subdivision for each water-use permit in force at any time during the year. Fees collected under this paragraph are credited to the water management account in the natural resources fund. The schedule is as follows, with the stated fee in each clause applied to the total amount appropriated:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) $140 for amounts not exceeding 50,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) $3.50 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 50,000,000 gallons but less than 100,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) $4 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 100,000,000 gallons but less than 150,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) $4.50 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 150,000,000 gallons but less than 200,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) $5 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 200,000,000 gallons but less than 250,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) $5.50 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 250,000,000 gallons but less than 300,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) $6 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 300,000,000 gallons but less than 350,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) $6.50 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 350,000,000 gallons but less than 400,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) $7 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 400,000,000 gallons but less than 450,000,000 gallons per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) $7.50 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 450,000,000 gallons but less than 500,000,000 gallons per year; and</p>
          <p class="in">(11) $8 per 1,000,000 gallons for amounts greater than 500,000,000 gallons per year.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For once-through cooling systems, a water-use processing fee must be prescribed by the commissioner in accordance with the following schedule of fees for each water-use permit in force at any time during the year:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) for nonprofit corporations and school districts, $200 per 1,000,000 gallons; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) for all other users, $420 per 1,000,000 gallons.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The fee is payable based on the amount of water appropriated during the year and, except as provided in paragraph (f), the minimum fee is $100.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) For water-use processing fees other than once-through cooling systems:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the fee for a city of the first class may not exceed $250,000 per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the fee for other entities for any permitted use may not exceed:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) $60,000 per year for an entity holding three or fewer permits;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) $90,000 per year for an entity holding four or five permits; or</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) $300,000 per year for an entity holding more than five permits;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the fee for agricultural irrigation may not exceed $750 per year;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the fee for a municipality that furnishes electric service and cogenerates steam for home heating may not exceed $10,000 for its permit for water use related to the cogeneration of electricity and steam;</p>
          <p>(5) the fee for a facility that temporarily diverts a water of the state from its natural channel to produce hydroelectric or hydromechanical power may not exceed $5,000 per year. A permit for such a facility does not count toward the number of permits held by an entity as described in this paragraph; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) no fee is required for a project involving the appropriation of surface water to prevent flood damage or to remove floodwaters during a period of flooding, as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Failure to pay the fee is sufficient cause for revoking a permit. A penalty of ten percent per month calculated from the original due date must be imposed on the unpaid balance of fees remaining 30 days after the sending of a second notice of fees due. A fee may not be imposed on an agency, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.01#stat.16B.01.2">16B.01, subdivision 2</a>, or federal governmental agency holding a water appropriation permit.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The minimum water-use processing fee for a permit issued for irrigation of agricultural land is $20 for years in which:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) there is no appropriation of water under the permit; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the permit is suspended for more than seven consecutive days between May 1 and October 1.</p>
          <p>(g) The commissioner shall waive the water-use permit fee for installations and projects that use stormwater runoff or where public entities are diverting water to treat a water quality issue and returning the water to its source without using the water for any other purpose, unless the commissioner determines that the proposed use adversely affects surface water or groundwater.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) A surcharge of $50 per million gallons in addition to the fee prescribed in paragraph (a) shall be applied to the volume of water used in each of the months of May, June, July, August, and September that exceeds the volume of water used in January for municipal water use, irrigation of golf courses, and landscape irrigation. The surcharge for municipalities with more than one permit shall be determined based on the total appropriations from all permits that supply a common distribution system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Fees for past unpermitted appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>An entity that appropriates water without a required permit under subdivision 1 must pay the applicable water-use permit processing fee specified in subdivision 6 for the period during which the unpermitted appropriation occurred. The fees for unpermitted appropriations are required for the previous seven calendar years after being notified of the need for a permit. This fee is in addition to any other fee or penalty assessed. The commissioner may waive payment of fees for past unpermitted appropriations for a residential system permitted under subdivision 5, paragraph (b), or for a hydroelectric or hydromechanical facility that temporarily diverts a water of the state from its natural channel.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.271.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Transferring permit.</span></h2>
          <p>A water-use permit may be transferred to a successive owner of real property if the permittee conveys the real property where the source of water is located. The new owner must notify the commissioner immediately after the conveyance and request transfer of the permit. The commissioner must not deny the transfer of a permit if the permittee is in compliance with all permit conditions and the permit meets the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.255">103G.255</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301">103G.301</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.271.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.27.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.49.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/597/#laws.0.63.0">1990 c 597 s 63</a>-65; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/214/#laws.0.6.0">1991 c 214 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/234/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 234 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.10.5.0">1991 c 354 art 10 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/366/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 366 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/601/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 601 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.121.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 121</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.21.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.5.19.0">2008 c 363 art 5 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.34.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.52.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.14.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.50.0">2012 c 272 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.69.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 69</a>,70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.20.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.92.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 92</a>-94; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.116.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 116</a>-119; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.81.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 81</a>,82;       </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.275">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.275 INSTALLATION FOR WATER USE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.275.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permit required.</span></h2>
          <p>The owner of an installation for appropriating or using waters of the state may not increase the pumping capacity or make any major change in the installation without first applying in writing for, and obtaining, the written permit of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.275.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Water use; data statement.</span></h2>
          <p>The owner or person in charge of an installation for appropriating or using waters of the state, whether or not under use permit, must file a water use data statement with the commissioner. The statement must be filed at the time the commissioner determines necessary for the statewide water information system. The water use data statement must be on forms provided by the commissioner and identify the installation's location, its capacity, the purposes for which it is used, and additional information required by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.275.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's examinations.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may examine an installation that appropriates or uses surface water or groundwater. The owner of the installation must provide information required by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.28.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.11.0">1995 c 218 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.281">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.281 WATER USE PROHIBITED WITHOUT MEASURING QUANTITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.281.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Measuring and records required.</span></h2>
          <p>The state, a political subdivision of the state, a person, partnership, public or private corporation, or association may not appropriate or use waters of the state without measuring and keeping a record of the quantity of water used or appropriated as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271">103G.271</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.275">103G.275</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.281.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Measuring equipment required.</span></h2>
          <p>An installation for appropriating or using water must be equipped with a flow meter to measure the quantity of water appropriated within the degree of accuracy required by rule. The commissioner can determine other methods to be used for measuring water quantity based on the quantity of water appropriated or used, the source of water, the method of appropriating or using water, and any other facts supplied to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.281.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Records of the amount of water appropriated or used must be kept for each installation. The readings and the total amount of water appropriated must be reported annually to the commissioner on or before February 15 of the following year on forms provided by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The records must be submitted with the annual water-use permit processing fee in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271">103G.271</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.281.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty for noncompliance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may assess penalties for noncompliant reporting of water use information as provided in this section. The penalty is ten percent of the annual water-use permit processing fee.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.281.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.29.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/597/#laws.0.66.0">1990 c 597 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.22.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.282">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.282 MONITORING TO EVALUATE IMPACTS FROM APPROPRIATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.282.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Monitoring equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may require the installation and maintenance of monitoring equipment to evaluate water resource impacts from permitted appropriations and proposed projects that require a permit. Monitoring for water resources that supply more than one appropriator must be designed to minimize costs to individual appropriators. The cost of drilling additional monitoring wells must be shared proportionally by all permit holders that are directly affecting a particular water resources feature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.282.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Measuring devices required.</span></h2>
          <p>Monitoring installations required under subdivision 1 must be equipped with automated measuring devices to measure water levels, flows, or conditions. The commissioner may determine the frequency of measurements and other measuring methods based on the quantity of water appropriated or used, the source of water, potential connections to other water resources, the method of appropriating or using water, seasonal and long-term changes in water levels, and any other facts supplied to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.282.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reports and costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Records of water measurements under subdivision 2 must be kept for each installation. The measurements must be reported annually to the commissioner on or before February 15 of the following year in a format or on forms prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The owner or person in charge of an installation for appropriating or using waters of the state or a proposal that requires a permit is responsible for all costs related to establishing and maintaining monitoring installations and to measuring and reporting data. Monitoring costs for water resources that supply more than one appropriator may be distributed among all users within a monitoring area determined by the commissioner and assessed based on volumes of water appropriated and proximity to resources of concern.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.282.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.53.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.51.0">2012 c 272 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.285">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.285 SURFACE WATER APPROPRIATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.285.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Waiver.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may waive a limitation or requirement in subdivisions 2 to 6 for just cause.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.285.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Natural and altered natural watercourses.</span></h2>
          <p>If data are available, permits to appropriate water from natural and altered natural watercourses must be limited so that consumptive appropriations are not made from the watercourses during periods of specified low flows. The purpose of the limit is to safeguard water availability for in-stream uses and for downstream higher priority users located reasonably near the site of appropriation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.285.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Water basins.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Permits to appropriate water from water basins must be limited so that the collective annual withdrawals do not exceed a total volume of water amounting to one-half acre-foot per acre of water basin based on Minnesota Department of Conservation Bulletin No. 25, "An Inventory of Minnesota Lakes," published in 1968.</p>
          <p>(b) As a condition to a surface water appropriation permit, the commissioner shall set a protective elevation for the water basin, below which an appropriation is not allowed. During the determination of the protective elevation, the commissioner shall consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the elevation of important aquatic vegetation characteristics related to fish and wildlife habitat;</p>
          <p>(2) existing uses of the water basin by the public and riparian landowners; and</p>
          <p>(3) the total volume within the water basin and the slope of the littoral zone.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.285.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Water basins less than 500 acres.</span></h2>
          <p>As part of an application for appropriation of water from a water basin less than 500 acres in surface area, the applicant shall obtain a statement of support with as many signatures as the applicant can obtain from property owners with property riparian to the water basin. The statement of support must:</p>
          <p>(1) state support for the proposed appropriation; and</p>
          <p>(2) show the number of property owners whose signatures the applicant could not obtain.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.285.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Trout streams.</span></h2>
          <p>Permits issued after June 3, 1977, to appropriate water from streams designated trout streams by the commissioner's orders under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.005">97C.005 </a>must be limited to temporary appropriations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.285.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Contingency planning.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for use of surface waters of the state is not complete until the applicant submits, as part of the application, a contingency plan that describes the alternatives the applicant will use if further appropriation is restricted due to the flow of the stream or the level of a water basin. A surface water appropriation may not be allowed unless the contingency plan is feasible or the permittee agrees to withstand the results of not being able to appropriate water.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.285.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.30.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.54.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.287">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.287 GROUNDWATER APPROPRIATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.287.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applications for groundwater appropriations; preliminary well-construction approval.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Groundwater use permit applications are not complete until the applicant has supplied:</p>
          <p>(1) a water well record as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103I.205#stat.103I.205.9">103I.205, subdivision 9</a>, information on the subsurface geologic formations penetrated by the well and the formation or aquifer that will serve as the water source, and geologic information from test holes drilled to locate the site of the production well;</p>
          <p>(2) the maximum daily, seasonal, and annual pumpage rates and volumes being requested;</p>
          <p>(3) information on groundwater quality in terms of the measures of quality commonly specified for the proposed water use and details on water treatment necessary for the proposed use;</p>
          <p>(4) the results of an aquifer test completed according to specifications approved by the commissioner. The test must be conducted at the maximum pumping rate requested in the application and for a length of time adequate to assess or predict impacts to other wells and surface water and groundwater resources. The permit applicant is responsible for all costs related to the aquifer test, including the construction of groundwater and surface water monitoring installations, and water level readings before, during, and after the aquifer test; and</p>
          <p>(5) the results of any assessments conducted by the commissioner under paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may waive an application requirement in this subdivision if the information provided with the application is adequate to determine whether the proposed appropriation and use of water is sustainable and will protect ecosystems, water quality, and the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall provide an assessment of a proposed well needing a groundwater appropriation permit. The commissioner shall evaluate the information submitted as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103I.205#stat.103I.205.1">103I.205, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (e), and determine whether the anticipated appropriation request is likely to meet the applicable requirements of this chapter. If the appropriation request is likely to meet applicable requirements, the commissioner shall provide the person submitting the information with a letter or electronically transmitted notice providing preliminary approval to construct the well and the requirements, including test-well information, that will be needed to obtain the permit.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner must provide an applicant denied a groundwater use permit or issued a groundwater use permit that is reduced or restricted from the original request with all information the commissioner used in making the determination, including hydrographs, flow tests, aquifer tests, topographic maps, field reports, photographs, and proof of equipment calibration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.287.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Relationship to surface water resources.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Groundwater appropriations may be authorized only if they avoid known negative impacts to surface waters. If the commissioner determines that groundwater appropriations are having a negative impact to surface waters, the commissioner may use a sustainable diversion limit or other relevant method, tools, or information to implement measures so that groundwater appropriations do not negatively impact the surface waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.287.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Protecting groundwater supplies.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish water appropriation limits to protect groundwater resources. When establishing water appropriation limits to protect groundwater resources, the commissioner must consider the sustainability of the groundwater resource, including the current and projected water levels, cumulative withdrawal rates from the resource on a monthly or annual basis, water quality, whether the use protects ecosystems, and the ability of future generations to meet their own needs. The commissioner may consult with the commissioners of health, agriculture, and the Pollution Control Agency and other state entities when determining the impacts on water quality and quantity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.287.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Groundwater management areas.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may designate groundwater management areas and limit total annual water appropriations and uses within a designated area to ensure sustainable use of groundwater that protects ecosystems, water quality, and the ability of future generations to meet their own needs. Water appropriations and uses within a designated management area must be consistent with a groundwater management area plan approved by the commissioner that addresses water conservation requirements and water allocation priorities established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.261">103G.261</a>. At least 30 days prior to implementing or modifying a groundwater management area plan under this subdivision, the commissioner shall consult with the advisory team established in paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271#stat.103G.271.1">103G.271, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), and Minnesota Rules, within designated groundwater management areas, the commissioner may require general permits as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271#stat.103G.271.1">103G.271, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), for water users using less than 10,000 gallons per day or 1,000,000 gallons per year and water suppliers serving less than 25 persons for domestic purposes. The commissioner may waive the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.281">103G.281</a> for general permits issued under this paragraph, and the fee specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.2">103G.301, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c), does not apply to general permits issued under this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(c) When designating a groundwater management area, the commissioner shall assemble an advisory team to assist in developing a groundwater management area plan for the area. The advisory team members shall be selected from public and private entities that have an interest in the water resources affected by the groundwater management area. A majority of the advisory team members shall be public and private entities that currently hold water-use permits for water appropriations from the affected water resources. The commissioner shall consult with the League of Minnesota Cities, the Association of Minnesota Counties, the Minnesota Association of Watershed Districts, and the Minnesota Association of Townships in appointing the local government representatives to the advisory team. The advisory team may also include representatives from the University of Minnesota, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, other institutions of higher learning in Minnesota, political subdivisions with jurisdiction over water issues, nonprofits with expertise in water, and federal agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.287.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Sustainability standard.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue water-use permits for appropriation from groundwater only if the commissioner determines that the groundwater use is sustainable to supply the needs of future generations and the proposed use will not harm ecosystems, degrade water, or reduce water levels beyond the reach of public water supply and private domestic wells constructed according to Minnesota Rules, chapter 4725.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.287.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.55.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.71.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 71</a>-73; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.55.0">2014 c 289 s 55</a>,56; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.95.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.120.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 120</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.88.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 88</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.85.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 85</a>,86</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.289">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.289 WELL INTERFERENCE; WELL SEALING.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall not validate a well interference claim if the affected well has been sealed prior to the completion of the commissioner's investigation of the complaint. If the well is sealed prior to completion of the investigation, the commissioner must dismiss the complaint.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.289.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.96.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 96</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.291">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.291 PUBLIC WATER SUPPLY PLANS; APPROPRIATION DURING DEFICIENCY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.291.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Declaration and conservation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the governor determines and declares by executive order that there is a critical water deficiency, public water supply authorities appropriating water must adopt and enforce water conservation restrictions within their jurisdiction that are consistent with rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The restrictions must limit lawn sprinkling, vehicle washing, golf course and park irrigation, and other nonessential uses, and have appropriate penalties for failure to comply with the restrictions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.291.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Modifying permit for noncompliance.</span></h2>
          <p>Disregard of critical water deficiency orders, even though total appropriation remains less than that permitted, is adequate grounds for immediate modification of a public water supply authority's water-use permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.291.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Water supply plans; demand reduction.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Every public water supplier serving more than 1,000 people must submit a water supply plan to the commissioner for approval by January 1, 1996. In accordance with guidelines developed by the commissioner, the plan must address projected demands, adequacy of the water supply system and planned improvements, existing and future water sources, natural resource impacts or limitations, emergency preparedness, water conservation, supply and demand reduction measures, and allocation priorities that are consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.261">103G.261</a>. Public water suppliers must update their plan and, upon notification, submit it to the commissioner for approval every ten years.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The water supply plan in paragraph (a) is required for all communities in the metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121">473.121</a>, with a municipal water supply system and is a required element of the local comprehensive plan required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.859">473.859</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Public water suppliers serving more than 1,000 people must encourage water conservation by employing water use demand reduction measures, as defined in subdivision 4, paragraph (a), before requesting approval from the commissioner of health under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.383">144.383, paragraph (a)</a>, to construct a public water supply well or requesting an increase in the authorized volume of appropriation. The commissioner of natural resources and the water supplier shall use a collaborative process to achieve demand reduction measures as a part of a water supply plan review process.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Public water suppliers serving more than 1,000 people must submit records that indicate the number of connections and amount of use by customer category and volume of water unaccounted for with the annual report of water use required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.281#stat.103G.281.3">103G.281, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) For the purposes of this section, "public water supplier" means an entity that owns, manages, or operates a public water supply, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.382#stat.144.382.4">144.382, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.291.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Demand reduction measures.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purposes of this section, "demand reduction measures" means measures that reduce water demand, water losses, peak water demands, and nonessential water uses. Demand reduction measures must include a conservation rate structure, or a uniform rate structure with a conservation program that achieves demand reduction. A "conservation rate structure" means a rate structure that encourages conservation and may include increasing block rates, seasonal rates, time of use rates, individualized goal rates, or excess use rates. If a conservation rate is applied to multifamily dwellings or a manufactured home park, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/327C.015">327C.015</a>, subdivision 8, the rate structure must consider each residential unit as an individual user.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) To encourage conservation, a public water supplier serving more than 1,000 people must implement demand reduction measures by January 1, 2015.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.291.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.31.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 186 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.57.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.5.20.0">2008 c 363 art 5 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/150/#laws.1.2.0">2012 c 150 art 1 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.97.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/57/#laws.5.1.0">2023 c 57 art 5 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.293">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.293 STATEWIDE DROUGHT PLAN.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall establish a plan to respond to drought-related emergencies and to prepare a statewide framework for drought response. The plan must consider metropolitan water supply plans of the Metropolitan Council prepared under section 473.1565. The plan must provide a framework for implementing drought response actions in a staged approach related to decreasing levels of flows. Permits issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271">103G.271</a> must provide conditions on water appropriation consistent with the drought response plan established by this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.293.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.3.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/434/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 434 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/13/#laws.1.23.0">1993 c 13 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.3.7.0">2006 c 212 art 3 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.103G.295"><b>103G.295</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.83.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.297">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.297 DIVERTING OR DRAINING WATER FOR MINING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority to issue permits.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue water-use permits for the diversion, drainage, control, or use of waters of the state for mining iron ore, taconite, copper, copper-nickel, or nickel as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An owner of the iron ore, taconite, copper, copper-nickel, or nickel deposits or the owner of the right to mine the deposits must apply to the commissioner for a permit in the form prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as otherwise provided in this section, the application and the proceedings related to the application and to a permit issued are governed by the applicable provisions of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant.</span></h2>
          <p>The permit may be granted only if the commissioner determines that:</p>
          <p>(1) the proposed drainage, diversion, control, or use of waters will be necessary for the mining of substantial deposits of iron ore, taconite, copper, copper-nickel, or nickel, and that another feasible and economical method of mining is not reasonably available;</p>
          <p>(2) the proposed drainage, diversion, control, or use of waters will not substantially impair the interests of the public in lands or waters or the substantial beneficial public use of lands or waters except as expressly authorized in the permit and will not endanger public health or safety; and</p>
          <p>(3) the proposed mining operations will be in the public interest and the resulting public benefits warrant the proposed drainage, diversion, or control of waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Operation.</span></h2>
          <p>If the operations authorized by a permit may affect public or private property not owned by the permittee, before proceeding with the operations the permittee must:</p>
          <p>(1) acquire all rights or easements necessary for the operation;</p>
          <p>(2) pay or give security for the payment of damages to the property that may result from the operations; and</p>
          <p>(3) give evidence of compliance with this subdivision as the commissioner may require.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Liability of state.</span></h2>
          <p>The state and its officers, agents, or employees do not incur liability on account of the issuance of a permit or on account of any act or omission of the permittee, or the permittee's agents or employees, under or in connection with the permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Permit period.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding other limitations prescribed by law, a permit must be granted for a term the commissioner finds reasonable and necessary for the completion of the proposed mining operations, and the commissioner may prescribe a time in the permit for the commencement or completion of operations or construction under the permit or the exercise of the rights granted by the permit.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may extend the original term of the permit or the time allowed for the performance of its conditions for good cause shown upon application of the permittee.</p>
        </div>
