        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Permit conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>In a permit, the commissioner may prescribe conditions the commissioner finds necessary and practicable for restoring the waters to their former condition after completion of the mining operations or after expiration or cancellation of the permit. The commissioner may also prescribe other conditions necessary to protect the public health, safety, and welfare, and may require the permittee to furnish a bond to the state in an appropriate form and amount as security for compliance with the conditions of the permit and applicable law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Modifying or canceling permit.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A permit issued under this section is irrevocable for the term of the permit and for any extension of the term except:</p>
          <p>(1) the permit may be modified or canceled by the commissioner at the request or with the consent of the permittee upon conditions the commissioner finds necessary to protect the public interest;</p>
          <p>(2) subject to appeal as provided for water-use permits, the commissioner may modify or cancel a permit as provided in paragraph (b) if:</p>
          <p>(i) the permittee or its employees or agents breach the permit's terms or conditions or violate pertinent law; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the commissioner finds the modification or cancellation necessary to protect the public health or safety, or to protect the public interests in lands or waters against substantial injury resulting in any manner or to any extent not expressly authorized by the permit, or to prevent substantial injury to persons or property resulting in any manner or to any extent not so authorized; or</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner immediately suspends operations under a permit by written order to the permittee if necessary in an emergency, to protect the public health or safety or to protect public interests in lands or waters against imminent danger of substantial injury in any manner or to any extent not expressly authorized by the permit, or to protect persons or property against the danger, and may require the permittee to take any measures necessary to prevent or remedy the injury.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may modify or cancel the permit upon at least 30 days' written notice to the permittee, stating the grounds of the proposed modification or cancellation and giving the permittee an opportunity to be heard.</p>
          <p>(c) An order under paragraph (a), clause (3), may not remain in effect for more than 30 days from the date of the order without giving the permittee at least ten days' written notice of the order and an opportunity to be heard relating to the order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.297.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Effect on other law.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not amend, supersede, or repeal any existing law, but is supplementary to it.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.297.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.33.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 33</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.298">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.298 LANDSCAPE IRRIGATION SYSTEMS.</h1>
        <p>All automatically operated landscape irrigation systems shall have furnished and installed technology that inhibits or interrupts operation of the landscape irrigation system during periods of sufficient moisture. The technology must be adjustable either by the end user or the professional practitioner of landscape irrigation services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.298.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/44/#laws.0.1.0">2003 c 44 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.299">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.299 ADMINISTRATIVE PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority to issue administrative penalty orders.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As provided in paragraph (b), the commissioner may issue an order requiring violations to be corrected and administratively assessing monetary penalties for violations of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.271">103G.271</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.275">103G.275</a>, and any rules adopted under those sections.</p>
          <p>(b) An order under this section may be issued to a person for water appropriation activities without a required permit or for violating the terms of a required permit.</p>
          <p>(c) The order must be issued as provided in this section and in accordance with the plan prepared under subdivision 12.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Amount of penalty; considerations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may issue orders assessing administrative penalties up to $40,000.</p>
          <p>(b) In determining the amount of a penalty the commissioner may consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the gravity of the violation, including potential for, or real, damage to the public interest or natural resources of the state;</p>
          <p>(2) the history of past violations;</p>
          <p>(3) the number of violations;</p>
          <p>(4) the economic benefit gained by the person by allowing or committing the violation based on data from local or state bureaus or educational institutions; and</p>
          <p>(5) other factors as justice may require, if the commissioner specifically identifies the additional factors in the commissioner's order.</p>
          <p>(c) For a violation after an initial violation, including a continuation of the initial violation, the commissioner must, in determining the amount of a penalty, consider the factors in paragraph (b) and the:</p>
          <p>(1) similarity of the most recent previous violation and the violation to be penalized;</p>
          <p>(2) time elapsed since the last violation;</p>
          <p>(3) number of previous violations; and</p>
          <p>(4) response of the person to the most recent previous violation identified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contents of order.</span></h2>
          <p>An order assessing an administrative penalty under this section must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a concise statement of the facts alleged to constitute a violation;</p>
          <p>(2) a reference to the section of the statute, rule, order, or term or condition of a permit that has been violated;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement of the amount of the administrative penalty to be imposed and the factors upon which the penalty is based; and</p>
          <p>(4) a statement of the person's right to review of the order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Corrective order.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may issue an order assessing a penalty and requiring the violations cited in the order to be corrected within a time period specified by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The person to whom the order was issued must provide information to the commissioner before the 31st day after the order was received demonstrating that the violation has been corrected or that appropriate steps toward correcting the violation have been taken.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must determine whether the violation has been corrected and notify the person subject to the order of the commissioner's determination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), if the commissioner determines that the violation has been corrected or appropriate steps have been taken to correct the action, the penalty must be forgiven. Unless the person requests review of the order under subdivision 6 or 7 before the penalty is due, the penalty in the order is due and payable:</p>
          <p>(1) on the 31st day after the order was received, if the person subject to the order fails to provide information to the commissioner showing that the violation has been corrected or that appropriate steps have been taken toward correcting the violation; or</p>
          <p>(2) on the 20th day after the person receives the commissioner's determination under subdivision 4, paragraph (c), if the person subject to the order has provided information to the commissioner that the commissioner determines is not sufficient to show that the violation has been corrected or that appropriate steps have been taken toward correcting the violation.</p>
          <p>(b) For repeated or serious violations, the commissioner may issue an order with a penalty that is not forgiven after the corrective action is taken. The penalty is due 31 days after the order is received, unless review of the order under subdivision 6 or 7 is sought.</p>
          <p>(c) Interest at the rate established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/549.09">549.09</a> begins to accrue on penalties under this subdivision on the 31st day after the order with the penalty is received.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Expedited administrative hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 30 days after receiving an order or within 20 days after receiving notice that the commissioner has determined that a violation has not been corrected or appropriate steps have not been taken, the person subject to an order under this section may request an expedited hearing, using the procedures under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8510">1400.8510</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8612">1400.8612</a>, to review the commissioner's determination. The hearing request must specifically state the reasons for seeking review of the order. The person to whom the order is directed and the commissioner are the parties to the expedited hearing. The commissioner must notify the person to whom the order is directed of the time and place of the hearing at least 20 days before the hearing. The expedited hearing must be held within 30 days after a request for hearing has been filed with the commissioner unless the parties agree to a later date.</p>
          <p>(b) All written arguments must be submitted within ten days following the close of the hearing. The hearing must be conducted under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8510">1400.8510</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8612">1400.8612</a>, as modified by this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) The administrative law judge must issue a report making recommendations about the commissioner's action to the commissioner within 30 days following the close of the record. The administrative law judge may not recommend a change in the amount of the proposed penalty unless the administrative law judge determines that, based on the factors in subdivision 2, the amount of the penalty is unreasonable.</p>
          <p>(d) If the administrative law judge makes a finding that the hearing was requested solely for purposes of delay or that the hearing request was frivolous, the commissioner may add to the amount of the penalty the costs charged to the department by the Office of Administrative Hearings for the hearing.</p>
          <p>(e) If a hearing has been held, the commissioner may not issue a final order until at least five days after receipt of the report of the administrative law judge. The person to whom an order is issued may, within those five days, comment to the commissioner on the recommendations, and the commissioner must consider the comments. The final order may be appealed in the manner provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>.</p>
          <p>(f) If a hearing has been held and a final order issued by the commissioner, the penalty must be paid by 30 days after the date the final order is received unless review of the final order is requested under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.63">14.63</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.69">14.69</a>. If review is not requested or the order is reviewed and upheld, the amount due is the penalty, together with interest accruing from 31 days after the original order was received at the rate established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/549.09">549.09</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Mediation.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to review under subdivision 6, the commissioner may enter into mediation concerning an order issued under this section if the commissioner and the person to whom the order is issued both agree to mediation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Penalties due and payable.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may enforce penalties that are due and payable under this section in any manner provided by law for the collection of debts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Revoking or suspending permit.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person fails to pay a penalty owed under this section, the commissioner has grounds to revoke a permit or to refuse to amend a permit or issue a new permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Cumulative remedy.</span></h2>
          <p>The authority of the commissioner to issue a corrective order assessing penalties is in addition to other remedies available under statutory or common law. The payment of a penalty does not preclude the use of other enforcement provisions in connection with the violation for which the penalty was assessed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Depositing fees.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees collected under this section must be credited to the water management account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.299.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Plan for using administrative penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must prepare a plan for using the administrative penalty authority in this section. The plan must include explanations for how the commissioner will determine whether violations are minor, moderate, or severe. The commissioner must provide a 30-day period for public comment on the plan. The plan must be finalized within six months after July 1, 2014.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.299.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.23.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.87.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 87</a>-90</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.2991">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.2991 PENALTIES; ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2991.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Civil penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.134">103G.134</a>, may issue a notice to a person who violates:</p>
          <p>(1) this chapter;</p>
          <p>(2) a permit issued under this chapter or a term or condition of a permit issued under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(3) a duty under this chapter to permit an inspection, entry, or monitoring activity or a duty under this chapter to carry out an inspection or monitoring activity;</p>
          <p>(4) a rule adopted under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(5) a stipulation agreement, variance, or schedule of compliance entered into under this chapter; or</p>
          <p>(6) an order issued by the commissioner under this chapter.</p>
          <p>(b) A person issued a notice forfeits and must pay to the state a penalty, in an amount to be determined by the district court, of not more than $10,000 per day of violation.</p>
          <p>(c) In the discretion of the district court, a defendant under this section may be required to:</p>
          <p>(1) forfeit and pay to the state a sum that adequately compensates the state for the reasonable value of restoration, monitoring, and other expenses directly resulting from the unauthorized use of or damage to natural resources of the state; and</p>
          <p>(2) forfeit and pay to the state an additional sum to constitute just compensation for any damage, loss, or destruction of the state's natural resources and for other actual damages to the state caused by an unauthorized use of natural resources of the state.</p>
          <p>(d) As a defense to damages assessed under paragraph (c), a defendant may prove that the violation was caused solely by:</p>
          <p>(1) an act of God;</p>
          <p>(2) an act of war;</p>
          <p>(3) negligence on the part of the state;</p>
          <p>(4) an act or failure to act that constitutes sabotage or vandalism; or</p>
          <p>(5) any combination of clauses (1) to (4).</p>
          <p>(e) The civil penalties and damages provided for in this subdivision may be recovered by a civil action brought by the attorney general in the name of the state in Ramsey County District Court. Civil penalties and damages provided for in this subdivision may be resolved by the commissioner through a negotiated stipulation agreement according to the authority granted to the commissioner in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.134">103G.134</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2991.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>This chapter and rules, standards, orders, stipulation agreements, schedules of compliance, and permits adopted or issued by the commissioner under this chapter or any other law for preventing, controlling, or abating damage to natural resources may be enforced by one or more of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) criminal prosecution;</p>
          <p>(2) action to recover civil penalties;</p>
          <p>(3) injunction;</p>
          <p>(4) action to compel performance; or</p>
          <p>(5) other appropriate action according to this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2991.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Injunctions.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of this chapter or rules, standards, orders, stipulation agreements, variances, schedules of compliance, and permits adopted or issued under this chapter constitutes a public nuisance and may be enjoined as provided by law in an action, in the name of the state, brought by the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.2991.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Actions to compel performance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In an action to compel performance of an order issued by the commissioner for any purpose related to preventing, controlling, or abating damage to natural resources under this chapter, the court may require a defendant adjudged responsible to do and perform any and all acts set forth in the commissioner's order and all things within the defendant's power that are reasonably necessary to accomplish the purposes of the order.</p>
          <p>(b) If a municipality or its governing or managing body or any of its officers is a defendant, the court may require the municipality to exercise its powers, without regard to any limitation of a requirement for an election or referendum imposed thereon by law and without restricting the powers of the commissioner, to do any or all of the following, without limiting the generality hereof:</p>
          <p>(1) levy taxes or special assessments;</p>
          <p>(2) prescribe service or use charges;</p>
          <p>(3) borrow money;</p>
          <p>(4) issue bonds;</p>
          <p>(5) employ assistance;</p>
          <p>(6) acquire real or personal property;</p>
          <p>(7) let contracts;</p>
          <p>(8) otherwise provide for doing work or constructing, installing, maintaining, or operating facilities; and</p>
          <p>(9) do all acts and things reasonably necessary to accomplish the purposes of the commissioner's order.</p>
          <p>(c) The court must grant a municipality under paragraph (b) the opportunity to determine the appropriate financial alternatives to be used to comply with the court-imposed requirements.</p>
          <p>(d) An action brought under this subdivision must be venued in Ramsey County District Court.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.2991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.91.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 91</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL PERMIT; PROCEDURE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.301">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.301 GENERAL PERMIT; APPLICATION PROCEDURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application documentation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An application for a permit must be accompanied by:</p>
          <p>(1) maps, plans, and specifications describing the proposed appropriation and use of waters;</p>
          <p>(2) the changes, additions, repairs, or abandonment proposed to be made;</p>
          <p>(3) the waters of the state affected; and</p>
          <p>(4) other data the commissioner may require.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may require a statement of the effect the actions proposed in the permit application will have on the environment, including:</p>
          <p>(1) anticipated changes in water and related land resources;</p>
          <p>(2) unavoidable but anticipated detrimental effects; and</p>
          <p>(3) alternatives to the actions proposed in the permit application, including conservation measures to improve water use efficiencies and reduce water demand.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Permit application and notification fees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A fee to defray the costs of receiving, recording, and processing must be paid for a permit application authorized under this chapter, except for a general permit application, for each request to amend or transfer an existing permit, and for a notification to request authorization to conduct a project under a general permit. Fees established under this subdivision, unless specified in paragraph (c), must comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285">16A.1285</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Proposed projects that require water in excess of 100 million gallons per year must be assessed fees to recover the costs incurred to evaluate the project and the costs incurred for environmental review. Fees collected under this paragraph must be credited to an account in the natural resources fund and are appropriated to the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The fee to apply for a permit to appropriate water, in addition to any fee under paragraph (b), is $150. The application fee for a permit to construct or repair a dam that is subject to a dam safety inspection, to work in public waters, or to divert waters for mining must be at least $1,200, but not more than $12,000. The fee for a notification to request authorization to conduct a project under a general permit is $400, except that the fee for a notification to request authorization to appropriate water under a general permit is $100.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Field inspection fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the application fee, the commissioner may charge a field inspection fee for:</p>
          <p>(1) projects requiring a mandatory environmental assessment under chapter 116D;</p>
          <p>(2) projects undertaken without a required permit or application; and</p>
          <p>(3) projects undertaken in excess of limitations established in an issued permit.</p>
          <p>(b) The fee must be at least $100 but not more than actual inspection costs.</p>
          <p>(c) The fee is to cover actual costs related to a permit applied for under this chapter or for a project undertaken without proper authorization.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall establish a schedule of field inspection fees under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285">16A.1285</a>. The schedule must include actual costs related to field inspection, including investigations of the area affected by the proposed activity, analysis of the proposed activity, consultant services, and subsequent monitoring, if any, of the activity authorized by the permit. Fees collected under this subdivision must be credited to an account in the natural resources fund and are appropriated to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Refund prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A permit application, general permit notification, or field inspection fee may not be refunded for any reason, even if the application or request is denied or withdrawn.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">State and federal agencies exempt.</span></h2>
          <p>A permit application, general permit notification, or field inspection fee may not be imposed on any state agency, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.01">16B.01</a>, or federal governmental agency applying for a permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Town exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding this section or any other law, no permit application, general permit notification, or field inspection fee shall be charged to a town in connection with the construction or alteration of a town road, bridge, or culvert.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Filing application.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a permit must be filed with the commissioner. If the proposed activity for which the permit is requested is within a municipality, is within or affects a watershed district or a soil and water conservation district, or is within the boundaries of a reservation or Tribal community of a federally recognized Indian Tribe in Minnesota, a copy of the application with maps, plans, and specifications must be served on the mayor of the municipality, the secretary of the board of managers of the watershed district, the secretary of the board of supervisors of the soil and water conservation district, or the Tribal chair of the federally recognized Indian Tribe, as applicable. For purposes of this section, "federally recognized Indian Tribe" means the Minnesota Tribal governments listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10.65#stat.10.65.2">10.65, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Recommendation of local units of government and federally recognized Indian Tribes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the proposed activity for which the permit is requested is within a municipality, or is within or affects a watershed district or a soil and water conservation district, the commissioner may obtain a written recommendation of the managers of the district and the board of supervisors of the soil and water conservation district or the mayor of the municipality before issuing or denying the permit.</p>
          <p>(b) The managers, supervisors, or mayor must file a recommendation within 30 days after receiving of a copy of the application for permit.</p>
          <p>(c) If the proposed activity for which the permit is requested is within the boundaries of a reservation or Tribal community of a federally recognized Indian Tribe in Minnesota, the federally recognized Indian Tribe may:</p>
          <p>(1) submit recommendations to the commissioner within 30 days of receiving the application; or</p>
          <p>(2) request Tribal consultation according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10.65">10.65</a> within 30 days of receiving the application.</p>
          <p>(d) If Tribal consultation is requested under paragraph (c), clause (2), a permit application is not complete until after the consultation occurs or 90 days after the request for consultation is made, whichever is sooner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.301.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Depositing fees.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees collected under this section must be credited to the water management account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.34.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/298/#laws.4.1.0">1991 c 298 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 186 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.13.0">1995 c 218 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.3.10.0">1996 c 305 art 3 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.3.9.0">1999 c 250 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/160/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 160 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/138/#laws.0.5.0">2005 c 138 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.122.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 122</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.135.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 135</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.35.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 35</a>,36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.56.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.15.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.52.0">2012 c 272 s 52</a>-55; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.98.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.89.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.92.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 92</a>-94; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.49.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 49</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.305">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.305 TIME LIMIT TO ACT ON WATER-USE PERMIT APPLICATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.305.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General 150-day limit.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in subdivision 2, the commissioner must act on a water-use permit within 150 days after the completed application for the permit has been submitted. Within 30 business days of application for a water-use permit, the commissioner shall notify the applicant, in writing, whether the application is complete or incomplete.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must direct a hearing to be held on a water-use permit application or make an order issuing a permit or denying a permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.305.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>The requirements of subdivision 1 do not apply to applications for a water-use permit for:</p>
          <p>(1) appropriations for diversion from the basin of origin of more than 2,000,000 gallons per day average in a 30-day period; or</p>
          <p>(2) appropriations with a consumptive use of more than 2,000,000 gallons per day average for a 30-day period.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.305.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.35.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/186/#laws.0.16.0">1993 c 186 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.57.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.57.0">2014 c 289 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.311">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.311 PERMIT HEARING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hearing requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>A hearing must be conducted as a contested case hearing under chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Hearing notice.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The hearing notice on an application must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the date, place, and time fixed by the commissioner for the hearing;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the waters affected, the water levels sought to be established, or control structures proposed; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the matters prescribed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.57">14.57</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.59">14.59</a> and rules adopted thereunder.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A summary of the hearing notice must be published by the commissioner at the expense of the applicant or, if the proceeding is initiated by the commissioner in the absence of an applicant, at the expense of the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The summary of the hearing notice must be:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) published once a week for two successive weeks before the day of hearing in a legal newspaper published in the county where any part of the affected waters is located; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) mailed or electronically transmitted by the commissioner to the county auditor, the mayor of a municipality, the watershed district, and the soil and water conservation district affected by the application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Subpoena of witnesses and evidence.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may subpoena and compel the attendance of witnesses and the production of books and documents that are material to the purposes of the hearing.</p>
          <p>(b) Disobedience of a subpoena is punishable in the same manner as a contempt of the district court. The commissioner must file a complaint of the disobedience of a subpoena with the district court of the county where the subpoena was disobeyed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Waiving hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may waive a hearing on an application and order the permit to be issued or deny the permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Demand for hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a hearing is waived and an order is made issuing or denying the permit, the applicant, the managers of the watershed district, the board of supervisors of the soil and water conservation district, or the governing body of the municipality may file a demand for hearing on the application. The demand for a hearing must be filed within 30 days after mailed or electronically transmitted notice of the order with the bond required by subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must give notice as provided in subdivision 2, hold a hearing on the application, and make a determination on issuing or denying the permit as though the previous order had not been made.</p>
          <p>(c) The order issuing or denying the permit becomes final at the end of 30 days after mailed or electronically transmitted notice of the order to the applicant, the managers of the watershed district, the board of supervisors of the soil and water conservation district, or the governing body of the municipality, and an appeal of the order may not be taken if:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner waives a hearing and a demand for a hearing is not made; or</p>
          <p>(2) a hearing is demanded but a bond is not filed as required by subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Bond for demanding public hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An applicant filing a demand for a public hearing must execute and file a corporate surety bond or equivalent security to the state of Minnesota, to be approved by the commissioner and in an amount and form determined by the commissioner. The bond or security must be conditioned to pay the costs of the hearing if the commissioner's order issuing or denying a permit is affirmed without material modification.</p>
          <p>(b) A bond or security is not required of a public authority that demands a public hearing.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may waive the requirement for a bond or other security.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.311.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Hearing costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraphs (b) and (c), the costs of a hearing must be paid as prescribed by chapter 14 and the chief administrative law judge.</p>
          <p>(b) If a hearing is waived by the commissioner, but the applicant other than a public authority demands a hearing on the application and the commissioner's order is affirmed without material modification, the applicant must pay the following costs up to $750:</p>
          <p>(1) costs of the stenographic record and transcript;</p>
          <p>(2) rental costs, if any, of the place where the hearing is held; and</p>
          <p>(3) costs of publication of orders made by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) If a hearing is waived by the commissioner, but a hearing is demanded by a public authority other than the applicant and the commissioner's order is affirmed without material modification, the public authority making the demand must pay:</p>
          <p>(1) costs of the stenographic record and transcript;</p>
          <p>(2) rental costs, if any, of the place where the hearing is held; and</p>
          <p>(3) costs of publication of orders made by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.311.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.36.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.58.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.76.0">2011 c 107 s 76</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.90.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 90</a>,91</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.315">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.315 DENYING AND ISSUING PERMITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's general authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may deny issuing permits and issue permits with or without conditions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Findings of fact.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make findings of fact on issues necessary for determination of the applications considered. Orders made by the commissioner must be based upon findings of fact made on substantial evidence. The commissioner may have investigations made. The facts disclosed by investigation must be put in evidence at the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Granting permit.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner concludes that the plans of the applicant are reasonable, practical, and will adequately protect public safety and promote the public welfare, the commissioner shall grant the permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Control levels.</span></h2>
          <p>If they are in issue, the commissioner shall also fix the control levels of public waters accordingly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Denial; modifications.</span></h2>
          <p>Otherwise the commissioner shall reject the application or may require modification of the plan as the commissioner finds proper to protect the public interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof; conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In permit applications, the applicant has the burden of proving that the proposed project is reasonable, practical, and will adequately protect public safety and promote the public welfare.</p>
          <p>(b) In granting a permit, the commissioner may include in it terms and reservations about the amount and manner of the use or appropriation or method of construction or operation of controls as appear reasonably necessary for the safety and welfare of the people of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Restoring effects of unlawful activities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may include in an order issuing or denying a permit a requirement for the applicant to take an action necessary to restore the public waters or their beds to the condition existing before unlawful activities, if any, were undertaken by the applicant. The restoration may include filling beds unlawfully dredged, removing fill unlawfully placed, or restoring water unlawfully appropriated.</p>
          <p>(b) If a hearing on the application was not held, the applicant may contest the order within 30 days of receiving it and must be given a contested case hearing as prescribed by chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notice of permit order.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice of orders made after hearing must be given by publication of the order once a week for two successive weeks in a legal newspaper in the county where the hearing was held and by mailing or electronically transmitting copies of the order to parties who entered an appearance at the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Time for issuing order.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make an order within 60 days after the completion of the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Charges for excavating minerals.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must impose charges for the excavation of minerals from the beds of public waters, as provided in chapter 93.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Limitations on permits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as otherwise expressly provided by law, a permit issued by the commissioner under this chapter is subject to:</p>
          <p>(1) cancellation by the commissioner at any time if necessary to protect the public interests;</p>
          <p>(2) further conditions on the term of the permit or its cancellation as the commissioner may prescribe and amend and reissue the permit; and</p>
          <p>(3) applicable law existing before or after the issuance of the permit.</p>
          <p>(b) Permits issued to irrigate agricultural land are subject to this subdivision and are subject to cancellation by the commissioner upon the recommendation of the supervisors of the soil and water conservation district where the land to be irrigated is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Permit not issued until fees paid.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for field inspection fees related to monitoring, the commissioner may not issue a permit until all fees required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301">103G.301</a> relating to the issuance of a permit have been paid. The time limits prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.305#stat.103G.305.1">103G.305, subdivision 1</a>, do not apply to an application for which the appropriate fee has not been paid. Field inspection fees relating to monitoring of an activity authorized by a permit may be charged and collected as necessary at any time after the issuance of the permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Period for activities under permit.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall set the time period within which:</p>
          <p>(1) construction authorized in the permit must be completed; or</p>
          <p>(2) an appropriation or use of water must be made.</p>
          <p>(b) The time must not exceed five years from the date of the permit.</p>
          <p>(c) The time period may be extended by the commissioner after application and for good cause demonstrated by the permittee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Irrevocability of certain permits related to mining.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Permits granted in connection with the mining, transporting, concentration, or shipment of taconite as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/93.20#stat.93.20.18">93.20, subdivision 18</a>, and permits granted in connection with the mining, production, or beneficiation of copper, copper-nickel, or nickel, are irrevocable for the term of the permits without the consent of the permittee, except for breach or nonperformance of any condition of the permit by the permittee.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may allow and prescribe in the permit any time the commissioner considers reasonable, notwithstanding the limitations under subdivision 13, limitations of time contained in this section for beginning or completing construction or operations under the permit, or exercising the rights granted under the permit.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may extend the time, for cause shown, upon the application of the permittee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.315.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt rules prescribing standards and criteria for issuing and denying water-use permits and public-waters-work permits. The authority to adopt the rules is exempt from the 18-month time limit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.125">14.125</a> and does not expire.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.315.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.37.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.14.0">1995 c 218 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.58.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.92.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 92</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.50.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 50</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WATER LEVEL; ESTABLISHMENT AND CONTROL</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.401">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.401 APPLICATION TO ESTABLISH LAKE LEVELS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Applications for authority to establish and maintain levels of public waters and applications to establish the natural ordinary high-water level of public waters may be made to the commissioner by a public body or authority or by a majority of the riparian owners on the public waters.</p>
        <p>(b) To conserve or utilize the water resources of the state, the commissioner may initiate proceedings to establish and maintain the level of public waters.</p>
        <p>(c) When establishing an ordinary high-water level, the commissioner must provide written or electronic notice of the order to the local units of government where the public water is located.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.38.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.83.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 83</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.405">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.405 WATER LEVEL CONTROL FOR LANDLOCKED LAKES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (c), the commissioner must issue a water level control permit to establish a control elevation for a landlocked lake below the ordinary high-water level for the lake if:</p>
        <p>(1) the commissioner finds that:</p>
        <p>(i) the control is necessary to prevent adverse impacts to the lake or adjoining property;</p>
        <p>(ii) other reasonable or cost-effective alternatives are not available; and</p>
        <p>(iii) natural resource or hydrologic conditions exist in the watershed that would limit the potential for continuous discharge of excess waters from the lake; and</p>
        <p>(2) the outlet and discharge of excess waters is addressed in an approved water management plan under chapter 103B or 103D.</p>
        <p>(b) In addition to the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.6">103G.301, subdivision 6</a>, if the proposed control elevation is more than 1-1/2 feet below the ordinary high-water level, the permit applicant shall serve a copy of the application on each county and municipality within which any portion of the lake is located and the lake improvement district, if one exists.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner may not issue a permit to establish a control elevation more than 1-1/2 feet below the ordinary high-water level of a lake if a county, municipality, watershed district, or lake improvement district required to be served under paragraph (b) or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.6">103G.301, subdivision 6</a>, files a written objection to the issuance of the permit with the commissioner within 30 days after receiving a copy of the application.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.405.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.39.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/407/#laws.0.45.0">1996 c 407 s 45</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.407">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.407 WATER LEVEL CONTROL FOR PUBLIC WATERS WITH OUTLET.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner, upon due consideration of recommendations and objections as provided in paragraph (c), may issue a public-waters-work permit to establish a control elevation for a public water with an outlet that is different than any previously existing or established control level when:</p>
        <p>(1) all of the property abutting the ordinary high-water mark of the public water is in public ownership or the public entity has obtained permanent flowage easements; and</p>
        <p>(2) the commissioner finds that the proposed change in the control level is in the public interest and causes minimal adverse environmental impact.</p>
        <p>(b) In addition to the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.6">103G.301, subdivision 6</a>, if the proposed control elevation differs from any historical control level, the permit applicant shall serve a copy of the application on each county and municipality within which any portion of the lake is located and on the lake improvement district, if one exists.</p>
        <p>(c) A county, municipality, watershed district, watershed management organization, or lake improvement district required to be served under paragraph (b) or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.6">103G.301, subdivision 6</a>, may file a written recommendation for the issuance of the permit or an objection to the issuance of the permit with the commissioner within 30 days after receiving a copy of the application.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.407.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/262/#laws.2.6.0">2004 c 262 art 2 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.408">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.408 TEMPORARY DRAWDOWN OF PUBLIC WATERS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner, upon consideration of recommendations and objections as provided in clause (2), item (iii), and paragraph (c), may issue a public-waters-work permit for the temporary drawdown of a public water when:</p>
        <p>(1) the public water is a shallow lake to be managed for fish, wildlife, or ecological purposes by the commissioner and the commissioner has conducted a public hearing presenting a comprehensive management plan outlining how and when temporary drawdowns under this section will be conducted; or</p>
        <p>(2) the permit applicant is a public entity and:</p>
        <p>(i) the commissioner deems the project to be beneficial and makes findings of fact that the drawdown is in the public interest;</p>
        <p>(ii) the permit applicant has obtained permission from at least 75 percent of the riparian landowners; and</p>
        <p>(iii) the permit applicant has conducted a public hearing according to paragraph (d).</p>
        <p>(b) In addition to the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.6">103G.301, subdivision 6</a>, the permit applicant shall serve a copy of the application on each county, municipality, and watershed management organization, if one exists, within which any portion of the public water is located and on the lake improvement district, if one exists.</p>
        <p>(c) A county, municipality, watershed district, watershed management organization, or lake improvement district required to be served under paragraph (b) or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.301#stat.103G.301.6">103G.301, subdivision 6</a>, may file a written recommendation for the issuance of a permit or an objection to the issuance of a permit with the commissioner within 30 days after receiving a copy of the application.</p>
        <p>(d) The hearing notice for a public hearing under paragraph (a), clause (2), item (iii), must:</p>
        <p>(1) include the date, place, and time for the hearing;</p>
        <p>(2) include the waters affected and a description of the proposed project;</p>
        <p>(3) be mailed or electronically transmitted to the director, the county auditor, the clerk or mayor of a municipality, the lake improvement district if one exists, the watershed district or water management organization, the soil and water conservation district, and all riparian owners of record affected by the application; and</p>
        <p>(4) be published in a newspaper of general circulation in the affected area.</p>
        <p>(e) Periodic temporary drawdowns conducted under paragraph (a) are not considered takings from riparian landowners.</p>
        <p>(f) This section does not apply to public waters that have been designated for wildlife management under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.101">97A.101</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.408.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/48/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 48 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.80.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.93.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 93</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.411">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.411 STIPULATION OF LOW-WATER MARK.</h1>
        <p>If the state is a party in a civil action relating to the navigability or ownership of the bed of a body of water, river, or stream, the commissioner, on behalf of the state, may agree by written stipulation with a riparian owner who is a party to the action on the location of the ordinary low-water mark on the riparian land of the party. After the stipulation is executed by all parties, it must be presented to the judge of the district court where the action is pending for approval. If the stipulation is approved, the judge shall make and enter an order providing that the final judgment when entered shall conform to the location of the ordinary, low-water mark as provided for in the stipulation as it relates to the parties to the stipulation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.40.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.121.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 121</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.17.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.412">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.412 STREAM GAUGE DATA.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall provide an easily accessible link to the Department of Natural Resources' and the Pollution Control Agency's cooperative stream gauging data, including lake level information for existing stations, including White Bear Lake and Turtle Lake, on the department's website.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.412.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.14.9.0">2014 c 312 art 14 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.413">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.413 APPEAL OF ORDER ESTABLISHING ORDINARY HIGH-WATER LEVEL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.413.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Petition.</span></h2>
          <p>A local unit of government may petition for review of the ordinary high-water level. A petition may be filed on behalf of the local unit of government or riparian landowner affected by the ordinary high-water level. The petition must be filed by the local unit of government and include reasons why the determination should be reviewed and evidence to be considered as part of the review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.413.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Review.</span></h2>
          <p>If a local unit of government files a petition under this section, the commissioner must review the petition within 90 days of the request and issue a final order. The commissioner may extend this period by 90 days by providing written notice of the extension to the applicant. Any further extension requires the agreement of the petitioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.413.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.84.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 84</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BIG STONE LAKE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.415">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.415 BIG STONE LAKE; SEASONAL WATER LEVEL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Desirable water level elevation determined.</span></h2>
          <p>The most desirable and beneficial level for the waters of Big Stone Lake from May 1 to October 1 is elevation 967, project datum, and the director of game and fish of South Dakota and the commissioner of natural resources of Minnesota shall maintain and operate the Big Stone control dam in conformance with this elevation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Regulation at levels less than desirable elevation.</span></h2>
          <p>When the water elevation of Big Stone Lake is 967 or less, project datum:</p>
          <p>(1) stop logs must be kept in place and maintained in the outlet dam of Big Stone Lake; and</p>
          <p>(2) the outflow from the outlet dam must be regulated not to exceed 100 cubic feet per second.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.41.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MISSISSIPPI HEADWATER LAKES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.421">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.421 CONTROL OF MISSISSIPPI HEADWATER LAKES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reason for control.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that the regulation, control, and utilization of waters in the headwater lakes in the Mississippi River, including Leech Lake, Winnibigoshish Lake, Pokegama Lake, Pine River, the Whitefish chain, Sandy Lake, and Gull Lake are of tremendous economic importance and value to the state and the utility of these lakes in aid of navigation has been very greatly diminished since the time of the establishment of the reservoirs, and that the economic values in utilization of these waters for state purposes has increased tremendously. These factors require the assertion on the part of the state of Minnesota of its rights to utilization and control of these water areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Joint federal-state control.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall enter into cooperative agreements with the United States of America acting through the Department of the Army for the joint control and regulation of the Mississippi headwater reservoirs to control the water elevations and the water discharges from the Mississippi headwaters lakes in the interests of the state, subject only to:</p>
          <p>(1) a paramount need of waters from these sources in aid of substantial navigation requirements; and</p>
          <p>(2) a substantial requirement of providing necessary flood control storage capacity as determined by the United States Department of the Army Corps of Engineers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Plan for dam operation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must develop a plan for the operation of the dams controlling each of the Mississippi headwater reservoirs that:</p>
          <p>(1) establishes the water elevation on each of the Mississippi headwater lakes at the most desirable height and stabilizes the stages at that point, as practicable, during the recreational use season;</p>
          <p>(2) considers reasonable fluctuations when desirable for the production of wild rice in the wild rice producing areas of Mississippi headwater lakes;</p>
          <p>(3) considers the elevations most desirable for the production and maintenance of wildlife resources;</p>
          <p>(4) considers the needs of water for recreation, agriculture, forestry, game and fish, industry, municipal water supply and sewage disposal, power generation, and other purposes in the Mississippi River headwaters and downstream;</p>
          <p>(5) establishes stages at which the water will be maintained, as practicable, but recognizing the following minimum stages in reference to present zeros on the respective government gauges:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:8.461538461538462%">
            <col style="width:46.1538461538462%">
            <col style="width:45.3846153846154%">
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(i)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Leech Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>0.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(ii)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Winnibigoshish Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>6.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(iii)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Pokegama Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>6.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(iv)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Sandy Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>7.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(v)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Pine River </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>9.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(vi)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Gull Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>5.0;</td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p>(6) prescribes maximum discharges when the elevations fall below the stages; and</p>
          <p>(7) prescribes maximum elevations and amounts of discharge from each lake to stabilize and effectuate the desired stages and, as practicable, does not allow the elevation to exceed the following maximum lake stages:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:8.461538461538462%">
            <col style="width:46.1538461538462%">
            <col style="width:45.3846153846154%">
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(i)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Leech Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>3.5;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(ii)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Winnibigoshish Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>12.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(iii)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Pokegama Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>12.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(iv)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Sandy Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>11.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(v)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Pine River </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>14.0;</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td style="text-align: right;">(vi)</td>
              <td>
                <span>Gull Lake </span>
                <span class="leader">.</span>
              </td>
              <td>7.0.</td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p>(b) The plan developed by the commissioner must consider the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) the necessity for changing discharges to meet emergencies resulting from unexpected or abnormal inflows;</p>
          <p>(2) the possibility of overriding requirements of the federal government for substantial discharges to meet reasonable and substantial navigation requirements; and</p>
          <p>(3) the overriding authority and needs as prescribed by the United States Department of the Army Corps of Engineers in discharging their functions of requiring additional storage capacity for flood control purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notice of plan.</span></h2>
          <p>Before the plan of operation for a headwater lake is effective, the commissioner must publish a notice of hearing on the plan of operation for two weeks in a newspaper in each county where the affected waters are located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Hearing on plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The hearing must be conducted by the commissioner or an appointed referee. The hearing will not be governed by legal rules of evidence, but the findings of fact and orders, to be made and formulated by the commissioner, must be predicated only on relevant, material, and competent evidence.</p>
          <p>(b) Interested parties must have an opportunity to be heard, under oath, and are subject to cross-examination by adverse parties and by the attorney general or the attorney general's representative who shall represent the commissioner at the hearing.</p>
          <p>(c) The findings of fact and orders incorporating the plan determined by the commissioner must be published for two weeks in the same manner as the notice of hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>A riparian owner or water user aggrieved by the commissioner's findings has the right to appeal by 30 days after completion of publication of the findings and order to the district court of a county where the regulated water is located. The appeal shall be determined by the court on the record made before the commissioner. Issues on the appeal are limited to the legal rights of the parties and whether the findings of the commissioner are reasonably supported by the evidence at the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.421.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Modifications.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After a plan has been put into effect, the commissioner is authorized to modify the stages sought to be maintained by modifying the plan with respect to any of the lakes involved to the extent of one foot in elevation according to the zeros of the present government gauges without holding additional hearings, except a departure from the elevation target may not be made to reduce proposed stages below the minimums prescribed by subdivision 3, paragraph (a), clause (5), during the recreational season.</p>
          <p>(b) A modification of the established plan that departs by more than one foot in elevation may be placed into effect only after a hearing is held in the same manner as the hearing establishing the plan as provided under subdivisions 4 and 5.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.421.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.42.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 42</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DAM CONSTRUCTION AND MAINTENANCE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.501">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.501 CONSTRUCTING PRIVATE DAMS ON NONNAVIGABLE WATERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.501.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Procedure to acquire flowage rights.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person desires to raise and extend or erect and maintain a dam on the person's property across a stream or other watercourse that is not navigable to create or improve a waterpower for milling or manufacturing purposes and property owned by other persons will be overflowed or otherwise damaged, the person desiring to erect or extend or raise the dam may acquire the right to do so by petitioning the court and having damages ascertained and paid as prescribed in chapter 117.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.501.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Damage to previous waterpower prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A dam may not be erected, raised, or maintained under this section that damages a waterpower previously developed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.501.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Project and repair completion times.</span></h2>
          <p>If the right to erect, raise, or extend a dam is acquired under subdivision 1, the project must be started within one year, completed, and the waterpower applied to the purpose stated in the petition within three years after the right to erect, raise, or extend the dam is acquired. If a dam or the machinery connected with the dam is destroyed, the rebuilding of the dam or machinery must be started and completed within the same periods after the destruction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.501.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Forfeiture.</span></h2>
          <p>Failure to comply with subdivision 3 or failure to operate a mill or machinery for one consecutive year after it is erected forfeits the rights acquired under subdivision 1 unless the owner is an infant, or is otherwise legally disabled, in which case the periods under this section begin after the disability is removed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.501.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.43.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 43</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.505">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.505 DAM CONSTRUCTION AND MAINTENANCE BY STATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.505.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may construct, maintain, and operate dikes, dams, and other structures necessary to maintain uniform water levels established under this chapter to improve navigation, protect and improve domestic water supply, protect and preserve fish and other wildlife, protect the public interest in the shore and shorelines of public waters, and promote public health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.505.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acquiring land.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may acquire lands or any necessary interest in lands by purchase, gift, or condemnation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.505.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Operating dams on state property.</span></h2>
          <p>Dams owned by the state or built on property owned or controlled by the state must be maintained under the direction of the commissioner and operated under the commissioner's direction and control.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.505.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Accepting local funding.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may accept funds from local governmental and civic agencies or persons to acquire property for or to construct, maintain, or operate dams and control structures.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.505.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.44.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 44</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.511">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.511 PUBLICLY OWNED DAM REPAIR.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.1">
