          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may:</p>
          <p>(1) repair or reconstruct state-owned dams;</p>
          <p>(2) make engineering evaluations related to the repair or reconstruction of dams owned by political subdivisions; and</p>
          <p>(3) grant aid to political subdivisions to repair or reconstruct dams owned by political subdivisions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Engineering evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>The engineering evaluations may include studies of the feasibility, practicality, and environmental effects of using dams for hydroelectric power generation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in this section, a grant to a political subdivision may not exceed the amount contributed to the project by the political subdivision from local funds.</p>
          <p>(b) Federal general revenue sharing money may be counted as local funds, but other federal grants or loans must be used to reduce equally the state share and the local share of project costs.</p>
          <p>(c) A grant to study the feasibility, practicality, and environmental effects of using a dam for hydroelectric power generation may be for an amount up to 90 percent of the costs of the study.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Investigation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may repair or reconstruct a state-owned dam or make a grant to a political subdivision only after making an investigation of the dam.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision desiring a grant for the repair or reconstruction of a dam may apply for the grant on forms supplied by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Determining grant.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall consider all relevant factors in determining whether to repair or reconstruct a state-owned dam or to make a grant to a political subdivision including:</p>
          <p>(1) the age and type of construction of the dam;</p>
          <p>(2) the use of the dam for water supply, flood control, navigation, hydroelectric power generation, recreation, wildlife management, scenic value, or other purposes related to public health, safety, and welfare;</p>
          <p>(3) the consequences of abandonment, removal, or alteration of the dam;</p>
          <p>(4) prospective future uses of the dam; and</p>
          <p>(5) the relative importance of the dam to the statewide water resource program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may hold a public hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.311">103G.311</a> on the proposed repair or reconstruction after giving notice. If the hearing is held at the request of a political subdivision, the costs of publishing notice and of taking and preparing the stenographic record must be paid by the political subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Operation agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>To receive a grant, the political subdivision must enter into an agreement with the commissioner giving assurance that the government unit will operate and maintain the dam in a safe condition for the benefit of the public and must agree to other conditions the commissioner considers reasonable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the cost of repair or reconstruction of a state-owned dam or a grant to a political subdivision is less than $250,000, the commissioner may direct that the state-owned dam be repaired or reconstructed or that a grant be made to repair or reconstruct a dam owned by a political subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) If the cost of repair or reconstruction of a state-owned claim or grant to a political subdivision is $250,000 or more, the commissioner may recommend the project to the legislature for its consideration and action, except in an emergency under paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner, with the approval of the commissioner of management and budget after consulting with the legislative advisory commission, may direct that a state-owned dam be repaired or reconstructed or a grant be made to a political subdivision if the commissioner determines that an emergency exists and:</p>
          <p>(1) there is danger that life will be lost; or</p>
          <p>(2) that substantial property losses will be suffered if action is not promptly taken.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Loans for local share of project costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the commissioner decides to recommend a dam repair or reconstruction grant for a political subdivision to the legislature, the commissioner must notify the political subdivision and the commissioner of management and budget of the decision. After being notified by the commissioner of natural resources, the political subdivision may apply to the commissioner of management and budget on forms supplied by the commissioner of management and budget for a loan up to 90 percent of the local share of the project costs.</p>
          <p>(b) The loan is repayable over a period not longer than 20 years, with interest at a rate sufficient to cover the cost to the state of borrowing the money.</p>
          <p>(c) A political subdivision receiving a dam safety loan must levy for the loan payment in the year the loan proceeds were received and each later year, until the loan is paid. The levy must be for:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount of the annual loan payment; or</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of the loan payment less the amount the political subdivision certifies is available from other sources for the loan payment.</p>
          <p>(d) Upon approval of the project grant by the legislature, the commissioner of management and budget shall make the loan in an amount and on terms that are appropriate. Loans made under this subdivision do not require approval by the electors of the political subdivision as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.58">475.58</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) Principal and interest payments received by the commissioner of management and budget in repayment of these loans are appropriated to the state bond fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's order to repair or reconstruct a dam.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a political subdivision fails to comply with a commissioner's order to repair or remove a dam under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.515">103G.515</a>, the commissioner may repair or remove the dam as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must hold a hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.311">103G.311</a> on the failure of the political subdivision to repair or remove the dam. After the hearing, the commissioner must make findings specifying the failure of the political subdivision to act and shall, by order, assume the powers of the legislative authority of the political subdivision in regard to the repair or removal of dams.</p>
          <p>(c) After issuing the order, the commissioner has the same powers, insofar as applicable to the repair or removal of dams, as the commissioners of administration and the Pollution Control Agency have in the construction, installation, maintenance, or operation of a municipal disposal system, or part of a system, or issuing bonds and levying taxes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.48">115.48</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.511.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Priority list of dams needing repair.</span></h2>
          <p>After reviewing examinations of dams owned by the state and political subdivisions, the commissioner shall prioritize the state and political subdivision dams in need of repair, reconstruction, or removal and report by June 1 of each odd-numbered year to the legislature. The commissioner must prioritize projects considering danger to life, damage to property, and the factors listed in subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.511.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.45.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.50.0">1994 c 643 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.16.0">1995 c 218 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.515">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.515 EXAMINING AND REPAIRING DAMS AND RESERVOIRS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.515.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Examining structures.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may examine a reservoir, dam, control structure, or waterway obstruction after receiving a complaint or determining an examination is needed. The commissioner, or an authorized agent, must be granted access at any reasonable time to examine the reservoir, dam, control structure, or waterway obstruction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.515.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Additional engineering investigations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After making an examination, if the commissioner determines that additional engineering investigations are necessary to determine the safety of a dam, reservoir, control structure, or waterway obstruction and the nature and extent of the necessary repairs or alterations, the commissioner must notify the owner to have investigations made at the owner's expense.</p>
          <p>(b) The result of the investigation must be filed with the commissioner for use in determining the condition of the structures and the need for their repair, alteration, or removal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.515.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Repair, alteration, or removal required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the commissioner determines that the reservoir, dam, control structure, or waterway obstruction is unsafe or needs repair or alteration, the commissioner shall notify the owner of the structure with an order to repair, alter, or remove the structure. The order must be issued in the same manner as if the owner had applied for a permit for the repairs, alterations, or removal.</p>
          <p>(b) The engineering investigations or the work of repair, alteration, or removal must begin and be completed within a reasonable time prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.515.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Dam inspection fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall adopt rules which must include a fee schedule to cover the cost of dam inspection and must classify structures to adequately define risks and hazards involved in relation to public health, safety, and welfare.</p>
          <p>(b) The rules may not impose a field inspection fee on any state agency, political subdivision of the state, or federal governmental agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.515.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Removing hazardous dams.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any provision of this section or of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.511">103G.511</a> relating to cost sharing or apportionment, the commissioner, within the limits of legislative appropriation, may assume or pay the entire cost of removal of a privately or publicly owned dam upon determining removal provides the lowest cost solution and:</p>
          <p>(1) that continued existence of the structure presents a significant public safety hazard, or prevents restoration of an important fisheries resource; or</p>
          <p>(2) that public or private property is being damaged due to partial failure of the structure.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.515.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.46.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 218 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.59.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 59</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.521">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.521 TRANSFER OF AUTHORITY OVER STATE DAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.521.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Upon application by resolution of the governing body of a political subdivision authorized to maintain and operate dams or other control structures affecting public waters, the commissioner, with the approval of the Executive Council, may transfer to the political subdivision the custody of a dam or other control structures owned by the state and under the supervision or control of the commissioner if the commissioner determines that the transfer will promote the best interests of the public. The transfer must be made by order of the commissioner on the terms and conditions the commissioner sets for maintenance and operation of the project.</p>
          <p>(b) In connection with the transfer, the commissioner may convey land, easements, or other state property pertaining to the project to the transferee by deed or another appropriate instrument in the name of the state, subject to conditions and reservations prescribed by the commissioner. A duplicate of each order, conveyance, or other instrument executed by the commissioner in connection with a transfer must be filed with the commissioner of management and budget.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.521.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Payment.</span></h2>
          <p>A transfer may be made with or without payment of money to the state, as agreed upon between the commissioner and the transferee. If a payment is received as part of the transfer, the amount must be deposited into the state treasury and credited to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.521.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.47.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.51.0">1994 c 643 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.525">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.525 LIMITATIONS ON TRANSFERRING DAM OWNERSHIP.</h1>
        <p>The state, a state department or agency, a county, municipality, town, or other governmental entity may not purchase or accept as a gift a privately owned dam subject to permit requirements until:</p>
        <p>(1) the commissioner has examined the dam;</p>
        <p>(2) the commissioner has prepared a report of the examination;</p>
        <p>(3) the report has been filed with the legislature; and</p>
        <p>(4) the legislature has had an opportunity to consider the report and has not prohibited the purchase or gift during the legislative session in which the report is filed or, if the report is filed when the legislature is not in session, the legislature has not prohibited the gift or purchase at the next session.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.525.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.48.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 48</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.531">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.531 DAM PERMIT EXCEPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.531.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Exemption for structures constructed before July 1, 1937.</span></h2>
          <p>This chapter does not authorize the commissioner to require a permit for the original construction of dams, reservoirs, or control works in existence on and before July 1, 1937.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.531.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Minor dam exclusion.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt rules that exclude minor dams such as those less than six feet in height or that impound less than 50 acre-feet of water storage at maximum storage elevations from permit requirements. The rules do not apply to a barrier six feet or less in height, regardless of storage capacity, or to a barrier creating a storage capacity at maximum water storage elevation of 15 acre-feet or less, regardless of height.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.531.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.49.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 49</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.535">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.535 HYDROPOWER GENERATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.535.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Public purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that:</p>
          <p>(1) the public health, safety, and welfare of the state is also promoted by the use of state waters to produce hydroelectric or hydromechanical power in a manner consistent with laws relating to dam construction, reconstruction, repair, and maintenance; and</p>
          <p>(2) the leasing of existing dams and potential dam sites primarily for power generation is a valid public purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.535.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Leasing authority.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision, or the commissioner with the approval of the state executive council for state-owned dams, may provide by a lease or development agreement for the development and operation of dams, dam sites, and hydroelectric or hydromechanical power generation plants by an individual, a corporation, an organization, or other legal entity on terms and conditions in subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.535.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Installations less than 15,000 kilowatts unused on January 1, 1984.</span></h2>
          <p>If an installation of 15,000 kilowatts or less at a dam site and reservoir was unused on January 1, 1984, in connection with the production of hydroelectric or hydromechanical power, the lease or development agreement negotiated by the political subdivision and the developer constitutes full payment by the lessee and may be in lieu of all real or personal property taxes that might otherwise be due to a political subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.535.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Municipality or town approval.</span></h2>
          <p>If the dam, dam site, or power generation plant is located in or contiguous to a municipality or town, other than the lessor political subdivision, the lease or agreement is not effective unless it is approved by the governing body of the municipality or town.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.535.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contents of development agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An agreement for the development or redevelopment of a hydropower site must contain provisions to assure the maximum financial return to the political subdivision or the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) An agreement may contain:</p>
          <p>(1) the period of the development agreement up to 99 years, subject to negotiations between the parties, and conditions for extension, modification, or termination;</p>
          <p>(2) provisions for a performance bond on the developer or certification that the equipment and its installation have a design life at least as long as the lease; and</p>
          <p>(3) provisions to assure adequate maintenance and safety in impoundment structures and access to recreational sites.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.535.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.50.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.52.0">1994 c 643 s 52</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.541">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.541 MUNICIPAL DAMS ON RED RIVER OF THE NORTH.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.541.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority to construct dam.</span></h2>
          <p>A municipality owning or permanently controlling property where a proposed dam is to be constructed may:</p>
          <p>(1) construct a dam on the property and across that portion of the Red River of the North that forms a part of the boundary common to this state and the state of North Dakota to conserve water for municipal, commercial, and domestic use; and</p>
          <p>(2) construct, in connection with the dam structures, fishways, raceways, sluiceways, and wasteways necessary or convenient for the proper construction and utility of the dam and as may be required by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.541.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consent of the United States and North Dakota.</span></h2>
          <p>If required by law or treaty, the municipality must first obtain the consent of the United States and of the state of North Dakota for the construction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.541.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.51.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 51</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.545">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.545 DAMS AND WATER LEVEL CONTROL IN COOK, LAKE, AND ST. LOUIS COUNTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.545.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of this section is to preserve shorelines, rapids, waterfalls, beaches, and other natural features in an unmodified state of nature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.545.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Legislative approval required.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in this section, specific authority must be given by law after consideration by the legislature with regard to control structures or water levels within or bordering on the area of Cook, Lake, and St. Louis Counties designated in the Act of Congress of July 10, 1930, United States Code, title 16, section 577, before:</p>
          <p>(1) dams or additions to existing dams may be constructed in or across public waters;</p>
          <p>(2) alteration of the natural water level or volume of flowage of public waters may be made; or</p>
          <p>(3) an easement for flooding or overflowing or otherwise affecting state property adjacent to public waters may be granted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.545.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recreational and logging dams.</span></h2>
          <p>With the written approval of the commissioner and the signed authority of the Executive Council, dams for public recreational uses or dams essential for logging or for logging reservoirs that do not exceed 100 acres in size may be constructed to temporarily maintain water levels up to but not higher than the normal high-water marks. The approval is subject to fees recommended by the commissioner, time limitation, and other conditions designed fully to protect the public interest and purpose of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.545.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exception and requirement for certain waterpower sites.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section does not apply to the portion of a proposed development for waterpower purposes that was actually occupied and maintained by an applicant for a license to make the development under the terms of the federal waterpower act if the application for the license was pending on or before January 1, 1928.</p>
          <p>(b) The occupant may occupy and use the state lands and waters occupied on January 1, 1928, and used up to an elevation not exceeding two feet above the lowest crest of the spillway or overflow dam of the occupant as constructed on January 1, 1928, for as long as the land and water is needed for waterpower purposes. Water control structures may not be used higher than the structures used before January 1, 1928.</p>
          <p>(c) The occupant shall pay to the state annual compensation determined by the commissioner after investigation for the use of the state lands affected. The occupant must promptly pay the state reasonable compensation for any further damage to state lands or timber caused by waterpower development, other than is covered by the compensation paid for the use of the lands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.545.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who willfully or knowingly violates a provision of this section or of an order made by the commissioner under this section is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.545.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.52.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.18.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.551">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.551 DAMS USED ONLY FOR WATER LEVEL REGULATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.555">103G.555</a> apply to public waters if:</p>
          <p>(1) a dam constructed or maintained in any manner has existed in the outlet of the lake affecting the water level of the lake for a continuous period of at least 15 years;</p>
          <p>(2) the lake has been used by the public for navigation, fishing, hunting, or other beneficial public purposes continuously throughout the 15-year period when allowed by natural conditions;</p>
          <p>(3) the use of the dam for a purpose other than regulating, controlling, or maintaining the water level of the lake in aid of navigation, propagation of fish or waterfowl, or other beneficial public purposes has been discontinued;</p>
          <p>(4) continued regulation, control, or maintenance of the water levels of the lake by the dam in the same manner as during the 15-year period would be desirable and in the public interests in navigation, propagation of fish or waterfowl, or other beneficial public uses of the lake; and</p>
          <p>(5) discontinuance of regulation of water levels through deterioration or removal of the dam would be detrimental to public interests in navigation, propagation of fish and waterfowl, or other beneficial public uses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Dedication of perpetual flowage easement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Owners of property and interests in property bordering on a lake or connecting waters affected by a dam are considered to have dedicated to the state for the use and benefit of the public a perpetual flowage easement on the property for all overflow and other effects of water on their property resulting from the existence, maintenance, or operation of the dam during the 15-year period.</p>
          <p>(b) The perpetual flowage easement has the extent and effect as if the state had:</p>
          <p>(1) owned and controlled the dam;</p>
          <p>(2) regulated, controlled, and maintained the water levels of the lake and connecting waters affected by the dam for public use and benefit under the conditions existing during the 15-year period; and</p>
          <p>(3) acquired the flowage easement by prescription.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Conveyance of flowage easement to commissioner authorized.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may accept a conveyance or release of a property or an interest in property that grants the state a flowage easement on the property for overflow or other effects of water resulting from the existence, maintenance, or operation of a dam, or reconstruction or improvement of a dam, or construction of a dam in the outlet of a lake to regulate, control, or maintain the water level of the lake in aid of navigation, propagation of fish or waterfowl, or other beneficial public purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Determining easement rights.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An action may be brought in the name of the state in the district court of the county where affected property is located to determine:</p>
          <p>(1) the extent and effect of a flowage easement obtained by the state;</p>
          <p>(2) the title and rights of the state under the flowage easement;</p>
          <p>(3) adverse claims to the flowage easement; and</p>
          <p>(4) the rights of all parties interested in or affected by the flowage easement.</p>
          <p>(b) The action may be brought by the attorney general upon the attorney general's own initiative or on request of the commissioner. On request of the attorney general, the county attorney of the county where the property affected by the flowage easement is located shall assist in bringing the action.</p>
          <p>(c) Part or all of the property affected by the flowage easement that is bordering on one lake and the connecting waters of the lake and located in one county may be included in one action. The parties interested in the affected property may be joined as defendants in the action.</p>
          <p>(d) Except as otherwise provided in this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.555">103G.555</a>, the law relating to actions for the determination of title to real estate in the district court governs the action.</p>
          <p>(e) The cost of the action may be paid from money appropriated for the maintenance, operation, and control of the dam involved, or may be paid by the county where the lake and connecting waters are located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Higher ordinary high-water levels.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the water levels maintained by a dam that has existed as provided in subdivision 1 have established an ordinary high-water level above the natural ordinary high-water level, the ordinary high-water level reestablishes the natural ordinary high-water level of the waters. Property owners and the owners of an interest in property affected by the reestablished natural ordinary high-water level are presumed to have consented to the reestablished natural ordinary high-water level and to have dedicated their property to the state for the use and benefit of the public.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may determine the reestablished natural ordinary high-water level in the same manner as provided by law for the determination of natural ordinary high-water levels. The determination is prima facie evidence of the level and has the same effect as a determination of natural ordinary high-water level by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Easements appurtenant to dam.</span></h2>
          <p>A flowage easement obtained by the state under this section attaches and is appurtenant to a dam that is acquired or taken over and maintained or controlled in aid of public navigation, propagation of fish or waterfowl, or other beneficial public purposes by the commissioner or another agency of the state, a county, a political subdivision, or a combination authorized by law. The flowage easement attaches and is appurtenant to the reconstruction or improvement of the dam and to a new dam constructed in the outlet of the lake that is taken over and maintained or controlled by the commissioner, a state agency, a county, a political subdivision, or a combination authorized by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.551.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">State control of abandoned dams.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a dam not owned or controlled by the state or a public agency is not used or maintained by or under the authority of the owner of the dam for a lawful purpose for a continuous period of at least 15 years, the dam and the dam site are presumed to be abandoned by the owner and dedicated to the state with flowage easements appurtenant for the use and benefit of the public. The commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) shall take possession of the dam and the dam site and the flowage easements on behalf of the state and use, maintain, operate, and control the dam, dam site, and flowage easements for public purposes; or</p>
          <p>(2) may dispose of the dam, dam site, or flowage easements for public purposes as provided in paragraph (b) or as otherwise authorized by law.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may dispose of a dam, dam site, or flowage easement after:</p>
          <p>(1) publishing notice of a hearing on disposing of the dam, dam site, or flowage easement in a legal newspaper in the county where the dam is situated;</p>
          <p>(2) holding a hearing; and</p>
          <p>(3) determining that it is not in the public interest for the state to use, maintain, operate, and control the dam.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may construct other or additional control works to supplement or supplant the dam under other provisions of law.</p>
          <p>(d) The title of the state to a dam, dam site, or flowage easement acquired under this subdivision may be established and determined by action in the district court as provided by law for actions for the determination of title to real estate.</p>
          <p>(e) Before taking possession of an abandoned dam, dam site, or flowage easement, the commissioner must file a written certificate executed by the commissioner stating the dam is abandoned and is acquired by the state, in the office of the county recorder of the county where the dam is situated. The responsibility for a dam, dam site, or flowage easement is not on the state until the certificate is recorded or a judgment entered in an appropriate action establishing the state's title to the dam, dam site, and flowage easement. If a county or other political subdivision of the state or combination desires to take over an abandoned dam, dam site, and flowage easement and maintain, operate, control, or dispose of the dam, dam site, and flowage easement for public purposes, the commissioner may convey the dam, dam site, and flowage easement from the state to the county or other political subdivision or combination.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.551.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.53.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 53</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.555">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.555 STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS FOR ACTIONS AGAINST PUBLIC OFFICIALS.</h1>
        <p>An action or proceeding against the state, the commissioner, a county, or political subdivision or their officers or agents, relating to the taking over, construction, reconstruction, repair, improvement, maintenance, operation, or control of a dam subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.551">103G.551</a> and this section or the effects of water levels regulated, controlled, or maintained by a dam is barred unless the action is started within one year after the taking over or after the completion of the construction, reconstruction, repair, or improvement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.555.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.54.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 54</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.561">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.561 STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS FOR ACTIONS ON FLOWAGE EASEMENTS AND ORDINARY HIGH-WATER LEVELS.</h1>
        <p>An action or proceeding that affects or seeks to adversely affect a perpetual flowage easement dedicated to the state for the use and benefit of the public as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.551#stat.103G.551.2">103G.551, subdivision 2</a>, or the maintaining or the right to maintain a reestablished natural ordinary high-water level above the natural ordinary high-water level of waters for which the state holds a perpetual flowage easement is barred unless the action or proceeding is commenced within one year from the date of the order of the commissioner determining the ordinary high-water level of the waters under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.551#stat.103G.551.5">103G.551, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.561.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.55.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 55</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FLOWAGE EASEMENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.565">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.565 RIGHT TO OVERFLOW, OBSTRUCT, OR IMPAIR HIGHWAYS GRANTED BY GOVERNING BODY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.565.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The governing body of a town or municipality may allow the overflow, obstruction, or impairment of a public street or other highway, or the digging of a raceway in a public street or highway if it is necessary for creating, improving, or operating a waterpower.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.565.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The waterpower must petition the governing body of the town or municipality where the street or highway is located for approval.</p>
          <p>(b) The governing body of the town or municipality must post notice of the time, location, and purpose of the meeting on the petition for ten days. At the meeting, testimony may be taken. The governing body must make an order specifying the terms and conditions of the approval.</p>
          <p>(c) The expenses of the meeting must be paid by the petitioner, whether the petition is granted or refused.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.565.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.56.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 56</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.571">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.571 BANK REPAIR ON PROPERTY WHERE OVERFLOW RIGHTS ARE ACQUIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.571.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Right to repair.</span></h2>
          <p>If the right to overflow the property of another by means of a dam is acquired by condemnation or contract and afterwards the waters of the stream are diverted because the banks of the property overflowed break away, the owner of the dam may enter the property of the person where the right to overflow is acquired and repair the banks to restore the previous flow of the stream.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.571.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Damages of entry and repair.</span></h2>
          <p>The damages caused by entry and repair under subdivision 1 must be paid by the owner of the dam.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.571.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.57.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 57</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.575">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.575 GRANT OF FLOWAGE EASEMENTS IN UPPER RED LAKE REGION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner, on behalf of the state and with the approval of the governor, may grant flowage easements on state-owned land or tax-forfeited land in the region of Upper Red Lake upon the terms and conditions prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.575.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.58.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 58</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WATER AERATION AND DEICING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.601">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.601 ICE-CUTTING FENCES AND GUARDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.601.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fence and guard requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>A person cutting ice in or on waters entirely or partly in the state to remove ice must surround the cuttings and openings with fences or guards sufficient to warn persons of the cutting before the cutting is started. The fence or guard must be maintained until the ice has formed in the openings to the thickness of at least six inches.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.601.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who fails to comply with this section is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.601.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.59.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 59</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.605">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.605 DEICING WATER BODIES.</h1>
        <p>A county board, lake improvement district, or governing body of a municipality under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/459.20">459.20</a> may regulate the construction and use of mechanical and chemical means of deicing the body of water in a manner consistent with rules of the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.605.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.60.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 60</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.611">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.611 WATER AERATION SAFETY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fee for a permit to operate an aeration system on public waters during periods of ice cover is $250. The commissioner may waive the fee for aeration systems that are assisting efforts to maintain angling opportunities through the prevention of winterkill. To be eligible for the fee waiver, the lake being aerated must have public access and aeration must be identified as a desirable management tool in a plan approved by the commissioner. Operation of the aeration system in a manner not consistent with the approved plan represents justification for rescinding the fee waiver. The fee may not be charged to the state or a federal governmental agency applying for a permit. The money received for permits under this subdivision must be deposited in the treasury and credited to the game and fish fund.</p>
          <p>(b) A person operating an aeration system on public waters under a water aeration permit must comply with the sign posting requirements of this section and applicable rules of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">General permits.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a general permit to a governmental subdivision or to the general public to conduct one or more projects described in subdivision 1. A fee of $100 may be charged for each aeration system used under a general permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Posting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If an aeration system is used on the ice of public waters, signs must be posted by the water aeration permittee at a height of from four to six feet in a rectangular pattern at each corner of the open water, and additional signs between the corner signs so that a sign is posted at least every 100 feet.</p>
          <p>(b) Additional signs must be posted by the permittee on the shoreline of the public waters at each public access point and other areas commonly used by the public for access to the lake.</p>
          <p>(c) The signs must comply with the applicable rules of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Publishing notice.</span></h2>
          <p>Advance public notice of the commencement of any aeration system, authorized by a water aeration permit from the commissioner during periods of ice cover on public waters, must be given by the permittee. Minimum notice consists of publication of the location and date of commencement of the aeration system in a newspaper of general circulation in the area where the system is proposed to be operated at least two times between five and 60 days before aeration is started.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Evidence.</span></h2>
          <p>In an action for negligence arising out of the conduct of aeration operations authorized by a water aeration permit from the commissioner during periods of ice cover on public waters, evidence of compliance with the posting and publication requirements of this section and applicable rules and permit provisions of the commissioner are prima facie evidence of the exercise of due care by the permittee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Water aeration rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt rules relating to the issuance of permits for aeration, bubbler, water circulation, and similar systems used to increase dissolved oxygen or to maintain open water on the surface of public waters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.24.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.611.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Public waters without access.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who receives a permit to operate an aeration system on a public water without a public access and who owns all of the riparian land or all of the possessory rights to the riparian land around that water is not subject to the provisions of subdivisions 2, paragraph (b), and 3.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.611.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.61.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/218/#laws.0.18.0">1995 c 218 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.118.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.22.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.56.0">2012 c 272 s 56</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">HARVESTING AND CONTROLLING AQUATIC PLANTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.615">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.615 PERMITS TO HARVEST OR DESTROY AQUATIC PLANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Issuance; validity.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may issue a state general permit to a governmental subdivision or to the general public to conduct one or more projects described in this subdivision. The commissioner may issue permits, with or without a fee, to:</p>
          <p>(1) gather or harvest aquatic plants, or plant parts, other than wild rice from public waters;</p>
          <p>(2) transplant aquatic plants into public waters;</p>
          <p>(3) destroy harmful or undesirable aquatic vegetation or organisms in public waters under prescribed conditions to protect the waters, desirable species of fish, vegetation, other forms of aquatic life, and the public.</p>
          <p>(b) Application for a permit and a notification to request authorization to conduct a project under a general permit must be accompanied by a fee, if required.</p>
          <p>(c) An aquatic plant management permit is valid for one growing season and expires on December 31 of the year it is issued unless the commissioner stipulates a different expiration date in rule or in the permit.</p>
          <p>(d) A general permit may authorize a project for more than one growing season.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall establish a fee schedule for permits to control or harvest aquatic plants other than wild rice. The fees must be set by rule, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a> does not apply, but the rule must not take effect until 45 legislative days after it has been reported to the legislature. The fees shall not exceed $2,500 per permit and shall be based upon the cost of receiving, processing, analyzing, and issuing the permit, and additional costs incurred after the application to inspect and monitor the activities authorized by the permit, and enforce aquatic plant management rules and permit requirements.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A fee for a permit for the control of rooted aquatic vegetation for each contiguous parcel of shoreline owned by an owner may be charged. This fee may not be charged for permits issued in connection with purple loosestrife control or lakewide Eurasian watermilfoil control programs.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A fee may not be charged to the state or a federal governmental agency applying for a permit.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A fee for a permit for the control of rooted aquatic vegetation in a public water basin that is 20 acres or less in size shall be one-half of the fee established under paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(e) The money received for the permits under this subdivision shall be deposited in the treasury and credited to the water recreation account.</p>
          <p>(f) The fee for processing a notification to request authorization for work under a general permit is $30, until the commissioner establishes a fee by rule as provided under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permit standards.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall, by rule, prescribe standards to issue and deny permits under this section. The standards must ensure that aquatic plant control is consistent with shoreland conservation ordinances, lake management plans and programs, and wild and scenic river plans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Invasive aquatic plant management permit.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Invasive aquatic plant management permit" means an aquatic plant management permit as defined in rules of the Department of Natural Resources that authorizes the selective control of invasive aquatic plants to cause a significant reduction in the abundance of the invasive aquatic plant.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may waive the dated signature of approval requirement in rules of the Department of Natural Resources for invasive aquatic plant management permits if obtaining signatures would create an undue burden on the permittee or if the commissioner determines that aquatic plant control is necessary to protect natural resources.</p>
          <p>(c) If the signature requirement is waived under paragraph (b) because obtaining signatures would create an undue burden on the permittee, the commissioner shall require an alternate form of landowner notification, including news releases or public notices in a local newspaper, a public meeting, or a mailing or electronic transmission to the most recent permanent physical or electronic mailing address of affected landowners. The notification must be given annually and must include: the proposed date of treatment, the target species, the method of control or product being used, and instructions on how the landowner may request that control not occur adjacent to the landowner's property.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may allow dated signatures of approval obtained for an invasive aquatic plant management permit to satisfy rules of the Department of Natural Resources to remain valid for three years if property ownership remains unchanged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Enforcement authority and restoration requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may make findings and issue an order to a person to stop the illegal gathering, harvesting, planting or transplanting, or destroying of aquatic vegetation or organisms in public waters.</p>
          <p>(b) In the same or a separate findings and order, the commissioner may require restoration or replacement of any emergent or floating leaf aquatic vegetation lost as a result of the illegal activities, to the condition existing before the illegal activities were undertaken. An order for restoration or replacement must state with specificity the work that is necessary to comply with the order and must specify a date by which the work must be completed.</p>
          <p>(c) The person or entity to whom the order is issued may request a review of the order by the commissioner within 30 days of receipt of written notice by filing a written request for review. If the written request is not submitted within 30 days, the restoration or replacement order becomes final. The commissioner shall review the request and supporting evidence and render a decision within 60 days of the request for review.</p>
          <p>(d) If the person or entity wishes to appeal the decision of the commissioner after review under paragraph (c), a written request must be filed with the commissioner within 30 days for a contested case hearing under chapter 14. A bond, as provided in subdivision 5, must accompany the demand for a hearing. The bond and demand for hearing must be filed 30 days after the person is served with a copy of the decision of the commissioner on review.</p>
          <p>(e) If the person or entity to whom the decision of the commissioner on review is addressed does not demand a contested case hearing under chapter 14 or demands a hearing but fails to file the required bond:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner's order becomes final at the end of 30 days after the person is served with the decision of the commissioner on review; and</p>
          <p>(2) the person may not appeal the order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Bond for demanding public hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person or entity filing a demand for a public hearing, under subdivision 4, must execute and file a corporate surety bond or equivalent security to the state of Minnesota, to be approved by the commissioner and in an amount and form determined by the commissioner. The bond or security must be conditioned to pay the costs of the hearing to the extent described in subdivision 6 if the commissioner's findings and order are affirmed without material modification.</p>
          <p>(b) A bond or security is not required of a public authority that demands a public hearing.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may waive the requirement for a bond or other security.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Hearing costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), the costs of a hearing must be paid as prescribed by chapter 14 and the chief administrative law judge.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner's order is affirmed without material modification, the appellant must pay the following costs, up to $750:</p>
          <p>(1) costs of the stenographic record and transcript; and</p>
          <p>(2) rental costs, if any, of the place where the hearing is held.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.615.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Misdemeanor.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of an order issued under this section is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.615.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.62.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/462/#laws.0.18.0">1992 c 462 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/235/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 235 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/351/#laws.0.25.0">2002 c 351 s 25</a>-28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.119.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.42.0">2004 c 255 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.123.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.5.22.0">2008 c 363 art 5 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.60.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.77.0">2011 c 107 s 77</a>,78; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.4.16.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.57.0">2012 c 272 s 57</a>,58; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.58.0">2014 c 289 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.94.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 94</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.103G.617"><b>103G.617</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.621">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.621 COUNTY WEED AND ALGAE DESTRUCTION AND REMOVAL.</h1>
        <p>A county board, lake improvement district, or governing body of a municipality under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/459.20">459.20</a> may regulate the mechanical and chemical means of removal of weeds and algae from the body of water in a manner consistent with the rules of the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.621.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.64.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 64</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.625">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.625 MUNICIPAL CONTROL OF AQUATIC VEGETATION AND ORGANISMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.625.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The governing body of a municipality or town may expend funds for the control or destruction of harmful or undesirable aquatic vegetation or organisms in public waters and may cooperate with other governing bodies and landowners in the control or destruction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.625.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Permit required.</span></h2>
          <p>The control or destruction of the aquatic vegetation or organisms may not be started unless a permit has been obtained from the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.615">103G.615</a> and the work is done in accordance with the terms and conditions of the permit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.625.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The governing body of a municipality or town may use any available funds and may levy a tax on all taxable property in the municipality or town to implement this section.</p>
          <p>(b) To provide funds in advance of collection of the tax levies, the governing body may, at any time after the tax has been levied and certified to the county auditor for collection, issue certificates of indebtedness in anticipation of the collection and payment of the tax. The total amount of the certificates, including principal and interest, may not exceed 90 percent of the amount of the levy and must become payable from the proceeds of the levy not later than two years from the date of issuance. The certificates shall be issued on terms and conditions as the governing body may determine and sold as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.60">475.60</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) If the governing body determines that an emergency exists, it may make appropriations from the proceeds of the certificates for authorized purposes without complying with statutory or charter provisions requiring that expenditures be based on a prior budget authorization or other budgeting requirement.</p>
          <p>(d) The proceeds of a tax levied or an issue of certificates of indebtedness must be deposited in a separate fund and expended only for purposes authorized by this section. If a disbursement is not made from the fund for a period of five years, money remaining in the fund may be transferred to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.625.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.65.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/505/#laws.3.3.0">1994 c 505 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SUNKEN LOG RECOVERY</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.103G.650"><b>103G.650</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.83.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 83</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.651">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.651 REMOVING SUNKEN LOGS FROM PUBLIC WATERS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources must not issue leases to remove sunken logs or issue permits for the removal of sunken logs from public waters.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.651.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.61.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 61</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STREAMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.701">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.701 STREAM MAINTENANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.701.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a stream maintenance program. The program must include grants-in-aid to participating counties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.701.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>A county desiring to participate in the stream maintenance program must submit an application for the proposed work to the commissioner on forms provided by the commissioner. Unless waived by the commissioner, the county must submit the following information with its application:</p>
          <p>(1) a map of the county showing the stream and the specific reaches of the stream to be maintained;</p>
          <p>(2) photographs showing the nature and extent of the maintenance problem; and</p>
          <p>(3) a resolution by the county board of commissioners asking to participate in the program and agreeing to provide at least 25 percent of the cost of the maintenance project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.701.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contract.</span></h2>
          <p>After approving a stream maintenance project, the commissioner shall contract with the county for performance of work necessary to do the project. The contract may provide that the county share of the cost of the project is paid in the form of services provided by the county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.701.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may grant money for:</p>
          <p>(1) cutting and removal of brush and dead or downed trees; and</p>
          <p>(2) removal of large rocks and other debris such as concrete, asphalt, or scrap material.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.701.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must apportion grant money according to the relative severity of the maintenance problem, the date of application for the grant, and the availability of funds.</p>
          <p>(b) A grant may not exceed 75 percent of the total cost of a stream maintenance project.</p>
          <p>(c) Money may not be disbursed for excavation, filling, or for work performed until an application for the project is filed with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) The stream maintenance work must be performed by the county or under county supervision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.701.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">County matching funds.</span></h2>
          <p>A county may appropriate from its general revenue fund sufficient funds to match the grants-in-aid authorized in this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.701.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.66.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 66</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.103G.705">
        <h1>103G.705 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.705.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.98.0">2012 c 272 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.103G.705.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/215/#laws.3.8.0">2010 c 215 art 3 s 8; 2012 c 272 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.711">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.711 STATE'S OWNERSHIP OF BED OF NAVIGABLE RIVER.</h1>
        <p>The ownership of the bed and the land under the waters of all rivers in the state that are navigable for commercial purposes are in the state in fee simple, subject only to the regulations made by the United States with regard to the public navigation and commerce and the lawful use by the public while on the waters.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.103G.711.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.7.67.0">1990 c 391 art 7 s 67</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GREAT LAKES COMPACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.103G.801">
        <h1 class="shn">103G.801 GREAT LAKES -- ST. LAWRENCE RIVER BASIN WATER RESOURCES COMPACT.</h1>
        <p>The Great Lakes -- St. Lawrence River Basin Water Resources Compact is enacted into law and entered into with all jurisdictions legally joining therein in the form substantially as follows:</p>
        <p class="ce">
          <b>ARTICLE 1</b>
        </p>
        <p class="ce">
          <b>SHORT TITLE, DEFINITIONS, PURPOSES AND DURATION</b>
        </p>
        <p>
          <b>Section 1.1. Short title.</b>
        </p>
        <p>This act shall be known and may be cited as the "Great Lakes -- St. Lawrence River Basin Water Resources Compact."</p>
        <p>
          <b>Section 1.2. Definitions.</b>
        </p>
        <p>For the purposes of this compact, and of any supplemental or concurring legislation enacted pursuant thereto, except as may be otherwise required by the context:</p>
        <p>"Adaptive management" means a water resources management system that provides a systematic process for evaluation, monitoring and learning from the outcomes of operational programs and adjustment of policies, plans and programs based on experience and the evolution of scientific knowledge concerning water resources and water dependent natural resources.</p>
        <p>"Agreement" means the Great Lakes -- St. Lawrence River Basin Sustainable Water Resources Agreement.</p>
        <p>"Applicant" means a person who is required to submit a proposal that is subject to management and regulation under this compact. "Application" has a corresponding meaning.</p>
        <p>"Basin" or "Great Lakes -- St. Lawrence River basin" means the watershed of the Great Lakes and the St. Lawrence River upstream from Trois-Rivieres, Quebec within the jurisdiction of the parties.</p>
        <p>"Basin ecosystem" or "Great Lakes -- St. Lawrence River basin ecosystem" means the interacting components of air, land, water and living organisms, including humankind, within the basin.</p>
        <p>"Community within a straddling county" means any incorporated city, town or the equivalent thereof, that is located outside the basin but wholly within a county that lies partly within the basin and that is not a straddling community.</p>
        <p>"Compact" means this compact.</p>
        <p>"Consumptive use" means that portion of the water withdrawn or withheld from the basin that is lost or otherwise not returned to the basin due to evaporation, incorporation into products, or other processes.</p>
        <p>"Council" means the Great Lakes -- St. Lawrence River Basin Water Resources Council, created by this compact.</p>
        <p>"Council review" means the collective review by the council members as described in Article 4 of this compact.</p>
        <p>"County" means the largest territorial division for local government in a state. The county boundaries shall be defined as those boundaries that exist as of December 13, 2005.</p>
        <p>"Cumulative impacts" mean the impact on the basin ecosystem that results from incremental effects of all aspects of a withdrawal, diversion or consumptive use in addition to other past, present, and reasonably foreseeable future withdrawals, diversions and consumptive uses regardless of who undertakes the other withdrawals, diversions and consumptive uses. Cumulative impacts can result from individually minor but collectively significant withdrawals, diversions and consumptive uses taking place over a period of time.</p>
        <p>"Decision-making standard" means the decision-making standard established by Section 4.11 for proposals subject to management and regulation in Section 4.10.</p>
        <p>"Diversion" means a transfer of water from the basin into another watershed, or from the watershed of one of the Great Lakes into that of another by any means of transfer, including but not limited to a pipeline, canal, tunnel, aqueduct, channel, modification of the direction of a water course, a tanker ship, tanker truck or rail tanker but does not apply to water that is used in the basin or a Great Lake watershed to manufacture or produce a product that is then transferred out of the basin or watershed. "Divert" has a corresponding meaning.</p>
        <p>"Environmentally sound and economically feasible water conservation measures" mean those measures, methods, technologies or practices for efficient water use and for reduction of water loss and waste or for reducing a withdrawal, consumptive use or diversion that (i) are environmentally sound, (ii) reflect best practices applicable to the water use sector, (iii) are technically feasible and available, (iv) are economically feasible and cost effective based on an analysis that considers direct and avoided economic and environmental costs and (v) consider the particular facilities and processes involved, taking into account the environmental impact, age of equipment and facilities involved, the processes employed, energy impacts and other appropriate factors.</p>
        <p>"Exception" means a transfer of water that is excepted under Section 4.9 from the prohibition against diversions in Section 4.8.</p>
        <p>"Exception standard" means the standard for exceptions established in Section 4.9.4.</p>
        <p>"Intra-basin transfer" means the transfer of water from the watershed of one of the Great Lakes into the watershed of another Great Lake.</p>
        <p>"Measures" means any legislation, law, regulation, directive, requirement, guideline, program, policy, administrative practice or other procedure.</p>
        <p>"New or increased diversion" means a new diversion, an increase in an existing diversion, or the alteration of an existing withdrawal so that it becomes a diversion.</p>
        <p>"New or increased withdrawal or consumptive use" means a new withdrawal or consumptive use or an increase in an existing withdrawal or consumptive use.</p>
        <p>"Originating party" means the party within whose jurisdiction an application or registration is made or required.</p>
        <p>"Party" means a state party to this compact.</p>
        <p>"Person" means a human being or a legal person, including a government or a non-governmental organization, including any scientific, professional, business, non-profit, or public interest organization or association that is neither affiliated with, nor under the direction of a government.</p>
        <p>"Product" means something produced in the basin by human or mechanical effort or through agricultural processes and used in manufacturing, commercial or other processes or intended for intermediate or end use consumers. (i) Water used as part of the packaging of a product shall be considered to be part of the product. (ii) Other than water used as part of the packaging of a product, water that is used primarily to transport materials in or out of the basin is not a product or part of a product. (iii) Except as provided in (i) above, water which is transferred as part of a public or private supply is not a product or part of a product. (iv) Water in its natural state such as in lakes, rivers, reservoirs, aquifers, or water basins is not a product.</p>
        <p>"Proposal" means a withdrawal, diversion or consumptive use of water that is subject to this compact.</p>
        <p>"Province" means Ontario or Quebec.</p>
        <p>"Public water supply purposes" means water distributed to the public through a physically connected system of treatment, storage and distribution facilities serving a group of largely residential customers that may also serve industrial, commercial, and other institutional operators. Water withdrawn directly from the basin and not through such a system shall not be considered to be used for public water supply purposes.</p>
